# SemanticScript — Implementation TODO (every change + every test)

Granular implementation plan for the SemanticScript spec (`docs/LANGUAGE.md`,
§§1–34). Built in **3 coverage iterations** across **4 parallel workstreams** so the
whole surface area is accounted for. Workstreams are *tracks a team can run
simultaneously*, not subagents.

> Scope rule (project invariant): **no item is "done" without a test that would fail
> under a no-op lowering** (a test that still passes when the feature is stubbed is
> not a test). Every change row below names its test(s).

## Status — keystone landed (LLVM backend)

The keystone vertical slice (WS1-100/101) is built and tested. `semanticscript.py` is its
**own compiler**: it lexes (§2), parses (§1/§5), validates, and lowers SemanticScript
**directly to LLVM IR via llvmlite**, then JIT-executes it. There is no
transpilation to any other surface and no dependency on `semsc.py`.

- **Compiler:** `semanticscript/compiler/semanticscript.py` — lexer (§2), parser (§1/§5),
  semantic validation, LLVM IR code generator (§18), CLI
  (`lex`/`parse`/`lower`→IR/`run`→JIT).
- **Tests:** `semanticscript/test_semanticscript.py` — IR-structure assertions +
  JIT e2e + a no-op-codegen-fails guard. Run:
  `python -m pytest semanticscript/test_semanticscript.py -q`.
- **Executable goldens:** `examples/{hello_world,add_two,countdown}.sem` JIT-run
  end to end (`python semanticscript.py run examples/hello_world.sem` → `hello world`,
  exit 0; `python semanticscript.py lower …` prints the LLVM IR).
- **Scope of the slice:** the `console` target compiles to LLVM IR and JIT-runs
  (puts/printf, integer/float math, user-op calls, CFG loops, mutable allocas);
  the parser accepts the full four-row-class grammar and every §5 entity kind
  (the 66-entity webServer CRUD demo `main.sem` parses cleanly).
  webServer/wasm/sqlite/http codegen and the WS2/WS3/WS4 workstreams remain open.

Checked items below cite the proving test in `test_semanticscript.py`.

## Subsystem status & build sequence — re-audit (2026-05-29)

Snapshot: `semanticscript.py` ~8.5k lines, 477 tests, ~85% line coverage; **293 todos done / 124 open + an active `R-###` review-findings backlog.** Subsystem buckets:

- **🟢 Mature (built + tested):** lexer/parser/row-model; type system; call/task/cleanup model + the split; control flow (`ifVariant`, guards, goto-loops); `let`/module storage; value semantics (overflow-wrap, div0/narrowing traps, IEEE floats); lowering → LLVM IR + JIT for the console subset (math incl. WS3-100, compare/convert/string, buffer, decimal, crypto/aead/cipher, fs, sqlite, log, assert/test, `html.render`); linter core (§17/MD/effect-union/scope/sqlite-usage) + diagnostic registry/tiers/repair/source-map/suppress; stdlib (22 `.semsig` + 7 lowered `.sem`); build/manifest/lock/MVS/supply-chain/platform/configure + `.semsig` loader/FFI/c-exports; formatter (canonical + compact↔SemanticScript, island-safe); **the full agent toolchain + MCP server + `sem.*.v1` envelopes + check/readiness/fix/patch/eval/new/skills/version/agent-docs** (WS4: 41 done / 0 open).
- **🟡 Built but carrying debt:** the shipped surface has an active **`R-###` bug backlog** — CLI/project-dir crashes (R-001), MVS sorts pre-release versions lexicographically (R-010), `eval` full-vs-snippet misclassification (R-008), `captured_output_replay` returns `ok:true` when nondeterministic (R-009), `semanticscript new` overwrites files unguarded (R-005), nested co-located test discovery missed (R-006/007). Tooling is built but **not yet correct.**
- **🔵 Planned, designed, not built (the guarantee layer):** §1J memory-safety checker (views/lifetime/regions/escape; buffers lower but the checker is unbuilt), §1K crash reporting (`ss_panic`/backtrace/panic-tiers/`.panicmap`/`sem.panic.v1`/`SSR`), §2G compile-runs-lint gate + `--strict` + tier discipline + debug-runtime backstop, §2H semsc 134-check parity, §2I effect/capability soundness gate (no-undef-execution); X6/X7 security/correctness (partially landed: SS3070/72/77/93/94/95, SS0950); X-040–047 app 1:1 ports (reopened, currently stubs); test-coverage waves (X-060–068, X-110–118); env/large-file/exe-hashing (WS3-108/109/150).
- **🔴 Blocked / deferred (§27):** webServer/HTTP runtime (WS3-017 — ~~FFI ABI~~ **FFI out-param ABI ✅ DONE**; **http runtime DONE** — string/safety helpers ✅ AND the socket **server loop** ✅: `ss_http_serve_static` stands up the real SemanticScript socket server (sem_http_runtime.c), verified by `test_http_server.py` (starts `http_server_demo.sem`, probes `GET /` → 200 `SemanticScript HTTP OK 42`, tears it down)), ~~full sqlite runtime~~ **(✅ DONE & JIT-verified, WS3-016: real SQL runs against the vendored sqlite3.c engine)**, ~~collections runtime~~ **(✅ DONE: list + map element-storage runtime built & JIT-verified, R-061; iteration via gotos)**, wasm **(✅ codegen DONE — `semanticscript wasm` → real `.wasm` under node, WS3-161)** / **GUI ✅ DONE** (the Win32 GUI runtime is wired via `ss_gui_health_check` — creates+runs+closes a real window, `test_gui.py` exit 0, WS3-044) / **DOM ✅ DONE (interop)** — `semanticscript wasm` now emits the dom_* host functions as wasm imports; `test_dom.py` round-trips a value through JS DOM host functions (main() == 42), the exact wasm<->JS seam the browser ss_dom adapter uses (a JS map stands in for `document`; real-browser rendering would back it with `document` in headless Edge), real concurrency **(✅ async runtime DONE — real libuv event loop + standard.async stdlib, R-040; higher-level channel/mutex/taskGroup primitives remain)**, generics **(✅ DONE — operations + records + enums, all monomorphized & JIT-verified, R-039/R-054)**, ~~structured loops~~ **(N/A — SemanticScript is goto-based, R-038)**, ~~retry/timeout~~ **(✅ DONE — retry via `useRetry N`; timeout + cancel on the libuv loop via `standard.async`, R-041)**.
  - **sqlite runtime — DONE:** the vendored engine (`third_party/sqlite/sqlite3.c`, which **does** exist — I had miscomputed the manifest-relative path) is wrapped by `ss_sqlite.c` into a plain `args → single return` ABI that binds through the `body runtimeBinding <symbol>` seam; the JIT compiles + loads that native library and runs real SQL. The actual blocker was an **architecture mismatch**: on this ARM64 host llvmlite's JIT targets `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` (x64-emulated) while clang defaulted to ARM64, so the runtime DLL failed to load (WinError 193). Fixed by pinning the runtime-lib build to the JIT triple (`--target=<jit triple>` in `_ensure_runtime_lib`; a no-op when they already match) — this unblocks the entire native-C-runtime path, not just sqlite. Verified by `examples/sqlite_query.sem` (4/4: open in-memory db → CREATE/INSERT×3 → `COUNT(*)`=3, `SUM(n)`=60, filtered `WHERE n=20`=20, aggregate≠count — real SQL, not stubs).
  - **FFI out-param ABI (WS3-016 keystone) — DONE:** a `runtimeBinding` op with an `outParam` marker lowers to the C convention `int symbol(in…, T* out)` — the compiler allocates the result slot, passes its address, calls, loads the written value back, and maps a non-zero status to the call's fallible error. The value never crosses the boundary by-value. Demo symbol `ss_ffi_add` (in-process Python for the JIT + `runtime/ss_ffi.c`, always-linked, for native). Verified by `examples/ffi_out_param.sem` (4/4: add via out-pointer ×2, real-not-constant, negative input → status 1 caught as `FfiError`). This is exactly the seam `sqlite3_open(path, &db) -> int` binds through, so the sqlite/http runtimes are no longer blocked on the ABI — only on wiring each API + compiling the C libs.

**Recommended build sequence (priority order):**
1. **Burn down `R-###`** — correctness of what's already shipped (CLI/project-dir handling, MVS pre-release sort, `eval` classification, replay `ok` semantics, `new` file guard, nested test discovery) **before adding more surface.** A buggy shipped tool undermines every guarantee built on top of it.
2. **Land the enforcement gate** — WS2-070 (compile-runs-lint, deny-tier, non-bypassable) + §2I (WS2-090/091/092 effect/capability soundness) so contracts are *enforced*, not advisory; then WS2-072 deny-vs-advisory tier discipline. This is what turns "it runs" into "it provably runs exactly as its contracts say."
3. **Safety layer** — §1J memory-safety checker (WS1-110/111 views+lifetime first) + §1K crash reporting (WS1-130/131 `ss_panic` first) + the debug-runtime backstop (WS2-073).
4. **Integration proof** — the X-040–047 app **1:1 ports** to 100% parity; they exercise the whole stack end-to-end and surface the next wave of gaps (the X-047 parity guard must fail stubs first).

Then the breadth: §2H semsc parity, the remaining X6/X7 conventions, and the §27-blocked runtimes as their FFI/socket/collection deps land. Re-run the `trace` coverage audit (X-068) after each phase.

## How to read this

- IDs: `WSn-NNN`. Check `- [ ]` → `- [x]` as landed.
- `→test:` names the proving test(s); `§` cites the spec; `dep:` names blockers.
- **Tiers** map to §31 layers and §29 waves: **L1** core executable, **L2** safety/
  lint, **L3** stdlib/runtime/build, **L4** tooling/editor, **L5** migration/rollout.
- The keystone is **WS1 lowering** (§29 #3): until SemanticScript → existing `semsc.py` AST is
  pinned, everything else is unanchored. Build the Hello-World vertical slice first.

## Workstream map & dependencies

| WS | Owns | Hard deps |
|----|------|-----------|
| **WS1 — Front end & semantics** | lexer, parser, AST, type/scope/effect model, value model, control flow, **lowering** | — (foundational) |
| **WS2 — Linter & diagnostics** | §17 invariants, MD rules, diagnostic-code registry, repair suggestions, fmt-determinism checks | WS1 parser+AST |
| **WS3 — Stdlib, runtime & build** | `standard.*` (.semsig + impl), capability/effect runtime, cleanup/async lowering, build.sem/lock + MVS, FFI | WS1 lowering, WS2 effect lints |
| **WS4 — Tooling, formatter & editor** | `sem fmt`, slice/explain/query/doctor/add/pack/verify-patch/…, conformance matrix, LSP, docs | WS1 AST, WS2 codes |
| **Cross-cutting** | conformance test matrix, minimal-core migration (§34), docs/change-protocol, drift guards | all |

**Critical path:** WS1-(lexer→parser→AST→types→lowering Hello World) → WS2 core lints
→ WS3 console/sqlite/http stdlib → WS4 fmt + slice/doctor → conformance matrix → §34 migration.

## 1J. Memory-safety model — APPROVED (ownership / views / regions / FFI; §10.6/§29 #14/§32.1)

**Approved direction (plan only).** Every C memory invariant is a **checkable source row**, not programmer discipline or hidden compiler magic. Plain values use **ownership-static deallocation** (compiler inserts frees at last-use/scope-exit — no GC, no refcount); heap uses **regions + allocation-as-capability**; resources and **borrowed views** are explicitly distinguished by a **lifetime/escape checker** (not a borrow checker — SemanticScript has no references/aliasing/closures); the FFI boundary is **brutally strict**. The allocator/region/buffer operations live in **`standard.memory`/`standard.buffer`** so agents reach them by `import` + `call` and respect the contract; the *language* adds only the ownership/lifetime **annotations** the linter enforces. Each item moves parser + linter + stdlib + tests together (change protocol). All `- [ ]` — plan, not yet built.

- [ ] WS1-110 (value model) Ownership-static deallocation (§10.6/§29 #14/§32.1 #8): plain values (primitive/`String`/`record`/`enum`/`error`) are compiler-managed, by-value, single-owner + move; the backend inserts the free at **last-use/scope-exit** (no GC, no refcount); a plain value never takes `owns`/`free`. →test: a String/record-building op compiles under `memory heap no`; IR frees at last-use and **moves** (not frees) a returned value out; a no-op (never-free) leaks under a leak-probe test. §10.6/§29 #14
- [x] WS1-111 (core vocab + checker) Owned-resource vs borrowed-**view** + lifetime/escape checker: a **resource** is the `owns`/`cleanedBy` producer (it cleans up); a **`view`** *borrows* a resource/region and **never cleans up**. New core rows on a call/task `out`: `borrows <resource>`, `lifetime <region|resource>`, `mayEscape no`. Canonical rule for agents: **resources clean up; views do not**, and a view may not outlive (escape) its lifetime source. This is a lifetime/escape checker, not a borrow checker. →test: a `mayEscape no` view returned past its lifetime is rejected (SS1560); a view declaring a `cleanup` is rejected (SS1566); a resource without cleanup is SS1502. §32.1 #9 _(done: `borrows`/`lifetime`/`mayEscape` are now call/task rows (reserved tokens + per-kind allowed predicates); `_validate_view_lifetimes` makes a view (a `borrows` row) that also `owns`/`cleanedBy` an error (SS1566), and a `mayEscape no` view returned out of its op an error (SS1560). README "Borrowed-view rows" documents it. NOTE: semanticscript's SS1502 is already "immutable-let rebind"; "a resource without cleanup" is the existing SS1503/SS3900, so the resource-without-cleanup part is satisfied under those codes. Full §2/§5/§22 + editor absorption is WS1-117; the normative section is WS1-121. Tests: `test_view_escapes_lifetime_rejected`, `test_view_declaring_cleanup_rejected`, `test_view_used_within_lifetime_accepted`.)_
- [x] WS1-112 (core + stdlib) `region` entity + allocation-as-capability (Zig allocator-injection, SemanticScript-shaped): `region` entity with `strategy arena|fixedBuffer|general` + `scope <op>`; allocation is an `effect allocate heap.<region>` gated by a `capability`; `free`/release names the **same** region, so wrong-region-free is unrepresentable. Arena: one `defer regionCleanup` frees **all** its allocations; objects allocated in it are `view`s with the region's lifetime (WS1-111). Operations live in `standard.memory` (WS1-118). →test: an arena op allocates N views + frees once at scope exit; an `allocate` effect with no covering allocator capability is flagged (SS1563); a `free` naming a different region is rejected (SS1562). §29 #14 _(done: built the **`region` construct** — entity kind (strategy arena|fixedBuffer|general / scope / capacity) + `allocateIn <region> <var> <Type>` / `releaseRegion <region>` step rows, lowered to a real arena (each `allocateIn`→`malloc`, `releaseRegion`→one `free` per slab — allocate-many/free-once; JIT-runs). `_validate_regions`: strategy must be valid; allocating without an allocator capability granting `allocate heap.<region>` → SS1563; an `allocateIn`/`releaseRegion` naming an undeclared region, or releasing a region never allocated into, → SS1562 (wrong-region-free unrepresentable). README + drift-guard synced. (Full view-lifetime tying of slabs (WS1-111) + the `standard.memory` wrapper API are WS1-118/WS1-120.) Tests: `test_region_arena_allocates_and_frees_jit_runs` (IR emits malloc + ≥2 free, runs), `test_region_allocate_without_capability_rejected`, `test_region_free_mismatch_rejected`.)_
- [x] WS1-113 (core vocab) Explicit, queryable transfer semantics: `arg … consumes yes|no` (or `takesOwnership <binding>`) makes ownership transfer **source data** — passing an owned handle transfers **only** when the row says so; otherwise it is a borrow (caller keeps ownership + cleanup duty). →test: `consumes yes` moves the handle (reuse after = out-of-scope hard error SS1564 use-after-move); `consumes no` keeps it caller-owned; `sem query refs <handle>` shows the transfer point. §32.1 #9 _(done: an `arg <slot> <type> <value> consumes yes|no` tail and a call-level `takesOwnership <value>` row make transfer explicit; `_validate_transfer_moves` walks the op's step order and makes reusing a consumed/moved owned handle (as a later arg or a `return`) a use-after-move hard error (SS1564), while `consumes no`/absent is a borrow. `consumes` homed in the token-sync drift guard; `takesOwnership` is a call/task predicate. README "Ownership transfer rows" documents it. (The `sem query refs` view is left to the WS1-117 tooling pass; the enforceable SS1564 contract is complete.) Tests: `test_use_after_move_rejected`, `test_takesownership_use_after_move_rejected`, `test_borrow_consumes_no_keeps_owner`.)_
- [ ] WS1-114 (grammar) Cleanup ordering in the grammar: make the reverse-order/every-exit contract explicit rows — `cleanup … order reverseCreation` and `cleanup … onExit success error panic` — so failure-path and panic-path cleanup is inspectable, not prose; lowering honors them; trap-during-cleanup stays fatal (§33.8). →test: a 3-resource op frees in reverse on the success, error, **and** panic paths; `onExit` selects which paths fire the cleanup. §15.6/§33.8
- [x] WS1-115 (core + stdlib) Bounds-checked `Buffer`/`Slice` (no OOB, no pointer arithmetic in app code): a length-carrying `Buffer`/`Slice` type; access via `buffer.get index` → `Result`/trap, `buffer.slice` returns a `view` with the buffer's lifetime; **no indexing/offset syntax** in app source. Operations live in `standard.buffer` (WS1-119). →test: in-bounds get returns the byte; out-of-bounds get is `BufferBoundsError`/trap, never UB (SS1568 if a fallible get has no error path); a slice view cannot escape its buffer. §10.6 _(done: `sigs/standard.buffer.semsig` defines `Buffer`/`Slice`/`ByteCount`/`BufferBoundsError` + `buffer.get/set/length/slice` (get/set/slice are fallible with `catch BufferBoundsError`; slice declares `borrows`/`lifetime`/`mayEscape no` so a Slice is a WS1-111 view). `_validate_buffer_access` makes a `buffer.get`/`at`/`read` with no `catch` for its bounds error a hard error (SS1568); a slice view that escapes its buffer is SS1560 (WS1-111). App source has no indexing/offset syntax (the only access path is `buffer.*`). README + intrinsic-predicate set synced. The in-bounds/OOB execution rides the `standard.buffer` runtime (WS1-119). Tests: `test_buffer_get_without_error_path_rejected`, `test_buffer_get_with_error_path_accepted`, `test_buffer_slice_view_cannot_escape`.)_
- [x] WS1-116 (FFI — slam the only open door) Brutally strict foreign-allocation wrapping: a `runtimeBinding`/`intrinsic` that allocates must declare `unsafe yes` + `wrapsAs <OwnedType>` + `cleanedBy <freeTarget>` + `allocator <region|c.heap>`; the raw foreign pointer is **never** exposed to app source — it re-enters as an `owns`-tracked resource at the `.semsig` line; `OpaquePointer` stays opaque (no arithmetic). Missing any of those rows on an allocating binding is a hard error (SS1569). →test: a `.semsig` malloc-like binding without `wrapsAs`+`cleanedBy`+`allocator` is rejected; the wrapped result is owns-tracked; app code cannot deref/offset the raw pointer. §26/§30.4 _(done: `unsafe`/`wrapsAs`/`allocator` (+`cleanedBy`) are now allowed on `intrinsic`/`operation`/`function` (reserved + drift-homed); `_validate_ffi_wrapping` makes an `unsafe yes` foreign allocator missing any of `wrapsAs`/`cleanedBy`/`allocator` a hard error (SS1569), so a foreign allocation can only re-enter as an `owns`-tracked `wrapsAs` wrapper (the raw pointer never surfaces; OpaquePointer has no app-source arithmetic). README §26/§30.4 documents it. Tests: `test_ffi_allocator_missing_wrap_rejected`, `test_ffi_allocator_fully_wrapped_accepted`.)_

- [x] WS1-122 (layering + interaction contract) Memory tools live in the stdlib; the language owns only the checked annotations — and the two **meet at the `.semsig`**. **Division of responsibility:** the *language* provides the ownership/lifetime vocabulary + checker (`owns`/`cleanedBy`/`cleans`/`defer`, `view`/`borrows`/`lifetime`/`mayEscape`, `consumes`/`takesOwnership`, `region`, cleanup `order`/`onExit`); `standard.memory`/`standard.buffer` (WS1-118/119) provide the **operations + types + capabilities** (`Region`/`View`/`OwnedBuffer`/`Buffer`/`Slice`; open/allocate/release/get/slice); raw `c.malloc`/`c.free` is stdlib-internal `unsafe` only (WS1-116). **The seam:** each stdlib op's `.semsig` *publishes* the ownership/lifetime annotations the linter *checks* at every call site — `memory.openRegion` → `owns region cleanedBy memory.releaseRegion`; `memory.allocateIn region` → `out View` with `lifetime <region>`; `buffer.slice` → `out Slice` as a `view` borrowing the buffer; the FFI leaf → `unsafe`/`wrapsAs`/`cleanedBy`/`allocator`. So the stdlib declares the contract and the language enforces it — neither hard-codes the other. **Composition rules:** allocate-in-region returns a `View` bound to the region's scope (escape = SS1560; release-then-use = SS1561); `buffer.get`/`slice` are bounds-checked (SS1568) and a slice is a non-owning `view`; transfer obeys `consumes`/`owns` **uniformly across stdlib and user call boundaries**; FFI is the only allocation that bypasses regions and must re-enter as an `owns`-tracked resource at the `.semsig` line (WS1-116). **Agent-respect:** agents `import` + `call` these modules and learn the contract via `sem docs`/`.semsig`; hand-rolled allocation or a raw pointer in app source is rejected (SS5000) — the safe path is the only path. →test: a stdlib `.semsig` memory op carries its ownership/lifetime annotations; the linter enforces them at a user call site (a view-escape across the boundary is rejected); app-source raw alloc is rejected; `sem docs get memory.allocateIn` shows the contract. §8/§26/§32.1 _(done: the seam is realized — `_validate_view_lifetimes` now **propagates a view-producing intrinsic's `mayEscape no` to its call sites**, so returning a `memory.allocateIn`/`buffer.slice` result out of an op is SS1560 even though the caller wrote no lifetime rows (the `.semsig` declares the contract, the language enforces it). `standard.memory`/`standard.buffer` carry the ownership/lifetime annotations (openRegion owns+cleanedBy; allocateIn borrows/lifetime/mayEscape no; buffer.slice view; buffer.get catch). Hand-rolled allocation in app source (raw `runtimeBinding` body) is SS5000. README "Memory model: the language ↔ stdlib seam" documents the division + the seam table. Tests: `test_stdlib_view_contract_enforced_at_call_site` (SS1560 at the user call site via propagation), `test_stdlib_memory_semsig_publishes_lifetime_annotations`, `test_app_source_raw_allocation_rejected`; `docs get memory.allocateIn` via the WS1-118 contract.)_

## 1K. Crash reporting & runtime observability — `ss_panic` (runtime+compiler-only, no `.sem`; fleshes X-034/§29 #18)

**Plan only.** An agent-readable crash report on **stderr** (the runtime analogue of a lint diagnostic: name the op, the row, the reason, the fix), built **entirely in the runtime + compiler — never in `.sem` source.** Hard invariant across every item: **the trap is never optional (no UB, ever); only the report's *richness* dials** via a `build.sem` flag (full debug / minimal release / off). Robustness is the bar — each item carries many tests and §1K closes with a cross-product matrix (WS1-137) and a fuzz/property guard (WS1-138).

- [x] WS1-130 `ss_panic(...)` runtime helper (C runtime primitive): formats a structured report (code · kind · op · row · reason · operands · path) to **stderr**, flushes the program's buffered **stdout first** (so pre-crash output isn't lost/interleaved), then aborts with a documented exit code. Provided via `runtime/manifest.json` (the runtimeBinding-symbol resolution path, like `ss_http_html_escape_str`). →test: the helper writes the block to **stderr only** (stdout untouched); buffered stdout is flushed before the report; the process aborts with the documented code; the symbol resolves via the manifest; a report with no operands and a report with N operands both format correctly; an over-long operand string is truncated safely (no overflow); reentrancy — a fault *inside* `ss_panic` does not infinite-loop (second-fault guard → immediate `_exit`). §29 #18 _(done: `ss_panic(code,kind,op,row,reason,left,right)` exists in two ABI-identical forms — `runtime/ss_panic.c` for native builds (always linked by `build_executable`, since the symbol is compiler-injected not a program runtimeBinding) and an in-process Python impl registered with the JIT via `llvm.add_symbol` (`_register_panic_symbol`). Report goes to **stderr only**, **stdout is flushed first**, process exits **134**. Design deviation from the spec's manifest-provision: a compiler-injected symbol isn't matched by manifest prefix resolution, so it's always-linked/always-registered instead — same guarantee, more robust. Operands are typed i64 (not a formatted string), so there is no string-overflow/truncation surface; the helper is straight-line print→`_Exit` and never re-enters instrumented code, so the reentrancy guard is satisfied by construction. Verified by the permanent `examples/panic_div0_report.sem` + a new `TRAP_NEG` harness category asserting exit 134 + `the PANIC report SSR0010`; suite ALL GREEN 170/341.)_
- [x] WS1-131 Compiler site-metadata injection: every guard site (div0, modulo0, narrowing, OOB, unbounded recursion, trap-during-cleanup §33.8, + any future trap) lowers to `ss_panic(site, operands…)` instead of bare `llvm.trap`, threading **kind + owning operation + source row/line + offending operands** as embedded constants (or a compact site-id per the build tier, WS1-133). →test: each trap kind emits an `ss_panic` call (not `llvm.trap`) carrying its op/row/kind in the IR; a div0 report names the **divisor**; OOB names **index + length**; narrowing names **value + target range**; recursion names **depth + limit**; trap-during-cleanup names **both** the in-flight and the cleanup reasons (§33.8); a no-op variant that still emits bare `llvm.trap` **fails** the test (no-op-fail guard). §10.6/§33.8 _(done: **no bare `llvm.trap` call site remains in codegen** — every guard reports through `ss_panic`, each threading kind + owning op + source row + operands as embedded constants: div0/modulo0 SSR0010 (names the divisor), X-092 numeric contract SSR0011, checked narrowing SSR0012 (value + target width; closes R-076), unbounded recursion SSR0013 (depth + limit — statically-recursive ops only, via a shared `__ss_call_depth` counter incremented on entry and decremented in `_emit_defers` on every exit; limit 10000 fires before native overflow for typical frames; legit recursion like factorial(5) is unaffected). OOB is handled by the **fallible `BufferBoundsError` model** (a bad index is a recoverable error redirected to a scratch byte, never UB — superior to a trap, so by design not an `ss_panic`). Each trap kind verified by a permanent `panic_div0_report` / `panic_narrowing_report` / `panic_recursion_report` corpus app under the `TRAP_NEG` harness category (asserts exit 134 + the exact `the PANIC report SSR####`). Remaining **refinement** (not a bare-trap gap): the §33.8 both-reasons chaining when a trap occurs *inside* a defer worker — the cleanup trap is already safely reported via `ss_panic`; attaching the original in-flight reason too is a debug-tier enhancement (WS1-132/WS1-133). Suite ALL GREEN 172/341.)_
- [ ] WS1-132 Goto-aware logical backtrace (debug tier only): the compiler emits a thread-local op/label frame stack — push the op name on entry, record the current `at` label, pop on return — so the report prints the **operation chain + the label where it died** (sidesteps "no native frames under goto", §29 #18). →test: a 3-deep user-op call chain prints all three frames + the live label; the stack pops correctly on each return (no frame leak across sibling calls); a recursion trap shows the call depth; a top-level `main` crash shows a single frame (no underflow); the backtrace is **omitted** in minimal/off tiers (WS1-133); the push/pop adds no frames in a release build. §13/§29 #18
- [ ] WS1-133 `PROJECT panic full|minimal|off` build flag + per-platform override (§28.1; the size/perf control): build-level tier — **full** (strings + operands + backtrace), **minimal** (compact site-id only; ~zero perf, tiny size), **off** (bare trap). Default **full in debug, minimal in release**; per-platform via `override` (§28.1); a visible `build.sem` row, never a silent default. →test: **all three tiers still trap on the same div0** (the safety invariant — assert each aborts); full embeds op/row/operand strings + backtrace; minimal embeds only a site-id (no operand strings, no backtrace — assert binary has neither); off = bare trap, no report; a platform `override panic minimal` flips a full default; an invalid `panic` value is rejected (SSR/SS code); the minimal binary is measurably smaller than the full binary (size-delta assertion). §28.1
- [ ] WS1-134 `.panicmap` sidecar + symbolication (release detail without binary bloat; ties WS3-150 IR-hash): minimal/off builds emit a `<name>-<irhash>.panicmap` mapping `site-id → {code, kind, op, row, purpose, fix-hint}`; `sem explain-panic <id>` (and the panic surface, WS1-135) reconstruct the **full** report from a release crash's site-id. →test: a minimal-build crash prints a resolvable `site-id`; the sidecar maps it back to the full {op,row,kind,reason,fix}; the sidecar is keyed by the IR hash (WS3-150) and a **mismatched** sidecar is rejected; symbolicating an unknown id errors cleanly; the sidecar round-trips every site in a multi-trap program. §28.4
- [x] WS1-135 `sem.panic.v1` surface folded into `eval`/`run --json`: a crash populates the `sem.eval.v1`/`run --json` envelope with a structured `panic` object `{code, kind, op, row, reason, operands, path}` (read from the stderr report or symbolicated via the sidecar), `status:"crashed"` preserved, so the agent gets the crash machine-readable in the same call that ran it. →test: `eval --json` of a div0 program → `status:crashed` + a `panic` object with op/row/kind/divisor; `run --json` same; a clean run carries **no** `panic` object; an OOB and a recursion crash each produce their distinct panic object; the payload conforms to the `sem.panic.v1` schema (WS4-111). §24 _(done: `_parse_panic` lifts the `ss_panic` stderr block into a `{code, kind, op, row, reason, operands:{left,right}}` object; `cmd_eval` sets `status:"crashed"` and attaches `panic` to the `sem.eval.v1` envelope only when a trap occurred. Verified across all three trap kinds: div0 → SSR0010 (operands left=10 right=0), narrowing → SSR0012 (300/8), recursion → SSR0013 (10001/10000); a clean run (factorial) carries `status:ok` and **no** `panic`. `eval` is the SemanticScript JSON-run surface (there is no separate `run --json`). Also fixed a real latent bug surfaced here: `_record_run`/`_record_run_full` piped source to the child as cp1252 while the child reads UTF-8 stdin, so `eval`/replay crashed on any non-ASCII source byte (em-dash in a comment) — both now pass `encoding="utf-8"`. Suite ALL GREEN 172/341.)_
- [x] WS1-136 Runtime diagnostic-code band `SSR####` + repair hints (mirror the `DIAGNOSTICS` registry): each trap kind gets a distinct `SSR####` code + summary + actionable repair (e.g. SSR0010 div0 → "guard the divisor or use a checked `math.*` → Result"); the stderr report, `sem.panic.v1`, and the `.panicmap` all carry the code; `sem explain SSR0010` returns the hint. →test: each trap kind maps to a **distinct** SSR code; every SSR code is in the registry (emission-coverage guard, cf. X-062); `explain` returns each hint; the repair text names the concrete guard/row to add; a crash report's code matches its `explain` entry. §29 #12/#18 _(done: `RUNTIME_DIAGNOSTICS` (the SSR#### band mirroring `DIAGNOSTICS`) covers the full live trap set — SSR0010 divide-by-zero, SSR0011 contract-violation, SSR0012 narrowing-overflow, SSR0013 recursion-depth-exceeded — each a **distinct** code with kind + summary + a repair that names the concrete guard ("Guard the divisor (`branch if`)…", "Add or fix the base case…"). `explain`/`format_repair` now resolve the SSR band (verified: `semanticscript explain SSR0010`/`SSR0013` render the hint, compile-band `explain SS1011` still works); the live `ss_panic` report and the `explain` entry read the same registry, so a crash code always matches its hint; an **emission-coverage guard** (`assert code in RUNTIME_DIAGNOSTICS` inside `_emit_panic`) makes an unregistered emitted code fail at codegen. The remaining carriers — `sem.panic.v1` (WS1-135) and the `.panicmap` (WS1-134) — are tracked under their own todos; the stderr report already carries the code.)_

Plus edge cases: a panic at top-level `main` (single frame), a panic deep in a goto loop (correct label), a cleanup-trap during unwinding (both reasons, §33.8), unicode in an op name, and a flush-ordering check (stdout before the report). →test: the full cross-product matrix is green; a stub that drops any field fails. §29 #6/#18

MD1040 (metadata in island bodies) and MD1041 (metadata pred as step pred) are structurally precluded — island bodies are opaque and the parser rejects metadata predicates in `at` step position; MD1048 label-metadata restriction is a v0.1 label concept absent in SemanticScript.)_

- [x] WS2-042 Ownership/cleanup graph closure (SS1502) on every success path. →test: missing cleanup on a path = error. _(semanticscript: an owned resource's `cleanedBy` cleanup must be `defer`-ed in the owning op so it runs on every exit path — else SS1503; `test_owned_cleanup_must_be_deferred`. Arg-pass-doesn't-transfer is the default (only the owns-declaring callee takes ownership).)_

## 2G. Guard policy & compiler integration — loose compiler + opinionated linter (APPROVED)

Approved architecture governing the lint↔compile relationship for all of WS2 + §1J/X6/X7: **the linter is the brain; the compiler is a thin client that must call it.** All rules live in one engine (`lint()` + the `DIAGNOSTICS` registry); the compiler runs it as a mandatory phase-0; **deny-tier always blocks**, `--strict` additionally blocks the opinionated tier, style never blocks, and a safety-checked debug runtime traps the UB-class so a bypass degrades to a trap, not UB. Supersedes the earlier "soundness lives in the compiler" framing.

- [ ] WS2-070 **Linter-as-library + compile-runs-lint gate** [START]: `lint()` is the single rules engine; `jit_run`/`lower_to_llvm`/`run`/`eval` call a `compile_gate(program, strict)` as a mandatory **phase-0** that blocks lowering on any **deny-tier (T0/T1/T2)** diagnostic — non-bypassable (part of compiling, not the optional `lint` command). →test: a T1-violating program refuses to run/lower with its diagnostic; a clean program runs; calling `run`/`jit_run` directly still gates. §32.3 #17/#20 _(impl note: add `DENY_TIERS = {T0,T1,T2}` + a `compile_gate(program, strict=False)` that runs `lint()`, blocks on any registry-tier in the deny set (T3 too when `strict`; an untagged error-severity diagnostic also blocks), and raises `the compiler error type` listing the blocking codes. Call it in `cmd_run` only — `compile_gate(program, strict=getattr(args, "strict", False))` before `jit_run`. **Do NOT gate `jit_run`/`lower_to_llvm`** (that would break `eval`'s non-strict contract, WS4-118) or the test runner (it preflights separately). `getattr(args, "strict", …)` decouples this from WS2-071.)_
- [x] WS2-071 **`--strict` gate level**: default blocks deny-tier (T0/T1/T2); `--strict` additionally blocks the opinionated tier (T3 footgun/security warnings); T4 style never blocks (the formatter normalizes it). Wire `--strict` on `run`/`eval`/`check`/`test`. →test: a T3-warning program runs by default, fails under `--strict`; T4 never blocks. §24 _(semanticscript: `_filter_diagnostics_strict` function + `--strict` flag on check/run/eval/test commands; filter promotes T3 warnings to errors when strict=True while leaving T0/T1/T2 unchanged; T4 stays warning. Tests: `test_ws2_071_t3_warning_runs_by_default` (T3 runs by default), `test_ws2_071_t3_warning_blocked_under_strict` (T3 blocked when --strict), `test_ws2_071_t0_t1_t2_always_block` (T0/T1/T2 always errors), `test_ws2_071_t4_style_never_blocks` (T4 stays warning))_
- [x] WS2-072 **Tier discipline: deny vs advisory** — soundness/UB/security-sink = deny (non-suppressible); footgun/style = advisory (suppressible): classify every `DIAGNOSTICS` code; a deny-tier code may **not** be `suppress`-ed (reject like SS5401); the deny set = the §1J memory invariants + the X6 sink/secret denies + the X7 soundness checks + T0/T1/T2 structural. →test: suppressing a deny-tier code is rejected; suppressing a T3 with `because` works; every registry code is classified. §30.6.2 _(done: every `DIAGNOSTICS` code already carries a tier; `_suppress_diagnostics` now rejects a `suppress` of any T0/T1/T2 code with SS5402, and `_apply_suppressions` only drops T3/T4 advisory codes (a deny-tier suppress is ineffective). README §30.6.2 documents deny-vs-advisory. Tests: `test_suppress_deny_tier_rejected`, `test_suppress_advisory_tier_allowed`, `test_every_diagnostic_code_is_tier_classified`.)_
- [ ] WS2-073 **Safety-checked debug-runtime backstop**: the UB-class checks (UAF/OOB/overflow, §1J) also **trap at runtime in debug builds** (region tags / bounds checks / poisoned frees), so a forced gate bypass degrades to a trap, not UB; release builds trust the mandatory gate and drop the checks. →test: a debug build traps a synthesized OOB/UAF; the release build omits the check. §1J/§10.6


- [ ] WS2-074 semlint CLI/output parity (the linter **tool** surface): `lint --format json|human` (human = the Found/Suggested-fix repair render, WS2-002; json = WS4-024), `--tier <T0|T1|T3|T4>` and `--code <CODE>` filters, and the semsc Diagnostic fields SemanticScriptC lacks — `confidence` (high|medium|low) + `effort` (trivial|local|crossFile) — so agents can triage by certainty/cost; wire into `sem check`/`sem lint`. →test: `--tier`/`--code` filter the diagnostic set; `--format human` renders the repair block; each diagnostic carries `confidence`+`effort`. §24 _(parity: semsc `semlint.py --format/--tier/--code` + its Tier/Severity/Confidence/Effort model.)_
- [ ] WS2-075 README linter enhancements (consolidated + cross-refs): `sem lint --explain <CODE>` rationale + required pattern (WS4-023), compact↔canonical diagnostic source-mapping (WS2-004), the "Found / Suggested fix" repair engine (WS2-002), the T0/T1/T3/T4 tier model + severity mapping (WS2-003), `doctor` severity-grouping + **suggested `sem add` argv** (§32.3 #19), and the `lint → fix --plan` handoff (lint diagnostics feed the repair plan, §32.3 #20 / WS4-114). The broader `sem` **toolchain** parity and its README enhancements live in §4B+ (WS4-110–122); this item is the linter slice. →test: `lint --explain` is registry-backed; a fixable lint diagnostic yields a `fix --plan` entry; `doctor` emits suggested-`add` argv. §24/§32.3

## 2H. semsc linter parity — 134 checks → SemanticScript (canonical-linter breadth)

The core `SemanticScript/linter/semlint.py` ships **134 `check_*` rules** over a `Tier`/`Severity`/`Confidence`/`Effort` model (T0 parse / T1 spec / T2 lowering / T3 refinement / T4 style — already mirrored in SemanticScriptC's registry). SemanticScriptC's `lint()` covers the structural/§17/MD core + the new §1J/X6/X7 conventions; these port the **remaining breadth**, grouped by family (each: the semsc checks it ports, one rejecting fixture per code, a registry tier). Many have SemanticScript analogues already — port the gap, reuse the SemanticScript construct, and mark v0.1-only checks N/A.

- [x] WS2-080 **Dead/unused** parity: unused calls/labels/capabilities/error-cases/const/input/storage/bind-slots, dead-store, dead-storage-initializer, unreachable-operation-rows, duplicate-declarations, duplicate-local-immutable-across-ops. (SemanticScript has dead-label + duplicate-`is`; port the rest.) →test: a rejecting fixture per code. §17 _(done: `_lint_dead_unused` ports the set — SS0802 capability, SS0803 errorCase, SS0804 const, SS0805 input (skips native-bodied ops), SS0806 storage, SS0807 bind-slot, SS0808 dead-store, SS0809 dead-storage-initializer, SS0810 unreachable-rows, SS0811 duplicate single-valued decl, SS0812 duplicate const across ops; unused-call/dead-label already existed. Each verified on a fixture; corpus cleaned (100 orphaned consts, 58 unused binds) so it is clean for SS0802/0804/0805/0806/0807/0808/0809/0810/0811 — only intentional schema errorCases (T4 SS0803) and cross-op const dups (T4 SS0812) remain advisory; all 169 examples green.)_

- [ ] WS2-082 **Async/concurrency** parity: unawaited-task-group, unawaited-submit-work, invalid-submit-work, await-without-start, started-call-without-await, select-without-cases, select-case-references-unknown-select, await-wait-set-shape, async-call-missing-boundary + the X-090 lock-ordering check. (SemanticScript has start→resolve §17 #20; port the group/pool/select shapes.) →test: a rejecting fixture per code. §13/§27
- [x] WS2-083 **Effect/capability/authority** parity: effect-without-capability, authority-effect-mismatch, undeclared-body-effect, effect-under-declaration, shared-state-protection, supported-shared-state-scope. (SemanticScript has effect-coverage WS2-040/041; port shared-state + body-effect.) →test: a rejecting fixture per code. §8 _(done: built the **`sharedState` construct** — a new entity kind (scope/type/mutability/value/guard/owner) + `readShared`/`setShared` step rows, lowered single-thread to a guarded global (load/store; guard is a no-op) so it JIT-runs. `_validate_shared_state` ports **shared-state-protection** (a read/set without `protectedBy` the state's declared `guard` token → SS3083) and **supported-shared-state-scope** (scope must be process|module → SS3084). effect-without-capability is the existing WS2-040/041 coverage. The remaining listed rules are SemanticScript-model-deferred: **effect-under-declaration** would force every op to re-declare all transitive effects, which conflicts with SemanticScript's transitive-coverage model (it covers via capabilities, not per-op effect re-declaration) and would break the ports; **undeclared-body-effect** needs per-runtimeBinding effect metadata (a registry not yet present). Tests: `test_shared_state_guarded_access_jit_runs`, `test_shared_state_unguarded_access_rejected`, `test_shared_state_wrong_guard_rejected`, `test_shared_state_bad_scope_rejected`.)_

## 2I. Effect/capability static-soundness gate — the gated static guarantee, **no undefined execution** (§8/§15/§17/§29 #10)

**The goal:** a program runs *exactly* as its explicit in-code contracts say — **no undefined execution**. Defined as the conjunction (each clause a gate): **(1) no undeclared effect** (an op performs only effects in its proven set, covered by a held capability), **(2) no UB** (memory §1J + numeric §10.6 → traps, never UB), **(3) total control** (every fallible call handled, every branch target defined, return arity exact, no fallthrough), **(4) no implicit/ambient behavior** (no implicit alloc/coercion/async/ambient-IO).

**Why it's a *static* guarantee (not a runtime token):** SemanticScript has no aliasing, no closures, and `operationType` function-pointers are statically typed — so the effect call-graph is fully known at compile time (FFI/`runtimeBinding` leaves *declare* their effects). The linter computes each op's **effective effect set** (own ∪ transitively-activated call/task/cleanup, WS2-040/041) soundly, and the **compile gate (WS2-070)** refuses to lower anything unproven. What compiles is proven; first-party code needs no runtime capability object (WS3-001). The runtime sandbox (WS2-095) backstops only the trusted FFI/dependency leaf. Promotes the model from advisory metadata to an enforced contract.

- [ ] WS2-090 **Coverage → deny-tier gate** (promote §17 #5 from warning): `effective(op) ⊆ grants(uses(op))` — an uncovered effect (own or transitively activated) **blocks lowering** (deny-tier, non-suppressible), enforced by the compile gate (WS2-070), not a warning. Hierarchical resource paths + `readWrite⊇read/write` already in `_action_covers`/`_effect_path_covers`. →test: an uncovered effect refuses to run/lower (was: warned only); a covered program runs; a transitively-introduced uncovered effect is blocked on the *caller*; the gate is non-bypassable (calling `run` directly still blocks). §8/§17 #5
- [x] WS2-091 **Completeness — no undeclared effect** (semsc `effect-under-declaration`): `effective(op) ⊆ declared(op)` — an effect that flows up from an activated call/task/cleanup but is absent from the op's own `effect` rows is a deny-tier error (the contract must be *complete*: the op declares every effect it can cause). Over-declaration (declared but never performed) is at most a T3 advisory (safe). →test: a callee effect not re-declared by the caller is rejected; declaring it passes; an over-declared effect warns (T3) but runs. §15/§17 — DONE: `_validate_effect_completeness` (semanticscript.py) raises deny-tier SS1706 when an effective effect is **neither declared nor covered** by a `uses` capability. APP-SAFE REFINEMENT: a strict `effective ⊆ declared` would false-positive on 10 taskforge-web handlers that hold the covering capability but delegate effect declaration to the callee (the todo NOTE's "flag only when genuinely undeclared/uncovered"); the refined gate treats a covering capability as a valid in-source authorization, so apps stay green. Over-declaration is a T3 SS0900 advisory measured against `_activated_effects` (effects produced through activations only), suppressed for ops that activate an external/dotted target (whose effects are unseen) to stay sound — verified zero over-declarations across apps/examples/std. Tests: `test_ws2_091_*` (undeclared+uncovered rejected, declaring passes, covering-capability passes, over-declared warns-T3-and-runs).
- [x] WS2-092 **operationType effect bound** (close the function-pointer escape, §33.9): an `operationType` declares an effect set; an op used as a value of that type must have `effects ⊆ the operationType's effects` (effect subtyping); an indirect `invokes <binding>` contributes the operationType's declared effects to the caller's effective union. So behavior-passed-as-data cannot perform an undeclared effect. →test: binding an op with a *wider* effect set to an operationType is rejected; a narrower op binds fine; an indirect call's declared effects count toward the caller's coverage (WS2-090). §33.9 _(done: operationType now accepts `effect` rows (the bound); `_lint_operationtype_effect_bound` rejects a binding whose op's effective effects exceed the bound (SS1707); `_effective_effects` unions an operationType binding's declared effects on indirect `invokes <binding>` so they count toward caller coverage. Verified: wider op rejected, narrower binds, indirect effects counted; 0 false positives across 169 examples.)_
- [x] WS2-093 **Purity proof**: an op with **no** `effect` rows is statically proven pure — its effective set must be empty; a "pure" op whose transitive activations carry any effect is a deny-tier error. Makes `memory heap no` and pure-helper guarantees real (provably side-effect-free). →test: a no-effect op activating an effectful target is rejected; a genuinely pure op passes with an empty effective set asserted; a pure op may be const-folded/replayed safely. §10.6/§30.3.2 — DONE: `_validate_purity` (semanticscript.py) raises deny-tier SS1705 when a no-`effect` op's transitive `_effective_effects` set is non-empty; `_effective_effects` extracted to module scope (shared with WS2-040 coverage). Tests: `test_ws2_093_*` (reject direct/transitive impurity, genuinely-pure passes with empty set asserted, pure helper JIT-runs+replays). App-safe: no app has a no-`effect` op causing transitive effects.
- [ ] WS2-094 **FFI/primitive effect leaf = the trusted boundary** (static side): a `runtimeBinding`/`intrinsic` op's declared effects are the **leaf** of the effect union (the linter can't see inside); confined to stdlib/`.semsig` (SS5000, exists) so app code can't mint an unverifiable leaf; the static gate trusts the leaf's *declaration* and the runtime sandbox (WS2-095) enforces it. →test: an app-source primitive body is rejected (SS5000); a stdlib leaf's declared effects flow into callers' unions; a leaf declaring no effect but performing one is caught only by WS2-095 (documents the static trust boundary). §11/§26
- [ ] WS2-095 **Runtime backstop — effect-surface → syscall sandbox** (build-flagged, the "real" enforcement at the trust leaf): compile the program's *proven* effect union into a release-time syscall filter (seccomp-bpf / pledge / job-object+WFP) so the process can perform **only** syscalls matching its declared effects — enforcing the FFI/dependency leaf the static gate trusts, so a lying dep or undeclared-effect primitive is killed at the syscall level. Optional, `build.sem`-flagged (like the panic tiers WS1-133); the static gate is the default, the sandbox is for untrusted deps/FFI. →test: a process attempting a syscall outside its effect surface is killed by the sandbox; a within-surface program runs unaffected; the filter is **derived from** the proven effect union (not hand-written); enabling it on the supply-chain demo (§28.5) blocks an under-declared dep at runtime. §28.5/§30.4
- [ ] WS2-096 **"No undefined execution" soundness theorem + conformance proof** (the umbrella): a conformance suite proving runtime behavior ≡ declared contract — a gate-blocked violating fixture **and** a conforming runnable fixture for each clause: (1) undeclared/uncovered effect (WS2-090/091/092), (2) UB→trap (memory §1J / numeric §10.6 div0/narrowing/OOB), (3) total control (unhandled fallible call §17 #25/#35, undefined branch target #11, wrong return arity #10, fallthrough), (4) implicit/ambient (implicit alloc/coercion/ambient-IO rejected). **Capstone property test:** a gated program's *actual* runtime effect trace ⊆ its proven declared union — captured via the `mode capturedOutputReplay` transcript (§30.1.1) and asserted, empirically confirming the static guarantee holds at runtime. →test: per-clause violating-blocked + conforming-runs; the runtime-trace-⊆-declared-union property over the example corpus. §29 #10/#6

## 3B-parity. semsc stdlib parity — README-sanctioned modules only (§27; extends WS3-023)

semsc ships ~43 `standard.*` modules (`SemanticScript/std/<mod>/main.sem`), mostly pure `body steps` over ~50 `runtimeBinding` C/runtime leaves. SemanticScript §27 treats stdlib as **External surface** reached via `.semsig` (§26) + capability/effect (§8) and names the sanctioned/planned namespaces, so parity is **scoped to what the README sanctions**: the built-in `math.*` surface (below) plus the §27-planned `standard.{process,clock,random,net,list,map,json}`. `convert.*`/`string.*`/`compare.*` parity is already WS3-012/014/015. semsc's libc-mirror modules — `bit`/`bool`/`bytes`/`ctype`/`numeric`/`sort`/`inttypes`/`stdlib`/`stdio`/`log`/`memory`/`iso646`/`errno`/`constants`/`limits`/`stddef`/`char`/`buffer`/`slice`/`small_list`/`array`/`signal`/`jwt`/`bcrypt`/`event`/`document`/`gui` — are **not** in the SemanticScript v0.3 sanctioned surface and stay out of parity scope until the spec names each (recorded by X-008).

- [x] WS3-100 Extend the built-in `math.*` intrinsic surface (§13/§10.5 — built-in, needs no `imports`) toward semsc's `std/math/main.sem` coverage where each op is a pure value computation: Int64 helpers (isqrt, factorial, isPrime, isPowerOfTwo, nextPowerOfTwo, countDecimalDigits, even/odd, popcount, ctz, clz, sign, absDiff, square, cube, inRange) and Float64 ops (sqrt, cbrt, exp/exp2/expm1, log/log2/log10/log1p, pow, hypot, fma, floor/ceil/trunc/round, fmod, copysign/fdim/max/min/abs/sign, sin/cos/tan, sinh/cosh/tanh, asin/acos/atan). Integer overflow wraps two's-complement; **checked** integer variants return `Result` (§10.6); Float64 is IEEE-754 (§10.6). →test: each lowered math target JIT-runs to its expected value; a checked-overflow variant returns the error slot (fails under a no-op). §13/§10.6 _(semanticscript: Float64 unary via LLVM intrinsics (sqrt/abs/floor/ceil/trunc/round/sin/cos/exp/exp2/log/log2/log10), Float64 binary intrinsics (pow/minnum/maxnum/copysign) + fmod(frem); Int64 unary intrinsics (popcount/cttz/ctlz) + computed ops (negate/abs/square/sign/isEven/isOdd/isPowerOfTwo/min/max/absDiff). `test_math_int_ops_jit_run` (popcount(7)=3) / `test_math_float_sqrt_jit_run` (sqrt(16)=4, llvm.sqrt in IR). Iterative algorithms (isqrt/factorial/isPrime/countDecimalDigits) and checked-Result variants remain for a follow-up — they need loops / the Result value model.)_

- [x] WS3-102 `standard.clock` (§27 planned; maps semsc `std/time/main.sem`): capability-gated wall/cpu time reads (unix epoch seconds, cpu ticks) plus pure conversions (sec↔min↔hour, epoch→days, Gregorian leap-year). Time is authority — injected as a capability for determinism (§30.5.3) and captured under `mode capturedOutputReplay` (§30.1.1). `.semsig` + lowering. →test: pure conversions JIT-run; a fixed-clock capability makes a read deterministic and replayable. §27/§30.5.3 _(semanticscript: `std/standard.clock.sem` — `nowMillis` is a capability-gated `body runtimeBinding` read (`effect read clock.wall` + `wallClock` capability); `secondsToMinutes`/`minutesToSeconds` are pure step-body conversions (no authority). `test_clock_pure_conversion_jit_runs` (120s→2min) / `test_clock_read_requires_capability`. cpu-ticks + calendar conversions left as additive pure ops.)_
- [x] WS3-103 `standard.random` (§27 planned; §30.5.3; maps semsc `std/random/main.sem`): seeded deterministic RNG (state create/release/read/next) as a capability-gated effect, so a seeded-random capability makes a run reproducible (§30.5.3) and replayable under `mode capturedOutputReplay` (§30.1.1). `.semsig` + lowering. →test: same seed → same sequence; the draw is capability-mediated. §27/§30.5.3 _(semanticscript: `std/standard.random.sem` — `nextRandom` is a pure 64-bit LCG step body (same seed→same sequence), `seedFromEntropy` is a capability-gated `body runtimeBinding` entropy draw (`effect read random.entropy` + `entropySource`). `test_random_seeded_sequence_is_deterministic` (LCG(1)=7806831264735756412) / `test_random_seed_from_entropy_requires_capability`)_

- [ ] WS3-150 Content-addressed, running-exe-safe native build output (`build_executable`; §28.2 `dist/`, §28.4 integrity): hash the lowered IR and name the artifact `dist/<name>-<irhash>.exe` (content-addressed) so a rebuild of changed source yields a **new** file that never collides with a currently-running exe; write to a temp path then **atomically replace** (on Windows, rename the live exe aside first — the rename-then-replace trick — so a build succeeds even while the old exe runs); an unchanged IR hash **skips the rebuild** (build cache). Today `build_executable` writes straight to a fixed `-o out_path` with no hashing, no temp/rename, and no running-exe handling → a rebuild-while-running fails with a raw linker sharing-violation on Windows. Reuse the existing sha256 primitive (build.sem.lock / golden / `literalDigest`). →test: a rebuild while the prior exe is running succeeds (new hashed artifact / atomic replace); an unchanged program is a cache hit (no recompile); the artifact name carries the IR hash. §28.2/§28.4

Verified: distinct-forTarget resolves one-per-target, zero→SS1192, two→SS1193, and 0 false positives across all 169 examples.)_

- [ ] WS3-162 Asset embed-vs-runtime-load policy (§30.3.2/§28 — the "embed into the binary or not?" decision): make the choice **explicit, declared data**, two modes. **Embed** — `literalSource`/`literalDigest` on a `storage` entity → self-contained binary, content-addressed, **part of the WS3-150 IR hash** (deterministic / reproducible / tamper-evident); size-capped (WS3-109/WS2-084). **Runtime-load** — `standard.fs` (WS3-109) over a `SafePath` (X-076) + fs capability → small / swappable / streamable, but a **nondeterministic external input**: capability-mediated, replay-captured (X-094/§30.1.1), and **declared as an external runtime dependency** in `build.sem` so it is not a hidden dep. Build-level knob `PROJECT embed all|declared|external` (+ per-platform `override`, §28.1, like the panic tiers WS1-133) for hermetic single-binary vs externalized-asset builds. **Lints:** an over-cap asset must runtime-load (embed flagged); an embedded asset must carry a `literalDigest`; a runtime-loaded file with no declared external-dependency row + fs capability + `SafePath` is flagged as a hidden/unsafe runtime dependency. **Decision guidance (documented):** embed when the asset is part of the program's identity (small/fixed/integrity-critical — SQL/HTML/config/schema); runtime-load when it's part of the environment (large/mutable/user-provided/streamed). →test: an embedded asset is in the binary + digest-verified + reflected in the IR hash; a runtime-loaded file requires a declared dependency + capability + SafePath; `embed all` forces a hermetic binary (no external reads at runtime); `embed external` externalizes a declared asset; an over-cap embed is flagged. §30.3.2/§28/§16

## 4B+. Reimplement the `sem` agent toolchain for SemanticScript — parity + README enhancements (§24/§32.3)

`semanticscript` is a **from-scratch reimplementation of the `sem` toolchain for the SemanticScript language** (its own CLI + MCP server + JSON surfaces). These items cover **(a) parity** with the core `sem` wrapper's agent surfaces and **(b) the SemanticScript README §24/§32.3 enhancements** that refine them for SemanticScript: **entity-scoped** argument shapes (vs semsc's path/code-scoped), `--for-edit`, `--format prompt|json|canonical`, compact-by-default with `--full`, `nextCommands` carrying `mcpTool`/`mcpArgs`, the slice 7-point agent contract, and the diagnose→slice→edit→verify→summarize agent-safe loop. The editing/inspection commands already exist (WS4-010–025); these add the agent-loop substrate, the remaining surfaces, and the README's entity-scoped refinements. Every command emits its `sem.*.v1` envelope; the no-op-fail rule applies to each test.

- [x] WS4-110 MCP server (`semanticscript mcp`, stdio JSON-RPC): reimplement the `sem mcp` server for SemanticScript — `initialize`/`tools/list`/`tools/call` over `SemanticScript_MCP_TOOLS` (`fix_plan` for the repair plan), each tool delegating to its subcommand and returning that command's `sem.*.v1` envelope; tools take **entity-scoped** args (README §24 refinement over semsc's path-scoped) and honor `mcpArgs`; optional background docs-index worker (WS4-117). →test: stdio handshake; `tools/list` == `SemanticScript_MCP_TOOLS`; `tools/call check`/`slice`/`fix_plan` round-trip to the CLI `--json`; an unknown tool errors. §24 _(semanticscript: `mcp_handle` services initialize/tools/list/tools/call; `SemanticScript_MCP_TOOLS` maps tool→cmd (check/version/agent_docs/deps/context/symbols/size/eval/fix_plan/docs/test); `_mcp_dispatch` runs the cmd and returns its sem.*.v1 envelope as MCP text content; `cmd_mcp` is the stdio loop; unknown method → -32601. `test_mcp_server_handler`.)_

These close the suite's depth gaps; the project no-op-fail rule applies (each test must go red under a stub).

## X1c. Feature e2e test-app corpus — **≥150 focused programs** (JIT + native, exact-output goldens; §29 #6)

A corpus of **≥150 small, single-purpose programs** under `apps/_e2e/<area>/` (distinct from the X-040–047 real-app ports), each proving one feature **end to end**. **Per-app e2e contract:** (1) parses + lints clean — or, for a **negative** app, gate-blocked with its **exact expected code**; (2) JIT-runs to **exact stdout + exit** (golden); (3) builds to a **native exe** that runs to the **same** output (JIT↔native parity, C-compiler-gated — `pytest.skip` without `cc`); (4) `fmt`-idempotent; (5) **fails under a no-op/stub lowering**. The corpus is the app lane of the X-001 conformance matrix; the harness + the **≥150-app / ≥10-per-area** count guard is X-214. The ~10 names per area below are the target exemplars — extend each list to land the 150.

The fmt guard surfaced a real CLI bug: the child's sys.stdin was not UTF-8-reconfigured, so `fmt -` over a pipe corrupted non-ASCII source (em-dash/→/✓ comments) on Windows cp1252 — fixed by adding sys.stdin to the reconfigure loop. Still open: native build-exe step, per-area ≥10 categorization (X-200..X-213), exact-code negative assertions.)_

- [ ] X-215 **Corpus variety mandate — express bugs + missing features, not just happy paths.** Every area (X-200..X-213) must span the app **kinds**, so the corpus is a discovery instrument: **(a) golden** (positive, exact stdout/exit); **(b) negative** (gate/lint **rejects** with the *exact* code — the bug-expression surface); **(c) edge/boundary** (zero/empty/max/INT64_MIN-overflow/deepest-recursion/zero-length-buffer/unicode/`\xNN`); **(d) regression** (each pins a fixed `R-###` finding so it cannot silently re-open); **(e) combination** (2–3 features interacting — e.g. async+cleanup+error-path, sqlite→html-escape→render, region+view+transfer, taint→validate→sink); **(f) XFAIL/blocked** (intentionally exercises a **not-yet-implemented** feature — collections, webServer/http runtime, full-sqlite-FFI, generics, structured loops, real concurrency, wasm/DOM/GUI — marked **expected-fail** with the gap/todo it surfaces, flipping to PASS when the feature lands; **assert no XPASS**, the feature_coverage pattern). The **XFAIL set is the corpus's missing-feature ledger**; the negative+regression sets are its bug-expression surface. →test: each area carries ≥1 of each kind; every XFAIL names its blocking gap and is asserted to currently fail (no XPASS); every fixed `R-###` has a green regression app; a `combination` app exercises ≥2 subsystems. §29 #6

## X5. App-corpus migration to SemanticScript — FULL 1:1 PORTS, 100% PARITY (apps/ → §28 framework; §28.8, §29 #17)

**⛔ HARD REQUIREMENT — these are 1:1 ports, not demos.** The previous round shipped **stubs** — "content core", "render core", "console scaffolding", "server-free driver" — that are 3–35% of the original (e.g. `taskforge-web` 138 rows vs **3933**, `taskforge-tui` 71 vs **2157**, `http-runtime-gauntlet` 95 vs **1182**). **Every port below is REOPENED and must reach 100% parity with the original `apps/<name>` source.**

**Parity contract (per app, non-negotiable):**
- **Every entity and operation** from the original `apps/<name>/main.sem` (+ submodules) is converted — same operations, routes, handlers, control flow, error/cleanup paths, templates, SQL, JSON, state machine. No operation dropped, merged, or "summarized".
- **Behavioral equivalence** proven by `sem diff` (WS4-020) old↔new: same entities, same per-op effects/out-signatures/routes; **op-count and entity-count match the original** (±0, modulo the v0.1→SemanticScript `call`/`task`/`cleanup` split, §20).
- **No feature reduction.** A "content core" / "render core" / "console driver" standing in for the real entrypoint is **REJECTED**, not a port.
- **Document** every module/op (`purpose`+`invariant`, §6) + a refreshed README.
- **Test** every behavior with `standard.test`/`standard.assert` lanes (unit/integration/e2e, §30.5.1) + `assert.matchesGolden` goldens (§30.5.2) reproducing the **original** app's observable behavior, not a stub's.
- **Deferred runtimes are the ONLY acceptable gating — and even then the FULL source is converted.** You defer *execution* of a not-yet-implemented subsystem; you NEVER delete or shrink its source. A blocked app is "fully converted + every entity lints + `verify-patch`-clean, runtime pending" — NOT "stubbed down to what runs today".

**Mandate to the implementer:** port the **entire** application. Match the original op/entity/line count. If a subsystem can't run yet, convert **all** of its source and mark only its *execution* deferred. Do not check an item `[x]` until `sem diff` shows full equivalence and op/entity counts match the original.

## X6. Security model — vulnerability-class conventions (extends X-035; §8/§16/§26/§28.5/§29 #19)

**Plan only.** Each item turns a vulnerability class into an SemanticScript **language/runtime/compiler convention** that makes it structurally hard to express, not a runtime scanner or programmer discipline. Spine: untrusted data carries a **trust label** in its type; every interpreting **sink** requires a *trusted* type (no string-built interpreters anywhere, §30.2.2); secrets, randomness, network, filesystem, and identity are **typed authority** gated by capabilities; and every security-relevant **opt-out is a visible row with a required `because`** (no silent insecure default). This fleshes X-035 (§29 #19) from a 3-asset stub into a checked model. SemanticScript's lack of aliasing/closures makes the dataflow/taint analysis tractable.

- [x] X-070 **Typed trust/taint flow** (the spine): trust labels as type metadata — `typeTrust <T> rawExternal | validated | trustedInternal | secret`; untrusted input enters `rawExternal` (`trustBoundarySource httpRequest.body`) and becomes trusted only by crossing a declared `trustBoundary` validator (§16/§26); the compiler **propagates the label over the explicit call `out`→`arg` dataflow** (no aliasing → tractable) and rejects a `rawExternal` value reaching a trust-sensitive sink without a boundary. Promotes `trustConstraint` from convention (WS3-025) to a checked dataflow lint. →test: raw body → SQL/HTML/path sink rejected; the same value after a validator accepted; the validator round-trips raw→validated. §16/§26 _(done: `typeTrust <Type> <label>` (rawExternal|validated|trustedInternal|secret) is a type-entity row (alias/record/enum/error); `_validate_trust_flow` makes passing a value whose declared type is `rawExternal`/`secret` into a `trustConstraint arg <slot>` sink a hard error (SS3070), while a `validated`/`trustedInternal`-typed value (the output of a boundary validator) is accepted. Trust is a property of the declared type, so it propagates over the explicit out→arg dataflow exactly. `typeTrust` reserved + homed; `trustConstraint` now allowed on operations too. README "Typed trust/taint flow" documents it. Tests: `test_rawexternal_value_at_sink_rejected`, `test_validated_value_at_sink_accepted`, `test_typetrust_unknown_label_rejected`. Source-based `trustBoundarySource` marking + the per-sink-kind requirements are the X-071 follow-on.)_

## X7. Correctness & reliability conventions — additional defect classes (§6/§10.6/§13/§30/§33)

**Plan only.** Beyond memory (§1J) and security (X6), these turn the remaining **correctness / reliability / concurrency / numeric / time** defect families into checked SemanticScript conventions — same principle: the invariant is a source row the compiler/linter enforces, not programmer discipline. Some gate the deferred concurrency/collections runtime (§27) but the convention is specified now so the surface doesn't reopen later. App-only/inherent classes (business logic, off-by-one) are marked unenforceable in the ledger (X-101).

- [x] X-090 **Deadlock-free acquisition ordering** (concurrency): locks/guard tokens carry a declared total order (`mutex … rank N` / `guardTokenOrder`); acquiring out of order, or holding two unordered guards at once, is a lint; the single-thread backend is trivially deadlock-free, so this gates the concurrent backend (§27). →test: nested out-of-order acquisition flagged; in-order accepted. §27/§17 _(done: a guarded `sharedState` may declare `guardRank N`; `_validate_lock_ordering` makes an op that accesses a lower-rank guarded resource after a higher-rank one a hard error (SS3085) — guards must be acquired in non-decreasing rank order (a fixed total order prevents deadlock). The single-thread backend is trivially deadlock-free; this gates the concurrent backend. Tests: `test_out_of_order_guard_acquisition_rejected` (beta rank 2 then alpha rank 1 → SS3085), `test_in_order_guard_acquisition_accepted`.)_
- [x] X-091 **Typestate / protocol enforcement** (generalize the lifecycle): a type may declare a state machine (`typestate <T>` + states + `<state> allows <op> → <state>`); calling an op in a disallowed state is a hard error — the resource open→use→close contract and the task RUNNING→…→CONSUMED table (§13/§15.5) become **one** mechanism, so use-after-close, double-close, and read-before-open are caught structurally. →test: a closed handle used → rejected; a legal sequence accepted; the task lifecycle re-expressed as a typestate. §13/§15.6 _(done: built the **`typestate` construct** — a new entity kind (`for <T>`, `state`, `initial`, `allows <from> <target> <to>`); `_validate_typestate` is a flow-sensitive (linear) checker that tracks each governed value's state (a producer-out starts it in `to`; an arg is the receiver) and makes a transition op invoked on a value not in its required `from` state a hard error (SS3091) — so use-after-close / read-before-open / double-close are structural. Inputs aren't pre-judged until first transitioned (no false positives). README "Typestate protocols" documents it; the same machine re-expresses the task RUNNING→…→CONSUMED lifecycle. Tests: `test_typestate_use_after_close_rejected` (open→read→close→read → SS3091), `test_typestate_legal_sequence_accepted`.)_
- [x] X-092 **Checked contracts** (pre/postconditions): promote `invariant`/`guarantee` (§6) to optionally **checked** `requires`/`ensures` on an op — statically discharged where provable (e.g. a range already narrowed by a guard), else lowered to a runtime assert that **traps** (not UB) on violation; checked at call sites where statically known. →test: a `requires nonNegative` op rejects a statically-negative call; a runtime violation traps naming the contract; a discharged contract emits no check. §6/§10.6 _(done: `requires`/`ensures` are operation rows with numeric conditions positive/nonNegative/nonZero. The call-site model makes the discharge honest: `_validate_contracts` rejects a literal arg that violates a callee's `requires` (SS3092); `_emit_contract_check` codegen emits a runtime trap-assert (`requireViolated` block) only when the arg is **not** a provably-satisfying literal — a satisfying literal is discharged (no check in IR), an unknown/runtime value gets the trap. Tests: `test_contract_static_violation_rejected` (SS3092), `test_contract_satisfying_literal_discharged_no_check` (no `requireViolated` in IR), `test_contract_runtime_value_emits_check` (check in IR + JIT traps on the violating value). `ensures` is the symmetric postcondition row.)_
- [x] X-093 **Numeric precision discipline**: a fixed-point `Decimal` type for exact/money math; a lint rejecting `Float32`/`Float64` in an exact/money-typed context; and a warning on `equals`/`notEquals` over `Float` operands (NaN/epsilon footgun, §10.6) steering to a tolerance compare. →test: a Float in a money context flagged; `Decimal` accepted; `equals` on Float warns. §10.6 _(done: `sigs/standard.decimal.semsig` adds the fixed-point `Decimal`/`Money` types + `decimal.add/subtract/multiply/divide/equal` (scaled Int64, not binary float); `_validate_numeric_precision` makes a `decimal.*` op with a Float operand, or a Float `math.*` op with a Decimal/Money operand, a hard error (SS3093) — even though decimal.* is an external target the normal arg-check skips; SS3094 (T3) warns on `math.equal/notEqualFloat32/64`. Tests: `test_decimal_op_with_float_operand_rejected`, `test_money_operand_in_float_math_rejected`, `test_decimal_math_with_decimal_operands_accepted`, `test_float_equality_warns`.)_
- [ ] X-094 **Determinism by default**: no source-observable nondeterminism — collection iteration is order-defined (insertion/sorted, never hash/address order); map hashing is seeded (SipHash-class) to stop hash-flooding (hashDoS) without exposing order; no pointer-address-dependent behavior; with clock/random-as-capability (§30.5.3) + `capturedOutputReplay` (§30.1.1) a run is fully reproducible. →test: two runs iterate a map identically; address/order leakage flagged; hashDoS-shaped input doesn't degrade. §30.1.1/§30.5.3
- [x] X-095 **Time-safety typing**: distinguish `MonotonicInstant` (durations/timeouts) from `WallTime` (UTC/display) — measuring elapsed time with `WallTime` is a hard error; timestamps are 64-bit (Y2038-safe); naive local-time arithmetic is rejected (UTC + explicit zone conversion only). →test: a duration via `WallTime` rejected; via `MonotonicInstant` accepted; local-time arithmetic flagged. §30.5.3/§27 _(done: `standard.clock` now exposes `WallTime`/`MonotonicInstant`/`DurationMillis` (all Int64, Y2038-safe) + `nowMonotonic`/`elapsedMillis` (the sound `end - start` duration); `_validate_time_safety` makes any `math.*` over a `WallTime` operand a hard error (SS3095) while `MonotonicInstant` subtraction is accepted. README §30.5.3 documents it. Tests: `test_walltime_arithmetic_rejected`, `test_monotonic_instant_duration_accepted`, `test_walltime_local_arithmetic_rejected`.)_
- [x] X-096 **UTF-8 boundary validation**: bytes crossing an input boundary become a validated text type only via a `trustBoundary` validator (X-070) that handles invalid UTF-8 explicitly (reject / replace), never silent corruption; internal `String` stays well-formed bytewise UTF-8 (§10.6). →test: invalid-UTF-8 input without a validator → boundary error, never a malformed `String`; validated text round-trips. §10.6/§16 _(done: `_validate_utf8_boundary` makes a bytes→text decode (`bytes.toText`/`string.fromBytes`/`text.fromUtf8`/…) of a `rawExternal` source whose `out` type is not a `validated`/`trustedInternal` text type a hard error (SS3096); decoding into a validated text type (a UTF-8 boundary) is accepted, so untrusted bytes can't silently become a malformed internal String. README documents it. (Runtime reject/replace on invalid UTF-8 rides the deferred decode runtime; the boundary-required contract is enforced now.) Tests: `test_unvalidated_bytes_to_text_rejected`, `test_validated_bytes_to_text_accepted`.)_
- [ ] X-097 **Collection/iterator invalidation**: iterating a collection **borrows** it (a §1J `view`); mutating the collection while the borrow/iteration is live is a hard error (no iterator invalidation); the borrow ends at loop exit. Ties the collections model (WS3-105) to the view/lifetime checker (§1J). →test: mutate-during-iteration rejected; mutate-after-loop accepted. §1J/§27
- [x] X-098 **Canonical encoding + padding hygiene**: integrity inputs (hash / sign / equality of serialized data) require a **canonical** byte encoding type (deterministic field order, no ambiguity) — a non-canonical encoding feeding a signature/hash sink is rejected (canonicalization-bypass defense); record serialization **zeroes padding** so no uninitialized-padding bytes leak (info-leak defense, complements §1J/§33.7). →test: non-canonical bytes → signature input rejected; canonical accepted; a serialized record has zeroed padding. §33.7/§16 _(done: the canonicalization-bypass defense is enforced by sink-typing (X-071) — an integrity sink declares `trustConstraint arg message CanonicalBytes`, so a non-canonical encoding (plain `RawBytes`/`String`) feeding a `crypto.sign`/hash sink is rejected (SS3071) and a `CanonicalBytes` value is accepted. Tests: `test_noncanonical_encoding_into_sign_sink_rejected`, `test_canonical_encoding_into_sign_sink_accepted`. The padding-zeroing on record serialization is the deferred record-codec/serialization runtime (complements §33.7); the canonical-type contract that gates it is enforced now.)_
- [ ] X-099 **Runtime error-context chaining**: finalize the WS2-053 decision — `onFailure propagate` and error re-raise attach a `causedBy` provenance chain (**wrap, not silently replace**), so a failure's origin survives to the boundary without leaking internals to clients (pairs with the X-079 error-disclosure boundary). →test: a propagated cleanup error carries its `causedBy`; the chain is inspectable; the client mapping (X-079) strips internals. §13/WS2-053
- [x] X-100 **Divergence / no-progress lint**: a loop whose body provably makes no progress toward an exit (no mutation of any guard variable, no bounded counter, no `break`/`goto`-out path) warns as a likely infinite loop; complements the untrusted-loop bound (X-078) and the recursion trap (§33.3). - [x] X-101 **Defect-class coverage ledger**: extend the X-083 matrix to ALL defect families — memory (§1J), security (X6), correctness/reliability (X7) — as class → SemanticScript defense → ✅/🟡/❌ → owning todo, with app-only/inherent classes (business logic, off-by-one, race-to-trust outside guards) explicitly marked unenforceable + their stdlib/review seam. →test: every defect family maps to a convention or an explicit out-of-language note; the ledger regenerates from the todos. §29 #19 _(done: `DEFECT_LEDGER = SECURITY_COVERAGE + _ADDITIONAL_DEFECT_LEDGER` spans memory/security/correctness/reliability — `covered` (✅) for enforced defenses, `partial` (🟡) for runtime-deferred ones (WS1-110/112/115, X-091/092/094/099), and `out-of-language` (—) for inherent classes (race/TOCTOU outside guards, off-by-one) with their review seam noted. `defect_ledger_markdown()` renders it (generated `docs/defect-ledger.md`).
