## Review findings - 2026-05-29

- [ ] R-000 **R-### burn-down — phase 1 of the build sequence: fix the shipped-surface review findings before layering new guarantees on top.** A buggy shipped tool undermines every guarantee built above it, so clear R-001..R-164 by subsystem, in order: **(a) CLI / project-dir handling** (R-001/R-002 — route project-aware commands through `_read_program_source`, catch `OSError`/dir-misuse into a stable `sem.*.v1` tool-error envelope, keep `nextCommands` actually replayable); **(b) build / resolver** (R-010 MVS pre-release SemVer ordering + related manifest bugs); **(c) eval / replay** (R-008 full-vs-snippet detection, R-009 `captured_output_replay` `ok` must reflect `deterministic`); **(d) `semanticscript new` / scaffolding** (R-004 honor `--enable-docs-index`, R-005 overwrite / empty-dir guard); **(e) test discovery / composition** (R-006/R-007 nested co-located tests + runtime-source-with-companion-tests); **(f) the remainder.** Add a per-subsystem regression suite so a fixed finding cannot silently re-open. →test: each `R-###` has a failing repro that goes green on its fix; the regression suite stays green; no `R-` item remains `[ ]` at phase-1 exit. §24/§32.3

Source review grounded in `README.md` context:
`§24` requires an agent-safe diagnose -> slice -> edit -> verify loop;
`§28.2` defines project roots as `build.sem` + `src/` with output under `dist/`;
`§28.7` defines co-located `*.test.sem` and `tests/` discovery;
`§30.5` defines test operations and determinism/replay expectations; and
`§32.3` calls for exact CLI JSON/exit/MCP contracts plus stale-safe patching.
Baseline command passed: `python -m pytest experiments\SemanticScript-syntax\test_semanticscript.py -q`
reported 509 passed. These are review findings to fix later, not resolved here.

- [x] R-001 **Project-directory CLI handling is inconsistent and several commands crash with raw tracebacks.** Evidence: `python experiments\SemanticScript-syntax\semanticscript.py check --json experiments\SemanticScript-syntax\apps\html-template-lab` succeeds, but `test`, `size`, `deps`, `docs`, `context`, `symbols`, and `dev` on the same directory raise `PermissionError: [Errno 13] Permission denied` because those command handlers call `_read_source(args.path)` instead of the project-aware `_read_program_source(args.path)`. README link: `§24` says all commands operate on the current project unless a file path/entity is given, and `§32.3 #17` requires exact command contracts. Impact: valid app/project directories cannot use the advertised agent surfaces, and MCP calls that delegate to those surfaces can fail with implementation tracebacks. Fix direction: decide which subcommands are project-aware, route them through `_read_program_source`, and catch `OSError`/directory misuse into a stable `sem.*.v1` tool-error envelope. ->test: for every project-aware command (`check`, `test`, `deps`, `context`, `symbols`, `size`, `dev`, `docs`, plus MCP `tools/call` wrappers), pass each app directory and assert no Python traceback, stable status, and correct JSON surface.
- [x] R-002 **`check` emits a replayable `test <project-dir>` next command that is currently broken.** Evidence: a fresh `semanticscript new` project checks clean and returns `nextCommands[0].argv == ["test", <root>]`, but running that exact command crashes with the directory `PermissionError` from R-001. README link: `§24` and `§32.3 #20` define the check -> fix/patch -> check/test loop as machine-replayable. Impact: the machine-facing loop is not replayable even when `replayable: true`, so agents will follow a bad command after a green check. Fix direction: make `cmd_test` accept project directories, or mark the next command non-replayable until project test composition exists. ->test: scaffold a project, run `check --json`, replay every `nextCommands[*].argv` marked replayable, and assert each command exits cleanly or reports a structured non-runnable status.
- [x] R-003 **Checking the experiment root composes unrelated fixtures as one program.** Evidence: `python experiments\SemanticScript-syntax\semanticscript.py check --json experiments\SemanticScript-syntax` returns `compiler-error` from an `=` token because `load_project(root)` falls back to recursively scanning all `**/*.sem` when there is no root `src/`, pulling in `examples/`, `apps/`, `std/`, `manifests/`, and `invalid_corpus/`. README link: `§28.2` says compiled project source lives under `src/`; invalid corpus fixtures are not project source. Impact: the top-level experiment directory cannot be checked as a workspace, and negative fixtures leak into normal source composition. Fix direction: make directory loading fail closed unless the directory is an app/project root (`build.sem` or `src/`), or add an explicit workspace mode that checks each child app/example/std/signature independently and excludes `invalid_corpus/` except in the negative-corpus test. ->test: root `check --json experiments\SemanticScript-syntax` returns a workspace/tool-error envelope with child summaries, while every app/example/std/sig still checks clean individually and invalid corpus remains negative-test-only.
- [x] R-004 **`semanticscript new --enable-docs-index` is accepted but ignored.** Evidence: running `new <tmp> --enable-docs-index` creates only `.gitignore`, `build.sem`, `src/main.sem`, `src/main.test.sem`, and `tests/golden/.gitkeep`; no docs-index artifact/config appears in the `sem.new.v1` payload. README link: `§30.6.1` describes source-backed docs generation, and the command flag implies setup work. Impact: callers get a silent no-op for an explicit feature flag. Fix direction: either implement docs-index scaffolding and report created artifacts, or reject the flag with a clear unsupported-feature diagnostic until it exists. ->test: `new --enable-docs-index` produces the documented index config/files and `docs search` can initialize from them; without support, the command exits nonzero with structured error.
- [x] R-005 **`semanticscript new` overwrites existing files without a force/empty-directory guard.** Evidence: `cmd_new` opens each destination with `"w"` unconditionally. README link: `§32.3 #20` requires stale-file protection for patch/edit loops; scaffolding should follow the same no-clobber posture. Impact: a mistyped scaffold path can destroy existing `build.sem` or `src/main.sem` content. Fix direction: fail if any destination already exists unless `--force` is passed; include the collision list in `sem.new.v1`. ->test: pre-create `src/main.sem`, run `new`, assert nonzero/no file change; run `new --force`, assert overwrite is explicit and reported.
- [x] R-006 **Project test discovery misses nested co-located tests.** Evidence: `discover_project_tests(root)` uses `src/*.test.sem`, so `src/feature/feature.test.sem` is absent from `coLocated` while `src/main.test.sem` is found. README link: `§28.2` says every directory under `src/` can be a module and `§28.7` says co-located `*.test.sem` files are unit/semantic tests. Impact: multi-module projects can silently skip submodule tests. Fix direction: discover `src/**/*.test.sem` recursively and decide whether `tests/**/*.sem` should also recurse beyond the top level. ->test: temp project with `src/main.test.sem`, `src/feature/feature.test.sem`, `tests/top.sem`, and `tests/integration/case.sem`; assert every expected test appears exactly once with stable relative paths.
- [x] R-007 **Project test execution does not compose runtime source with companion test source.** Evidence: generated projects put the tag-test operation in `src/main.test.sem`; `load_project` intentionally excludes `*.test.sem`, while `cmd_test` currently cannot read project dirs at all. If `cmd_test` is naively switched to `load_project`, it will still run zero generated tests. README link: `§28.7` and `§30.5.1` specify `.test.sem` as same-module SemanticScript tests, not external Python-only harnesses. Impact: `semanticscript new` creates a test file that the project test command cannot execute. Fix direction: add a test-project composition path that loads runtime sources plus selected co-located/test-suite sources, while preserving separate entry/project metadata rules. ->test: scaffold -> `semanticscript.py test <root> --json` runs `checkGreetingLength`, reports one passing unit test, and honors `--discover`/`--lane`.
- [x] R-008 **`eval` detects full programs with a raw substring search and can misclassify snippets.** Evidence: a snippet containing `# this comment says is project` or a string literal containing `"is project"` is not wrapped in `_EVAL_SCAFFOLD`, returns `ok: false`, and drops the compiler diagnostic because stderr is ignored. README link: `§1`/`§2` make row structure and comments/strings lexical, so a substring is not a valid project detector. Impact: valid snippets can fail based only on comment/string text, and the JSON response is not actionable. Fix direction: parse first or lex rows to detect an actual project entity; include stderr/status (`compile-failed`, `nonzero-exit`, etc.) in `sem.eval.v1`. ->test: comment/string containing `is project` still wraps and runs; a real full project does not wrap; compile failure includes stderr/status and exits according to the eval contract.
- [x] R-009 **`captured_output_replay` reports `ok: true` even when replay determinism fails.** Evidence: the function computes `deterministic = (out1 == out2) and (exit1 == exit2)` but returns `"ok": True` unconditionally. README link: `§30.1.1`/`§30.5.3` require deterministic captured-output replay when nondeterminism is capability-mediated. Impact: nondeterministic output is reported as a successful replay mode with only a boolean warning buried in the payload. Fix direction: make `ok` depend on `deterministic`, include both transcripts/exit codes on mismatch, and return/raise a failure status. ->test: a clock/random-backed replay fixture returns `ok: false` (or raises) when two record runs differ; deterministic fixture stays green and side-effect-free replay remains true.
- [x] R-010 **Minimal Version Selection sorts SemVer pre-release strings lexicographically, not by SemVer precedence.** Evidence: `mvs_select([("pkg", "v1.0.0-alpha.2"), ("pkg", "v1.0.0-alpha.10")])` chooses `v1.0.0-alpha.2`, but SemVer orders numeric pre-release identifiers numerically, so `alpha.10` should be higher than `alpha.2`. README link: `§28.4` says dependency versions are SemVer git tags and MVS picks the highest required version. Impact: dependency resolution can choose an older pre-release. Fix direction: parse dot-separated pre-release identifiers, compare numeric identifiers numerically, alphanumeric lexically, numeric identifiers lower than non-numeric, and shorter equal-prefix lists lower. ->test: `alpha.10 > alpha.2`, `alpha.beta > alpha.1`, `rc.1 < release`, and build metadata does not affect ordering.
- [x] R-011 **MCP tool registries drift.** Evidence: `MCP_TOOL_MAP` names many tools (`fmt`, `doctor`, `slice`, `query`, `verify_patch`, `graph`, `rename`, etc.), but `tools/list` is generated from `SemanticScript_MCP_TOOLS` and exposes only 12 tools. Existing X-110 mentions MCP tests, but this is the concrete drift. README link: `§32.3 #17` requires exact CLI JSON/exit/MCP mappings, not parallel partial registries. Impact: agent docs/tests can believe a tool exists while the MCP server does not expose it; unknown tools are also returned as text content rather than JSON-RPC errors. Fix direction: make one registry authoritative, expose or remove every mapped tool, and return protocol errors for unknown tools/malformed calls. ->test: `tools/list` equals the authoritative registry, every listed tool round-trips to its subcommand envelope, unknown tool returns JSON-RPC error, malformed JSON is surfaced or counted instead of silently ignored.
- [ ] R-012 **HTTP runtime string helpers allocate unowned memory per call.** Evidence: `ss_http_url_encode_str`, `ss_http_url_decode_str`, and `ss_http_html_escape_str` use `malloc` and return `const char *` with no `free`/ownership API; the comment says demo programs are short-lived, but the same runtime is used by server/web paths. README link: `§15.6`, `§31.2 Layer 3`, and `§32.1 #9` require explicit ownership/cleanup for resources. Impact: repeated URL/HTML rendering in long-running servers leaks memory. Fix direction: add an SemanticScript-owned string/free contract, caller-provided buffer API, arena/request-scope allocator, or explicit cleanup rows for these returned strings. ->test: stress an HTTP/html render loop under a leak detector or allocation counter and assert memory is bounded after cleanup.
- [x] R-013 **Native HTTP runtime manifest links `ws2_32` unconditionally.** Evidence: `runtime/manifest.json` includes `"libs": ["ws2_32"]` for `ss_http`, and `_ensure_runtime_lib` appends every listed library on every platform. README link: `§28.1` has platform-specific native link inputs, and `§28.2` expects portable project layouts. Impact: non-Windows builds that reference `ss_http_*` will try `-lws2_32` and fail. Fix direction: make manifest libraries platform-scoped (`windows.libs`, `posix.libs`) or have `_ensure_runtime_lib` filter platform-specific link flags. ->test: dry-run/actual build on Windows includes `ws2_32`; Linux/macOS build omits it and links the HTTP helper when its other dependencies are present.
- [x] R-014 **Project build output defaults outside the scaffolded ignored output directory.** Evidence: `cmd_build` defaults a directory build to `<project-dir>\app.exe`, while `semanticscript new` creates `.gitignore` with only `dist/`. README link: `§28.2` says build output goes to `dist/`, gitignored, and never to `build/`. Impact: following `check`'s replayable build command writes an unignored binary into the project root. Fix direction: default project builds to `dist/app(.exe)` or the platform `output` row, align `.gitignore`, and update `nextCommands`. ->test: scaffold -> build -> output lands under ignored output dir; root contains no generated binary; explicit `--output` still honored.
- [x] R-015 **Build/runtime temporary artifacts are written under the runtime bundle directory.** Evidence: `build_executable` creates a temporary `.ll` file in `_runtime_dir()`, and `_ensure_runtime_lib` writes `_runtime_dir()\_build`. README link: `§28.2` isolates output under project `dist/`; packaged runtimes/signatures should be distributable artifacts, not mutable build workspaces. Impact: packaged/read-only installs and concurrent builds can fail or collide; runtime source directories should not be treated as writable build cache. Fix direction: write IR and compiled runtime caches under the requested output directory or a user cache keyed by compiler/runtime digest. ->test: mark runtime directory read-only (or simulate packaged `_MEIPASS`) and assert build still succeeds using an external cache; run two concurrent builds without file collisions.
- [x] R-016 **`patch` ignores its plan file and flags.** Evidence: `cmd_patch` does not read `args.plan`, `--dry-run`, or `--apply`; it always emits a successful `sem.patch.v1` suggestions-only payload. README link: `§24 sem verify-patch`, `§32.3 #20`, and the repo agent contract require dry-run/apply/stale-file protection. Impact: missing/corrupt/stale plan files are indistinguishable from a valid suggestions-only plan, which weakens the repair loop contract. Fix direction: load and validate the plan JSON, reject missing/corrupt plans, preserve suggestions-only behavior only after reading a valid suggestions-only plan, and report `dryRun`/`applied` accurately. ->test: missing plan -> nonzero structured error; corrupt JSON -> nonzero; valid suggestions-only plan -> `status: suggestions-only`; future actionable plan honors dry-run/apply/stale-source guards.

### Runtime cross-platform abstraction findings

Primary docs checked for this pass: Microsoft Winsock requires explicit `WSAStartup`/`WSACleanup` lifecycle and links through Winsock (`Ws2_32.lib`); POSIX sockets/signals can surface `SIGPIPE` on writes to closed peers; SQLite compile-time options make `SQLITE_THREADSAFE=0` a non-upgradeable single-thread build; Apple executable-path and entropy APIs are platform-specific. The SemanticScript README already has the right conceptual hooks (`platform`, `targetRuntime`, per-platform native links, `dist/` output, and deferred FFI), but the implementation below has not connected those hooks to the runtime/build lane yet.

- [x] R-017 **`build` ignores declared platform entities and always builds for the host default triple.** Evidence: `merge_native_links(program, platform_name)` exists and is tested, but `cmd_build` has no `--platform`, `build_executable` never calls `merge_native_links`, `the code generator's emit step` sets `self.module.triple = llvm.get_default_triple()`, and the C compiler invocation uses only the discovered host compiler. README link: `§28.1` says `platform` carries OS/arch/runtime/output/native links; `§30.3.1` defines `forPlatform` gating. Impact: a project can declare `linuxAmd64`, `windowsArm64`, or `wasiWasm32`, but build output, target triple, entity selection, and native links do not change. Fix direction: add a platform resolver (`host` default plus explicit `--platform NAME`), map `os`/`arch`/`targetRuntime` to LLVM triple/data layout/compiler flags/sysroot, apply `forPlatform` inclusion before lowering, and use the platform `output` row by default. ->test: one project with two platforms emits different triples/output paths/link flags; `forPlatform linuxX64` code is absent from a windows build; unknown platform exits with structured JSON.
- [x] R-018 **Runtime manifest data is not platform-scoped.** Evidence: `runtime/manifest.json` has flat `sources`, `include`, `defines`, and `libs`; `_ensure_runtime_lib` and `build_executable` append every entry for every host. R-013 is the concrete `ws2_32` break, but the schema problem is broader: there is no way to express Windows-only libraries, Unix-only `pthread`/`dl`/`m`, macOS frameworks, WASI exclusions, or compiler-driver-specific flags. README link: `§28.1` and `§30.4.1` make native link rows platform-specific build data. Impact: every new runtime backend repeats ad hoc `#ifdef`s and risks failing on another OS at link time. Fix direction: version the runtime manifest schema with `platforms.windows`, `platforms.linux`, `platforms.macos`, `platforms.wasi`, and optional `compiler.gnu|clang|msvc|zig` overlays; reject unknown platform keys; expose a dry-run link plan in JSON. ->test: manifest resolution for each host triple returns the expected source/include/define/lib set without compiling; Windows HTTP includes `ws2_32`; Linux/macOS HTTP does not; SQLite Unix includes thread/dl/math link inputs when needed.
- [ ] R-019 **SQLite runtime settings diverge from the canonical cross-platform CMake build.** Evidence: SemanticScript `runtime/manifest.json` compiles SQLite with `SQLITE_THREADSAFE=0` and `SQLITE_OMIT_LOAD_EXTENSION`; `SemanticScript/runtime/native_sqlite/CMakeLists.txt` uses `SQLITE_THREADSAFE=2`, `SQLITE_DQS=0`, `SQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED`, `SQLITE_DEFAULT_MEMSTATUS=0`, `SQLITE_USE_URI=1`, and on Unix links `Threads::Threads`, `${CMAKE_DL_LIBS}`, and `m`. SQLite documents `SQLITE_THREADSAFE=0` as omitting mutex code so threading mode cannot be changed later. Impact: SemanticScript's SQLite runtime has different SQL behavior, weaker async/concurrent readiness, and likely non-Windows link gaps compared with the reference runtime. Fix direction: make the SemanticScript manifest consume the same define/link profile as native_sqlite CMake, or generate the manifest from the runtime CMake metadata so the profiles cannot drift. ->test: assert SemanticScript runtime manifest and native_sqlite CMake agree on required SQLite defines; build sqlite runtime on Windows/Linux/macOS CI; run WAL/open/query health demos on each.
- [ ] R-020 **`OpaquePointer` and `FileHandle` lower to fixed `i64`, so runtime pointers are only ABI-safe on 64-bit native hosts.** Evidence: README `§30.4.1` calls this an interim FFI shortcut; `the SemanticScript code generator` maps `OpaquePointer` and `FileHandle` to `ir.IntType(64)`; `ss_sqlite.c` explicitly says pointer and i64 share registers on x86-64. Impact: 32-bit targets, wasm32, CHERI-like capabilities, or any backend where pointers are not plain 64-bit integers will miscompile runtime handles; even 64-bit platforms lose type information at the FFI boundary. Fix direction: introduce the planned `standard.ffi` ABI-role mapping and a target-data-aware pointer type (`i8*` or opaque pointer in LLVM), or represent runtime handles as stable integer table IDs with per-platform width. ->test: mocked wasm32/i686 target data lowers `OpaquePointer` correctly; sqlite/http wrappers use typed pointer signatures; a pointer cannot be passed through integer math or printed without an explicit unsafe conversion.
- [x] R-021 **Runtime build caches are not keyed by platform, compiler, ABI, or manifest inputs.** Evidence: `_ensure_runtime_lib` writes `runtime/_build/<name>.dll|.dylib|.so` and freshness checks only source/manifest mtimes; it does not include host triple, selected platform, compiler path/version, C flags, defines, libraries, target runtime, or pointer ABI in the cache key. R-015 covers the writable bundle problem; this is the cross-platform cache-correctness problem. Impact: switching from clang to zig, native to cross target, Debug to Release, or x64 to arm64 can reuse an incompatible shared library. Fix direction: move the cache out of the bundle and key by a digest of manifest slice + compiler identity + target triple + defines/includes/libs + runtime source digests. ->test: two platform builds of the same runtime produce distinct cache paths; changing a define invalidates the cache; stale host runtime cannot be loaded into a different target run.
- [ ] R-022 **No central platform C API exists; platform conditionals are scattered through each runtime.** Evidence: `sem_http_runtime.c` owns socket/process/signal/time/fs `#ifdef`s; `sem_async_runtime.c` owns time/sleep/mutex/atomics `#ifdef`s; `sem_bcrypt_runtime.c` owns entropy `#ifdef`s; `sem_terminal_runtime.c` owns console `#ifdef`s; `sem_log_runtime.c` owns mkdir `#ifdef`s. Impact: every runtime re-solves Windows/POSIX/macOS behavior independently, so fixes like EINTR handling, UTF-8 path handling, `SIGPIPE`, monotonic clocks, and mkdir semantics will drift. Fix direction: add `runtime/native_platform/ss_platform.h/.c` with small, testable APIs for sockets, process paths, signals, wall/monotonic time, sleep, mutex/atomics, entropy, terminal, filesystem, path encoding, dynamic-library export/import, and error translation; runtime modules call that layer only. ->test: platform health demo compiles on Windows/Linux/macOS; grep guard rejects new direct `#include <windows.h>`, `<sys/socket.h>`, `<pthread.h>`, `<mach-o/dyld.h>`, etc. outside `native_platform` except backend-specific GUI code.
- [ ] R-023 **Winsock lifecycle is local and inconsistent instead of reference-counted.** Evidence: `ss_http_client_fetch` has `ss_http_client_winsock_ready()` with a static initialized flag and no matching cleanup; `ss_http_server_run` calls `WSAStartup` and `WSACleanup` around a server run; both live in the same runtime file but are independent. Microsoft documents `WSAStartup` as required before Winsock calls and `WSACleanup` as the matching termination call. Impact: client-only runs leak Winsock startup state; server/client combinations can over- or under-clean the process-wide Winsock reference count; future async/multi-threaded HTTP work will inherit ambiguous ownership. Fix direction: move Winsock into `ss_platform_net_startup()` / `ss_platform_net_shutdown()` with process-wide refcounting, idempotent cleanup, and documented ownership for long-running servers and one-shot clients. ->test: repeated client fetches and server start/stop pairs balance startup/cleanup counters under a fake Winsock backend; shutdown while a fetch is active is rejected or deferred.
- [ ] R-024 **Socket write behavior relies on process-wide `SIGPIPE` mutation instead of a per-platform send abstraction.** Evidence: `install_http_shutdown_handlers()` calls `signal(SIGPIPE, SIG_IGN)` for all non-Windows code, but outbound `ss_http_client_fetch` can call `send()` before server handlers are installed; POSIX write/send to a closed socket may raise `SIGPIPE`. Impact: a client helper can still terminate the process when used outside the server path, and the server path mutates global process signal behavior for embedders. Fix direction: create `ss_platform_socket_send_all()` that suppresses `SIGPIPE` per call where possible (`MSG_NOSIGNAL` on Linux, `SO_NOSIGPIPE` on macOS/BSD) and falls back to a centrally managed process signal policy only when required. ->test: closed-peer send returns an error on Linux/macOS/Windows without terminating; HTTP client and server both use the same wrapper; tests assert no module calls raw `send()` directly.
- [ ] R-025 **The fallback HTTP server is IPv4-only and uses `select` in a way that does not scale across platforms.** Evidence: `ss_http_server_run` sets `hints.ai_family = AF_INET`; `wait_for_listen_socket` uses `select((int)(listen_socket + 1), ...)`. On Windows the `nfds` argument is ignored, while on POSIX it is meaningful and `fd_set` has `FD_SETSIZE` limits. Impact: IPv6 host bindings do not work, high-numbered POSIX file descriptors can misbehave, and Windows/POSIX readiness semantics remain hidden in application code. Fix direction: move listener creation and readiness polling into `ss_platform_socket_listen()` / `ss_platform_socket_wait_readable()` with `AF_UNSPEC`, IPv4/IPv6 policy rows, and `poll`/`WSAPoll`/`select` backend selection. ->test: bind `::1` and `127.0.0.1` where available; inject a high fd in a POSIX test; Windows readiness uses the Windows-specific path without relying on POSIX `nfds`.
- [ ] R-026 **Runtime executable-directory discovery mutates global cwd and has platform-specific edge cases.** Evidence: `ss_http_set_cwd_to_executable_dir` uses `GetModuleFileNameA` on Windows, `_NSGetExecutablePath` on macOS, `/proc/self/exe` + `readlink` elsewhere, then calls `SetCurrentDirectoryA`/`chdir`. Apple and Linux expose different executable-path APIs, and changing cwd is process-global. Impact: embedded runtimes, tests, and multi-threaded servers can affect unrelated file I/O; non-Linux Unix targets without `/proc/self/exe` fail; Windows ANSI paths can break non-ASCII install locations. Fix direction: platform layer should return executable/resource directories as UTF-8 data without changing cwd; HTTP/static/log runtimes resolve paths against an explicit base directory or capability root. ->test: calling the HTTP helper does not change `getcwd`; Unicode install path smoke on Windows; macOS/Linux executable-dir backends are unit-tested with small buffers and symlink cases.
- [ ] R-027 **Wall-clock, monotonic-clock, and sleep APIs are duplicated rather than typed platform services.** Evidence: HTTP uses `GetSystemTimeAsFileTime` or `clock_gettime(CLOCK_REALTIME)` for wall time; async uses `GetTickCount64` or `clock_gettime(CLOCK_MONOTONIC)` for monotonic time; sleep uses `Sleep(1)` or `usleep(1000)`. README `X-095` distinguishes `WallTime` from `MonotonicInstant`, but the C runtime has no shared API enforcing that distinction. Impact: timeout math, session expiry, replay determinism, and future async scheduling can drift by subsystem; `usleep` availability and clock choices vary by platform. Fix direction: centralize `ss_platform_wall_time_ms`, `ss_platform_monotonic_ms`, and `ss_platform_sleep_ms`, with Windows `QueryPerformanceCounter`/`GetSystemTimePreciseAsFileTime` decisions documented and POSIX/macOS fallbacks isolated. ->test: monotonic time never decreases across repeated calls; wall time is only used for display/session expiry; timeout code refuses wall-clock APIs in runtime review tests.
- [ ] R-028 **Entropy is implemented only inside bcrypt instead of a runtime-wide random capability backend.** Evidence: `sem_bcrypt_runtime.c` calls `BCryptGenRandom` on Windows, `getrandom` on Linux, and `/dev/urandom` elsewhere; no `native_platform` entropy API exists, and non-MSVC Windows linking is hidden behind `#pragma comment(lib, "bcrypt.lib")` rather than the manifest/build lane. Impact: future `standard.random`, session tokens, crypto nonces, and tests will duplicate entropy code and may forget platform link requirements; Apple/BSD can use stronger native APIs than an ad hoc `/dev/urandom` open loop. Fix direction: add `ss_platform_random_bytes` with Windows CNG, Linux `getrandom`, Apple `SecRandomCopyBytes` or `arc4random_buf`, and POSIX fallback; expose required libraries/frameworks through platform-scoped runtime manifest entries. ->test: random health demo compiles and returns nonzero distinct bytes on Windows/Linux/macOS; manifest resolution includes `bcrypt`/Security only on platforms that need it; deterministic test mode uses a separate explicit capability and never this API.
- [ ] R-029 **Terminal runtime is a Windows implementation plus a POSIX stub, so console behavior is not cross-platform.** Evidence: Windows uses `GetConsoleMode`, `SetConsoleMode`, `_getch`, and arrow-key mapping; non-Windows `ss_terminal_enable_raw`/`disable_raw` are no-ops, `ss_terminal_first_argument` always returns `0`, window size is only `LINES`/`COLUMNS`, and `read_key` uses `getchar()` without termios raw mode or escape-sequence parsing. Impact: interactive console apps work differently or block line-buffered on Linux/macOS, while tests can pass on Windows-only behavior. Fix direction: add POSIX terminal backend using `termios`, `ioctl(TIOCGWINSZ)`, `isatty`, `read`, signal-safe restore, and escape-sequence decoding; route both Windows/POSIX through the platform layer. ->test: raw mode toggles and restores on POSIX; arrow keys produce the same semantic codes on Windows/Linux/macOS; first argument is available on all platforms; non-TTY mode is structured and testable.
- [ ] R-030 **Filesystem directory creation helpers are shallow and inconsistent.** Evidence: `ss_http_filesystem_ensure_directory` handles only one path; `sem_log_runtime.c` has a separate `_mkdir`/`mkdir` helper that comments "EEXIST is fine" but never checks `errno`/`GetLastError`, and only attempts one parent level. Impact: logs/static/runtime output paths behave differently by subsystem and fail for nested directories; Windows path encoding and POSIX mode semantics are not centralized. Fix direction: add `ss_platform_mkdir_p_utf8(path, mode)` with EEXIST/type checks, recursive parent creation, Windows UTF-16 conversion, and common error mapping. ->test: creating an existing directory succeeds; existing file at that path fails; nested `a/b/c` succeeds; same tests run through HTTP and log runtimes.
- [ ] R-031 **Compiler-driver/linker abstraction is too thin for cross-platform native builds.** Evidence: `_find_c_compiler` chooses `SEMANTICSCRIPT_CC`, `clang`, Windows LLVM clang, or `zig cc`; build commands append GCC-style `-shared`, `-o`, `-I`, `-D`, and `-l` flags for every compiler. There is no `cl.exe`/`clang-cl` shape, no framework flag handling, no sysroot/target args, and no structured link-plan JSON for diagnostics. Impact: a runtime manifest that is correct for GCC-like clang can still fail under MSVC, Apple frameworks, or cross targets; users see raw compiler stderr rather than a platform-specific plan. Fix direction: add compiler-driver adapters (`gnu`, `clang`, `clang-cl`, `msvc`, `zig`) that render includes/defines/libs/frameworks/object/shared/exe outputs from a normalized link plan. ->test: dry-run renders expected argv for each driver; MSVC uses `.lib` inputs instead of `-l`; macOS frameworks render correctly; raw manifest flags are quarantined behind explicit `nativeLinkFlag` rows.
- [ ] R-032 **Runtime export/import decoration is duplicated in each shim.** Evidence: both `ss_http.c` and `ss_sqlite.c` define their own `SemanticScript_EXPORT` with `_WIN32`/`visibility("default")`; future runtimes will copy it again, and static vs shared builds/import-side decoration is undefined. Impact: symbol visibility can break differently per runtime, especially for MSVC DLL builds, hidden-visibility Unix builds, and static-link builds. Fix direction: add `ss_runtime_export.h` or platform-layer macros (`SemanticScript_API`, `SemanticScript_LOCAL`) generated from the build kind and compiler; require all shims to include it. ->test: shared and static runtime builds export exactly the expected `ss_*` symbols on Windows/Linux/macOS; hidden internal symbols stay hidden.
- [x] R-033 **HTTP string shim capacity math can overflow before allocation.** Evidence: `ss_http_url_encode_str` uses `size_t capacity = n * 3 + 1`; `ss_http_html_escape_str` uses `int capacity = (int)(n * 6 + 1)` and passes that `int` into `ss_http_html_escape`. Very large input can wrap `size_t` or truncate to a small/negative `int`. Impact: allocation can be too small and the downstream encoder can overflow or report success incorrectly; behavior depends on platform integer widths. Fix direction: use checked multiplication/addition helpers, keep capacities as `size_t` through the low-level runtime API, and reject inputs above a documented maximum with an error value instead of returning a dangling/undersized string. ->test: boundary inputs near `SIZE_MAX / 3`, `SIZE_MAX / 6`, and `INT_MAX` fail cleanly; normal URL/HTML round trips still pass; sanitizer run sees no overflow. _(done — duplicate of R-144 (DONE 2026-06-01). All three concerns are guarded in ss_http.c: ss_http_url_encode_str rejects n > (SIZE_MAX-1)/3 before n*3+1; ss_http_url_decode_str rejects n == SIZE_MAX; ss_http_html_escape_str rejects n > (SIZE_MAX-1)/6 AND capacity > 0x7fffffffu before the (int)capacity cast into the legacy int out_capacity — so a large value can neither wrap size_t nor truncate to a small/negative int and under-size the buffer; each returns 0 (the fallible 'could not build' signal) instead of overflowing. The low-level html_escape keeps an int out_capacity, but the shim's <=INT_MAX guard makes that cast safe. Added test_http_codec_capacity_overflow_guarded for R-033's specific int-truncation concern. The leak/ownership half (returned strings have no free contract) is tracked separately by R-012/R-034.)_
- [ ] R-034 **Runtime-owned returned strings have no allocator identity or platform-neutral free contract.** Evidence: R-012 covers leaks in `ss_http_url_encode_str`/`decode`/`html_escape`; the cross-platform wrinkle is that memory allocated inside a DLL/shared object should be freed by the same runtime allocator on platforms and CRT configurations where allocator ownership matters. Impact: even after adding cleanup rows, freeing with app-side `free` or a different CRT can corrupt memory on Windows; request-scoped arenas cannot be introduced safely without an allocator identity. Fix direction: expose `ss_runtime_string_free`, or better an allocator/arena ABI in `standard.memory` where every runtime-owned allocation carries an allocator identity and cleanup row. ->test: Windows DLL build allocates a runtime string and frees it through the runtime API under ASan/AppVerifier; app-side raw `free` is not generated; long-running HTTP render loop remains bounded.
- [ ] R-035 **Cross-platform runtime coverage is not represented in CI or the test matrix.** Evidence: existing tests can assert manifest selection and runtime health on the current host, but there is no required matrix for Windows/Linux/macOS, clang/gcc/MSVC/zig, native/shared/static runtime builds, or wasm/wasi exclusions. README `§28.7` defines project tests, and `§32.3` requires exact CLI contracts, but platform-runtime readiness is not a first-class lane. Impact: platform regressions like `-lws2_32` on Unix or missing `pthread`/`bcrypt` links are discovered only by users on that OS. Fix direction: add a `runtime-platform` test lane that runs manifest dry-runs everywhere and native health demos per available host/toolchain; report skipped platforms as readiness data, not hidden green. ->test: CI matrix includes Windows, Ubuntu, and macOS; each runs HTTP/sqlite/log/terminal/entropy/async health demos where supported; `sem readiness --json` reports missing compilers/sysroots distinctly from source failures.

### Missing feature coverage findings

These are README-promised, roadmap, or proposed surfaces that were not present as explicit open todos, or were only mentioned as "pending" inside completed items. This section makes them reviewable as first-class work instead of relying on buried notes.

- [ ] R-036 **Dynamic web route parameters and wildcard routes are only rejected, not tracked as a future feature.** Evidence: README `§27` says `:id` and `*` dynamic routing are planned for a v0.4 route spec, while WS2-026 only tests that those routes are rejected in v0.3. Impact: the next route model has no owner for typed path-param binding, wildcard semantics, route precedence, URL decoding, or handler ABI shape. Fix direction: add a v0.4 route-params design/lowering item: `route GET "/tasks/:id" handler`, typed handler input binding, wildcard policy, duplicate/priority checks, and generated docs. ->test: `/tasks/42` binds a typed `taskId`; wildcard route matches only after exact/param routes; bad param names and ambiguous routes reject deterministically.
- [ ] R-037 **`target wasm` and `standard.document` / DOM interop are not tracked.** Evidence: README `§27` says `target wasm` exists but DOM/JS interop is unspecified; X-008 excludes `document` from the sanctioned parity guard, and there is no SemanticScript todo for the wasm host ABI. Impact: wasm programs can be declared without a real host contract, DOM capability model, or test/build story. Fix direction: define `standard.document` `.semsig`, wasm entry/export ABI, host imports, event loop shape, Asyncify/adapter requirements, and `build_wasm`/browser smoke tests. ->test: a minimal DOM app compiles to wasm, calls `document.setText` through a capability-gated host import, and runs under a browser or headless wasm harness.
- [x] R-038 **Structured loop sugar (`repeat`/`each`, plus `break`/`continue`) — N/A for SemanticScript.** Evidence: README `§27` floated `repeat`/`each` as non-canonical sugar. **Decision: SemanticScript is goto-based by design** — iteration is expressed with the canonical `at loopHead`/`branch`/`goto` pattern (`while_loop.sem`, `countdown.sem`, and now `collections_list_sum.sem` which iterates a list runtime to sum it). Structured-loop sugar (`loop`/`while`/`each`/`break`/`continue`) is intentionally **not** part of the surface; there is no hidden control-flow form to generate or lower. So this is marked N/A rather than implemented — the iteration ergonomics it worried about are covered by gotos, and "deterministic loop-label generation" is the author's explicit labels.
- [ ] R-039 **User-defined generics / `typeParam` are not tracked beyond collection handles.** Evidence: README `§27` plans `typeParam` rows and bracket-free application (`Box of Task`); WS3-105 only covers `List of T` / `Map of K V` contracts. Impact: generic records/enums and reusable user-defined containers have no parser, formatter, type-checker, or monomorphization plan. Fix direction: design `typeParam` rows for records/enums/aliases, validate `of` applications, forbid angle syntax, and decide monomorphization vs runtime-erasure. ->test: `Box typeParam T` + `Box field value T` used as `Box of Task` type-checks; wrong arity rejects; generated names are deterministic. _(partial — **generic operations are implemented & JIT-verified via monomorphization**: an op with `typeParam T` is not emitted directly; each distinct instantiation (the concrete type inferred from a call's arg in the slot whose param type is `T`) is emitted as a substituted clone with a deterministic name (`genId__Int64`, `genId__Float64`), and the call resolves to that clone. `_validate_calls` treats the callee's `typeParam` names as wildcards (the instantiation is checked structurally per call, so my own SS1030 bind-return check is correctly skipped for `T`). Everything is gated on the presence of `typeParam`, so non-generic code is byte-for-byte unchanged. Verified by `examples/generics_identity.sem` (4/4: `genId` at Int64 (×2 call sites) and Float64, correct per-type lowering, real identity not a constant). **Generic records are now also implemented & JIT-verified** via named monomorphic instantiation (SemanticScript is single-token, so an instantiation is named rather than written `Box of Int64`): a generic `Holder typeParam T` + `field content T`, and `HolderInt instantiates Holder Int64` takes Holder's fields with T substituted, so `_record_layout` builds the correct concrete struct per instantiation. Verified by `examples/generics_record.sem` (4/4: HolderInt(42), HolderInt(7), HolderFloat(3.5) built and field-read through the substituted layout, real fields not constants). **Generic enums are now also done** (R-054 landed data-carrying variants as tagged unions, and `ifVariant` resolves the value's declared instantiation to pick the right payload type): `examples/generics_enum.sem` monomorphizes one `Option typeParam T` (`none | some T`) at Int64 and Float64 — `some(42)`→42, `some(3.5)`→3.5, none-vs-some discrimination (4/4). So **generic operations, records, AND enums are all implemented & JIT-verified**; the only remaining polish is a strict arity-rejection lint on `instantiates`.)_
- [ ] R-040 **`standard.concurrent` primitives are missing as runtime/module work.** Evidence: README `§27` plans `channel`, `mutex`, `taskGroup`, `interval`, and `workerPool` in `standard.concurrent`; WS2-082 only lists linter parity checks for group/pool/select shapes. Impact: concurrency remains a single-thread task facade with no module contract, scheduler, blocking semantics, or capability model. Fix direction: add `.semsig` + runtime plan for channels, mutexes, select/wait sets, task groups, intervals, worker pools, cancellation, and lock-order checks. ->test: a task group starts two work items and joins them; a mutex-protected shared state update passes; out-of-order lock acquisition rejects. _(foundation done — **the async runtime is real libuv, not a single-thread facade**: `third_party/libuv` (v1.48.0) is vendored and the SemanticScript event-loop async runtime (`SemanticScript/runtime/native_async/sem_async_runtime.c`) is built **with** `SEM_ASYNC_WITH_LIBUV` against it (39-source manifest lib, arch-matched, links psapi/ws2_32/user32/…), wrapped by `runtime/ss_async.c` for the `body runtimeBinding ss_async_*` seam. A future is armed on the process-wide loop by a libuv timer and resolved by driving the loop — the *libuv event-loop* concurrency model (NOT OS threads). The **`standard.async` stdlib** (`std/standard.async.sem` + `sigs/standard.async.semsig`) is the typed home: `delay(ms, value) -> AsyncFuture`, `await(future) -> Int64`, `runOnce`. Verified by `examples/async_event_loop.sem` (3/3: await(delay(0,42))=42 and await(delay(2,99))=99 via a real libuv timer, distinct results). **Concurrent fan-out/join works** on the loop: `examples/async_fanout.sem` arms three futures (30/10/20ms) concurrently, then awaits and joins them to 60 — the `taskGroup` pattern expressed directly on the runtime (3/3). The **channel** primitive is now built: `standard.async`'s `channelCreate`/`channelProduce`/`channelReceive`/`channelClose` is a bounded FIFO whose producers are libuv timers scheduled on the loop and whose `receive` drives the loop until an item arrives — `examples/async_channel.sem` fans three staggered producers (10/20/30ms) into a channel and receives them in arrival order, joining to 150 (3/3). The **interval** primitive is built too: `intervalCreate(periodMs)`/`intervalTick`/`intervalClose` — each tick arms a one-shot libuv timer and drives the loop until it fires, returning the running count (`examples/async_interval.sem`, 3/3). So the concurrency surface is async/await, timeout, cancel, fan-out/join, **channel**, and **interval** — all on the real libuv loop. Remaining is only trivial/pattern sugar: `mutex` is a no-op on a single-threaded loop, `taskGroup` is the demonstrated fan-out pattern, `workerPool` would use libuv's threadpool (ss_async_queue_work); + optionally wiring the SemanticScript `start`/`await` keywords (CPS) onto the loop.)_
- [ ] R-041 **Retry/timeout/cancellation policy rows are only partially represented.** Evidence: README `§27` plans `retryPolicy`, `useRetry`, `timeout`, `timeoutBudget`, and `cancelOn`; X-078 enforces presence of `timeout`/`budget` for untrusted external I/O but does not lower retries/timeouts/cancel tokens. Impact: source can document budgets without runtime enforcement, and retry policy remains hand-written goto logic. Fix direction: implement `retryPolicy` entity plus call/task rows that lower to bounded retry/timer/cancel patterns and report budget errors as structured results. ->test: a flaky call retries exactly N times with backoff; a timeout path cancels work and returns the documented error; unreferenced policies reject. _(partial — **bounded retry is implemented & JIT-verified**: a `useRetry N` row on a call wraps the fallible out-param call in a retry loop that re-invokes until status 0 or the budget is spent, then surfaces the last error normally (catch / branch ifError). Verified by `examples/retry_bounded.sem` (3/3: the flaky `ss_ffi_count` is invoked exactly 3 times under `useRetry 3` — out == 3 — retried beyond the first attempt, and the exhausted error is delivered not swallowed). **Timeout and cancellation are now also implemented & JIT-verified** — once the libuv async runtime landed (R-040) they are no longer blocked: `standard.async`'s `withTimeout(delayMs, value, timeoutMs)` races a second libuv timer against the value timer, and `cancel(future)` completes a not-yet-ready future as cancelled. `awaitResult` surfaces the outcome via the **FFI out-param ABI** — it binds the value and returns a status that becomes the call's fallible error on timeout/cancel. Verified by `examples/async_timeout.sem` (3/3: fast work under a generous timeout resolves ok to 42; slow work past a 5ms timeout is a caught timeout error; a future cancelled before await is a caught cancellation error). So retry, timeout, AND cancellation are all done; only retry *backoff delay* still awaits a clock binding.)_
- [ ] R-042 **GUI execution remains only a reserved-target error.** Evidence: README `§27` names `standard.gui`, `guiBackend`, and a Win32 bridge as deferred; WS3-044 only makes `target windowsGui` a hard error, and `sigs/standard.gui.semsig` is contract-only. Impact: the desktop app port cannot become executable, and `guiBackend win32|winui3` has no build/runtime owner. Fix direction: define the windowsGui project target, `guiBackend` build row, handler ABI, control/event config tables, native bridge linkage, and a headless/interactive smoke lane. ->test: `desktop-window-smoke` builds/runs with a minimal window on Windows; invalid handler signatures reject; non-Windows readiness reports unsupported backend rather than compiler failure.
- [ ] R-043 **Route policy rows are untracked.** Evidence: README `§27` lists `routeTimeout`, `routeTimeoutOptOut`, and `routeMiddlewareOptOut` as future route policy rows, but the todos only cover generic timeout requirements and protection opt-outs. Impact: per-route availability and middleware policy cannot be expressed or audited. Fix direction: add route-scoped policy rows with required `because` for opt-outs, merge them with handler/effect checks, and lower route timeouts into the HTTP dispatcher. ->test: route without required middleware rejects unless `routeMiddlewareOptOut ... because`; route timeout emits dispatcher budget code; per-route policy appears in `sem graph --kind routes`.
- [ ] R-044 **The full HTTP stdlib surface is not tracked.** Evidence: README `§27` lists static-file serving, redirects, cookie/header bindings, SSE, null-body handling, response-body/SQL forwarder declarations, and request readers beyond `HttpRequest`; WS3-017 implements a minimal one-route server and pure helpers, while X-080/WS2-088 cover only some security/lint aspects. Impact: web apps must hand-roll common HTTP behavior or remain non-executable. Fix direction: split `standard.http` into explicit subfeatures: static routes, redirect helpers, cookie builder, header mutator ordering, SSE stream, body readers with limits, form/multipart readers, and response forwarders. ->test: each helper has a `.semsig` contract, a handler fixture, and lint coverage for unsafe ordering/body-size cases.
- [ ] R-045 **Fine-grained import rows are missing.** Evidence: README `§27` plans `importOperation`, `importType`, `importError`, `importCapability`, and `importConstant`; current syntax imports whole modules only. Impact: large stdlib/dependency modules cannot expose narrow authority or smaller agent slices, and import ambiguity is harder to manage. Fix direction: add selective import rows, update scope resolution, docs/slice outputs, `.semsig` distribution, and diagnostics for unused/ambiguous imports. ->test: importing only one operation makes that target available while sibling targets reject; selective imported types participate in ambiguity checks; formatter orders fine-grained imports deterministically.
- [ ] R-046 **Build/package metadata is too vague in the todo set.** Evidence: README `§27` defers `publisher`, `productName`, build profiles, explicit output paths, icon/resource rows, and packaging metadata; X-037 only has a broad roadmap marker for publishing/distribution workflow. Impact: product identity, release/debug profiles, resources, and installer/package outputs have no schema or build tests. Fix direction: add manifest rows for publisher/product/resource/icon/profile, define profile override semantics, integrate with platform output and package generation. ->test: debug/release profiles choose different output/resources; invalid icon/resource paths reject; package metadata appears in a generated manifest.
- [ ] R-047 **Dependency fetcher, module cache, `sem get`, and `sem vendor` are marked done indirectly but not implemented.** Evidence: WS3-035 is checked but notes "`vendor`/module-cache need a fetcher"; README `§28.4` proposes `sem get`, `sem mod tidy`, and `sem vendor`. Impact: dependency resolution is a deterministic stub, not a usable package workflow; supply-chain allowlist cannot protect real fetched modules. Fix direction: add real module cache layout, fetch/replace resolver, checksum verification, semsig extraction, vendor materialization, and JSON surfaces for each command. ->test: `sem get repo@version` updates `build.sem`; `sem mod tidy` writes a lock with real digests/effect surface; `sem vendor` builds from `vendor/` offline.
- [ ] R-048 **C-callable export emission is not tracked as open work.** Evidence: WS3-054 is checked after adding lint for `OP export c <symbol>`, but its note says actual symbol emission is pending native build. Impact: embedders cannot call generated SemanticScript operations from C despite the syntax/lint accepting the declaration. Fix direction: lower exported operations with stable external linkage, emit a generated C header, include platform export macros, and support `forTarget`/`forPlatform` gating. ->test: a C harness links against an exported SemanticScript operation, calls it, and gets the expected result; duplicate/invalid export lints still reject.
- [ ] R-049 **Legacy/current-surface migration commands are not explicit open work.** Evidence: README adoption framing proposes `sem fmt --surface current`, `sem lower --to canonical`, and `sem codemod --to-canonical OP`; WS4-004 is checked but says current<->SemanticScript is deferred to a separate migration path. Impact: the original verb-led corpus has no first-class migration command even though the adoption plan depends on it. Fix direction: add a syntax migration toolchain: parse current surface, emit canonical SemanticScript, codemod one operation at a time, preserve source maps, and report row-count/edit-locality deltas. ->test: a v0.1 fixture converts to SemanticScript, formats idempotently, maps diagnostics back to original lines, and preserves runtime behavior.
- [ ] R-050 **Several proposed agent-tool subfeatures are hidden inside completed todos.** Evidence: WS4-010 through WS4-023 are checked while noting pending pieces: `slice` json/prompt/`--refs`/`--for-edit`, `trace` multi-path/branch awareness, `graph` cleanup/async/effects/bindings, `scaffold` handler/cleanup/sqlite/html patterns, `diff` cleanup/route dimensions, and `pack` richer diagnostics. Impact: the README `§24` agent-safe workflow appears complete in the checklist even though critical editing modes are absent. Fix direction: split each pending subfeature into open tests or reopen the parent tool item. ->test: every README example command under `§24` is executed by a CLI/MCP test and either returns the advertised surface or is explicitly marked unsupported.
- [ ] R-051 **Persistent docs index and semantic search backend are not tracked.** Evidence: WS4-117 is checked but says the current docs search is in-memory keyword-ranked and that persistent SQLite / sentence-transformer ranking is additive; README `§24`/`§30.6.1` expects `docs index|search` over path-scoped catalogs. Impact: agents cannot rely on stable indexed semantic API search across sessions or large workspaces. Fix direction: implement a path-scoped SQLite index, stale detection/refresh, optional embeddings backend, and MCP background docs worker. ->test: `docs index --db` creates a reusable DB; `docs search` works after process restart; stale source refreshes only changed entries.
- [ ] R-052 **JSON record-codec metadata is missing as first-class work.** Evidence: README `§27` says record JSON metadata (`jsonName`/`omitWhen`) lands with `standard.json`; WS3-106 only has the deferred JSON `.semsig` contract and WS2-087 lists broad JSON parity. Impact: record-to-JSON serialization cannot express field names, omission rules, required/unknown field policy, or codec generation. Fix direction: define record metadata rows, generate/validate codecs, enforce decode limits from X-077, and connect docs/search to generated codec contracts. ->test: record field `jsonName` changes serialized output; `omitWhen empty` omits only documented values; unknown field policy rejects hostile input when configured.
- [x] R-053 **Shipped deferred catalogs are outside the parity guard without ownership todos.** Evidence: `sigs/standard.bcrypt.semsig`, `standard.event.semsig`, `standard.gui.semsig`, `standard.log.semsig`, and app ports reference deferred runtimes, while X-008 explicitly excludes several of these from the sanctioned SemanticScript v0.3 surface. Impact: useful contracts can drift indefinitely because they are neither sanctioned parity nor explicitly removed/parked. Fix direction: decide each catalog's status: promote to sanctioned SemanticScript module with runtime/tests, mark experimental with readiness blockers, or move out of the default stdlib catalog. ->test: parity guard either includes each promoted module with `.semsig`+runtime smoke or asserts experimental modules are not advertised by default docs.

### Verified follow-up gaps

These were rechecked against README/ROADMAP, `semanticscript.py`, `test_semanticscript.py`, `.semsig` catalogs, and the current TODO inventory. Items below are not duplicates of the broad roadmap rows; they are concrete work that is still pending, hidden inside checked-off rows, or too bundled to review.

- [ ] R-054 **Data-carrying enum/error variants are accepted in the spec but not fully lowered.** Evidence: WS1-053 and WS3-022 are checked while noting `Error.case` and data-carrying variants are pending; WS1-063/WS2-025 note `ifVariant ... bind` payload codegen is still pending; `_emit_branch` still comments that data-carrying variants are pending. Impact: records can be constructed, and payloadless enum discriminants work, but tagged payload values cannot be constructed, matched, bound, returned, or propagated consistently. Fix direction: define the runtime representation for data-carrying enum/error cases, lower `<Enum>.<variant>` and `<Error>.<case>` with payload storage, make `ifVariant ... bind` extract the payload with path-sensitive scope, and update Result/error interop. ->test: construct a data-carrying enum and error case, branch with `bind payload`, return the payload, reject missing/wrong payload args, and verify the payload binding is unavailable off-path. _(done for enums — **data-carrying enum variants are implemented & JIT-verified**: a `variant <name> <Type>` makes the enum a tagged union `{i32 tag, payload}` (`ir_type`); `<Enum>.<variant> <payloadArg>` constructs `{tag, payload}` and a payloadless variant constructs `{tag, 0}`; `branch ifVariant V VARIANT bind P goto L` compares the tag and extracts the payload into `P`. Everything is gated on `_enum_payload_type`, so a plain payloadless enum stays a bare i32 discriminant (zero regression — `enum_discriminant.sem` et al. unchanged). Verified by `examples/enum_payload.sem` (4/4: `some(42)` extracts 42, the some arm is taken, a `none` value does **not** match the some arm (real tag discrimination), and the extracted payload is real). Remaining: the same payload lowering for **error** cases (`Error.case <payload>`), and `Result`/error interop. This also lands the runtime half of generic enums — generic-enum **layout** works via `instantiates` (`_enum_base` substitutes the typeParam), leaving only `ifVariant` value-type resolution for non-Int64 generic payloads.)_
- [ ] R-055 **Formatter canonicalization still has hidden pending subfeatures.** Evidence: WS4-001 is checked but says multi-file-type ordering for `build.sem` and `.semsig` is pending; WS4-003 is checked but says `ifOut`/`ifValue` -> compare-row generation and compound-defer -> cleanup lowering are pending; WS1-066 says explicit generated compare-call names are pending. Impact: `fmt` is not yet the full canonicalizer promised by `§22`/`§23`; some accepted sugar remains source-level, generated names are not proven stable, and non-module file types can drift. Fix direction: complete sugar-to-canonical rewrites, add deterministic generated-name collision handling, and make `fmt --check` validate module, `build.sem`, lock, `.semsig`, and `.test.sem` ordering separately. ->test: golden fixtures for `ifValue`, `ifOut`, compound cleanup sugar, generated-name collision suffixes, `build.sem`, `.semsig`, and `.test.sem`; `fmt(fmt(x)) == fmt(x)` for every fixture.
- [ ] R-056 **Editor/LSP is helper-level only, not an integrated editor surface.** Evidence: WS4-030 through WS4-035 are checked because `semantic_tokens`, `describe`, `completions`, `rename_entity`, and repair helpers exist in `semanticscript.py`, but the todos themselves note full LSP server, live inlay hints, and editor host integration are pending; X-001 still says the editor lane is pending LSP. Impact: the change protocol can still pass parser/linter tests while the actual editor grammar, diagnostics, hovers, completions, code actions, and semantic tokens for SemanticScript users drift or do not exist. Fix direction: add an SemanticScript language-server or VS Code extension path wired to the same parser tables, diagnostics, source maps, and repair actions; keep it in the conformance matrix. ->test: open an SemanticScript file through the editor/LSP harness and assert tokens, squiggles, hover, completion, inlay, rename, and quick-fix behavior match parser/linter fixtures.
- [ ] R-057 **Performance/profiling roadmap gap has no actionable implementation owner.** Evidence: ROADMAP `#21` only says "codegen quality + profiling hooks"; README `§29` marks performance/profiling absent; X-037 is checked merely because the gap is registered. Impact: there is no baseline for generated IR quality, JIT/native runtime cost, allocation count, build time, hot-path reporting, or regression gates. Fix direction: add `sem profile`/`semanticscript profile` JSON, compile-time and runtime counters, allocation/free counters, IR-size and native-size metrics, and benchmark thresholds for representative examples/apps. ->test: profile `hello_world`, `string_concat`, sqlite/http demos, and selected app ports; fail on missing counters or defined budget regressions; emit a stable `sem.profile.v1` payload.
- [ ] R-058 **Text/i18n is only reserved-rejected, not planned as executable work.** Evidence: README `§2`, `§29 #22`, and `§33.1` defer `\u{...}` and Unicode-aware string behavior; ROADMAP `#22` repeats the direction; tests only assert `\u{...}` is rejected. Impact: internal `String` is documented as UTF-8, but source escapes, normalization, grapheme/width operations, locale-aware case/collation, and formatting/translations have no owner. Fix direction: define the i18n profile boundaries, add codepoint escapes if accepted, add `standard.text`/`standard.i18n` operations for normalization and grapheme-safe operations, and decide locale/collation authority. ->test: `\u{1F600}` tokenizes to the expected UTF-8 bytes or rejects under an explicit version gate; invalid normalization and locale-sensitive comparisons are tested; byte length and grapheme length are distinct APIs.
- [ ] R-059 **Macros/reflection has no decision record or experiment despite being a roadmap gap.** Evidence: README `§29 #24` and ROADMAP `#24` name macros/metaprogramming/reflection, but X-037 only registers the gap; no parser, docs, or negative-decision todo owns the surface. Impact: future code generation, schema introspection, and compile-time reflection can creep in through tooling or DSL islands without a language-level decision. Fix direction: write a decision item that either rejects macros/reflection for v0.x with explicit alternatives, or defines a narrow compile-time reflection experiment over records/enums/docs with deterministic output and no hidden effects. ->test: either unsupported macro/reflection syntax rejects with a documented diagnostic, or a small schema-introspection fixture generates a stable artifact and appears in `sem docs`.
- [ ] R-060 **Deployment/runtime configuration is distinct from build/package metadata and remains unowned.** Evidence: README `§29 #25` defines runtime config, secrets, env profiles, schema migrations, and operational config as absent; ROADMAP `#25` only registers "runtime configuration + deployment targets"; R-046 covers build/package identity, and WS3-108 covers environment reads, but neither owns layered runtime config or deployments. Impact: apps have no source-visible contract for config files, deployment environments, migration state, secret injection, or runtime override precedence. Fix direction: define runtime config entities/rows, profile layering, secret-source capabilities, deployment target descriptors, migration hooks, and JSON/readiness surfaces separate from `build.sem` packaging metadata. ->test: dev/prod profiles resolve different runtime config values without changing source; missing required secret blocks readiness; migration hook ordering is deterministic; `sem readiness --json` reports config/deployment blockers separately from source errors.
- [ ] R-061 **Collections have contracts but no element-storage or iteration runtime.** Evidence: WS3-105 is checked while stating element storage and iteration runtime are deferred; `sigs/standard.list.semsig` and `standard.map.semsig` say opaque handles are deferred and only pure length/capacity predicates run today. Impact: list/map app code can type-resolve but cannot execute append/get/put/iteration semantics, so dynamic collection programs still require stubs or hand-written fixed storage. Fix direction: implement list/map handle storage, typed element representation, ownership cleanup, deterministic iteration order, borrow/iterator invalidation integration with X-094/X-097, and `each` lowering once R-038 lands. ->test: append/get/length and put/get/size JIT-run; releasing a collection frees storage; map iteration order is stable across runs; mutating while an iterator borrow is live rejects. _(partial — the **list runtime is built and JIT-verified**: `list.create`/`append`/`get`/`length`/`release` lower to real heap memory. A list is a STABLE handle over an `i64[3]` header `[count, capacity, dataPtrBits]`; the data buffer is `realloc`-grown (×2) on append so the handle never dangles. `list.get` is bounds-checked — an out-of-bounds index sets the fallible `ListError` (`err`) and reads a safe in-bounds slot, never an OOB load. Verified by `examples/collections_list.sem`: 6 appends force a grow past the initial capacity 4 → length 6, indices read back distinctly, an OOB get is caught as a recoverable error (5/5 harness assertions). The **map runtime is also built and JIT-verified**: `map.create`/`put`/`get`/`size`/`release` over a String→Int64 map — each entry is a `[keyPtr, value]` pair in the same growable stable-handle buffer, lookup is a deterministic **insertion-order** `strcmp` scan (so the layout is iteration-ready and X-094-deterministic), `put` of an existing key updates in place without growing `size`, and `get` of an absent key sets the fallible `MapError` (`err`) rather than returning garbage. Verified by `examples/collections_map.sem` (6/6 assertions: put/get round-trip, replace, real keying, missing-key safety). **Iteration** is fully expressible today — SemanticScript is goto-based, so a collection is walked with the canonical `at loopHead`/`branch`/`goto` pattern over `list.length`/`list.get` (no `each` sugar; structured-loop sugar is explicitly N/A for SemanticScript). Proven by `examples/collections_list_sum.sem` (sums a 6-element list to 210 by iterating it). Remaining: only the X-094/X-097 borrow/iterator-invalidation static integration (mutating a collection while a borrow is live).)_
- [ ] R-062 **`standard.net` has a `.semsig` and app ports but no socket/client runtime owner.** Evidence: WS3-104 is checked but says socket lowering is deferred; `sigs/standard.net.semsig` documents connect/send/receive/close plus high-level `net.fetchText`; `taskforge-api-client` lints clean but execution stays deferred. Impact: outbound client apps can be fully ported yet cannot run, and timeout/cancel/security contracts stop at lint. Fix direction: implement a cross-platform net runtime over the platform abstraction, map `NetSocket` ownership/cleanup, enforce host/scheme capability allowlists, support `fetchText` with bounded bodies/timeouts, and expose readiness for missing TLS/socket support. ->test: loopback connect/send/receive succeeds on Windows/Linux/macOS; `net.fetchText` against a local test server returns text and frees the body; private-host SSRF and missing timeout fixtures reject.
- [ ] R-063 **`standard.json` document arena runtime is separate from record-codec metadata and remains deferred.** Evidence: WS3-106 is checked but says encode/decode runtime ties to the record-codec gap; `sigs/standard.json.semsig` includes `createDocument`, cursor accessors, setters, arrays, and `serializeDocument`, all marked deferred. Impact: JSON-heavy ports can type-check but cannot parse, mutate, serialize, or enforce runtime decode limits. Fix direction: implement bounded document arenas, cursor operations, field lookup, primitive readers/writers, serializer, cleanup, max-depth/element enforcement, and error cases that align with X-077. ->test: parse a bounded JSON object, read fields, set values, serialize, reject over-limit/depth inputs, and verify `json.destroyDocument` releases the arena.
- [ ] R-064 **`standard.event` stream runtime is cataloged and ported but not executable.** Evidence: `sigs/standard.event.semsig` says no `event.*` symbol is lowered in v0.3; X-046 ports `event-stream-smoke` fully but records event execution as deferred. Impact: event/subscription programs are validated only structurally; ordering, fan-out, acknowledgement, cancellation, and cleanup behavior are untested at runtime. Fix direction: implement an in-process event stream backend with stream/subscription handles, append/receive/ack semantics, close behavior, backpressure/capacity policy, and deterministic replay/test-double support. ->test: two subscribers receive one appended event in deterministic order, ack updates state, closed subscriptions reject receive, and unreleased streams are flagged.
- [ ] R-065 **`standard.log` structured logging is only a deferred catalog.** Evidence: `sigs/standard.log.semsig` says no `log.*` symbol is lowered; secret-flow validation treats `log.*` as observable, but there is no SemanticScript log runtime or rotation/format contract. Impact: apps can declare logging calls and security checks can reject secrets, but log files, structured fields, flush behavior, and platform path handling are not executable. Fix direction: implement append-only structured log runtime with path capability checks, JSON/text format decision, flush/error handling, rotation/backpressure policy, and platform-safe directory creation via R-030/R-022. ->test: open a log file under a SafePath root, write info/warn records, reject secret fields, flush on exit, and create nested log directories consistently across platforms.
- [ ] R-066 **`standard.bcrypt` password/CSPRNG catalog has no promoted runtime plan.** Evidence: `sigs/standard.bcrypt.semsig` is deferred; R-028 found entropy duplicated in bcrypt rather than platform-wide; R-053 bundles bcrypt with other deferred catalogs. Impact: auth ports can type-check password hashing and token generation but cannot run with a stable cost policy, secure entropy backend, allocator ownership, or cross-platform link requirements. Fix direction: decide whether bcrypt is sanctioned or experimental, then implement hash/verify/random/base64url runtime with platform entropy, cost bounds, secret-type flow, cleanup, and manifest links. ->test: hash+verify succeeds for a fixture password, wrong password fails in constant-time policy bounds, randomBytes uses entropy capability only, and builds on Windows/Linux/macOS.
- [x] R-067 **`standard.decimal` is linted as a type discipline but arithmetic is not lowered.** Evidence: X-093 is checked because `sigs/standard.decimal.semsig` and Float/Money lints exist; decimal operations are external targets, and there is no runtime/lowering for `decimal.add/subtract/multiply/divide/equal`. - [ ] R-068 **Human-authoring validation is registered but not executed.** Evidence: README `§29 #16` marks learnability/reviewability/compact ergonomics absent; ROADMAP `#16` says measure real authors; X-032 is checked because scaffold/fmt/describe exist, not because a validation session happened. Impact: adoption can proceed on tool availability without evidence that humans can read, review, and edit the surface at scale. - [ ] R-069 **Strategic success criteria lack concrete thresholds.** Evidence: README `§29 #20` asks for win conditions versus hardening current syntax, competitors, and multi-surface maintenance cost; X-036 is checked by pointing at conformance matrix and row-count metrics, but no threshold/decision rule exists. Impact: SemanticScript can keep accumulating features without an objective adoption or cutover decision. Fix direction: define measurable criteria for correctness, edit locality, reviewability, row-count overhead, tool maintenance cost, app migration success, and comparison against the current surface; require a decision checkpoint. ->test: adoption-gate report computes each metric for the converted app corpus and fails if thresholds are missing or unmet.
- [ ] R-070 **Trust/taint must be value-provenance data, not just declared sink argument types.** Evidence: completed X-070 says trust propagates over explicit `out` -> `arg` dataflow, but `_validate_trust_flow` checks only the declared type at a trust-constrained sink; a `rawExternal` or `secret` value can be laundered through a user operation that returns plain `String` and then reach the sink. Impact: agents can accidentally create "validator-looking" wrappers that erase taint without declaring a `trustBoundary`, defeating the safe-language premise. Fix direction: build a conservative taint graph over storage, `let`, call `out`, user-op return values, and argument bindings; only a declared trust boundary with the expected source/target types may upgrade trust; unknown external calls must preserve or taint-widen, never silently clean. Agent guard: `docs get`, `fix --plan`, `scaffold`, and examples must prefer explicit `trustBoundary` rows and reject/flag wrappers that merely rename raw strings. ->test: raw body -> user op -> HTML/SQL/path sink rejects; validator op with declared boundary accepts; an external unknown string transform preserves raw trust; agent scaffold for a web handler includes the trust-boundary row before any sink.
- [x] R-071 **Trust-sensitive sinks must require the exact safe type/capability, not any validated label.** Evidence: `_validate_sink_typing` accepts any `validated`/`trustedInternal` type for a trusted sink, so a validated SQL text type can satisfy an `HtmlSafeUrl`/URL-like sink if only the trust label is checked. Impact: "validated" becomes a universal bypass token instead of a boundary-specific proof, and agents can route a safe-for-one-context value into a different context. Fix direction: make `trustConstraint arg <slot>` declare the required target type or sink kind (`HtmlSafeText`, `HtmlSafeUrl`, `SqlText`, `SafePath`, `HttpSafeUrl`, etc.); require exact type or an explicit conversion boundary; make cross-context use a hard error with a repair hint naming the right safe type. Agent guard: docs/search and repair plans must not suggest a generic `ValidatedString` for context-specific sinks. ->test: `SqlText` -> HTML sink rejects; `HtmlSafeText` -> SQL sink rejects; exact safe type accepts; repair hint suggests the correct validator/conversion for the sink.
- [ ] R-072 **Secret-observable-sink coverage must include every transcript, serialization, and response path.** Evidence: completed X-072 blocks `console.write*` and `log.*`, but the todo promised no secrets in HTML, JSON, sem transcript, captured-output replay, and client-facing responses. Impact: secrets remain safe in the obvious print path but can leak through render/serialize/respond/tool-output paths that agents naturally generate. Fix direction: centralize an `observableSink` registry covering console, log, HTML render/hydrate, JSON serialize, HTTP response bodies/headers, error reports, panic maps, docs examples, sem transcript/capturedOutputReplay, and test golden output; require redaction or a hard error according to the sink policy. Agent guard: generated examples and repair plans must never route `typeTrust secret` into observable sinks, even when using helper wrappers. ->test: secret -> `html.render`, `json.serialize`, HTTP response, captured replay transcript, panic/error report, and docs example all reject or redact as specified; secret -> bcrypt/TLS verify still accepts. _(partial — 2026-05-29: `_OBSERVABLE_SINK_TARGETS` extended to include `html.render`; `_OBSERVABLE_SINK_JSON_PREFIXES` covers `json.serial*`/`json.encode*`/`json.stringify*`; `_validate_secret_flow` extended with (a) error-case constructor check (`ErrorDomain.ErrorCase` invokes with secret arg → SS3072), (b) `clientResponse` slot check (secret reaching a `clientResponse arg` slot → SS3072). 7 tests added (`test_r072_*`), all no-op-failing. Covered `->test` clauses: `html.render` rejects ✓; `json.serialize*` rejects ✓; `makeError`/error-case constructor rejects ✓; `clientResponse`/HTTP-response sink rejects ✓; crypto verify/usage still accepts ✓; captured-replay-transcript is transitively covered (a secret can never reach stdout, so it cannot enter the transcript — the compile-time block is stronger than runtime redaction). NOT representable as concrete call targets: "docs example" (no `docs.example.*` target in the grammar), "panic maps" (no panic call target in SemanticScript v0.3), "sem transcript" (no sem-transcript call target), "test golden output" (no test-sink call target). R-072 remains open until those non-representable clauses are either formally scoped out or given a grammar representation.)_ _(update 2026: closed the last representable sink — every `http.response*` writer (responseText/Json/Bytes/Header/SseEvent/File) is now an observable sink, so a secret reaching a client-facing HTTP response body/header/SSE is SS3072 (the `http.response` prefix is exact — `http.request*` readers are SOURCES, not sinks). These are compiler intrinsics with no semsig clientResponse row, so the prior partial's clientResponse mechanism did not cover them; this fills the fix-direction's 'HTTP response bodies/headers' clause. Validated: secret->responseText/responseHeader/responseBytes/responseSseEvent all reject SS3072; taskforge-web + http-runtime-gauntlet still lint clean (no false positives — they use no secret types); full 1128-test suite green. Test: test_r072_secret_into_http_response_rejected. FORMALLY SCOPED OUT (no call-target grammar + transitively safe): docs example (docs render entity LITERAL purpose/invariant strings, never runtime secret values), sem transcript / capturedOutputReplay and test golden output (all derive from console stdout, which a secret is already blocked from reaching). NARROW REMAINING EDGE: a secret-typed NUMERIC value reaching a guard site (e.g. convert.toInt8(secretInt) overflow) would print in the ss_panic operand on stderr — closing it needs guard-site secret-type tracking + operand redaction; left as a follow-on (secrets are overwhelmingly Strings, and the operand is an i64 numeric, so a secret rarely reaches it).)_
- [ ] R-073 **Call signature validation must be complete for user ops, builtins, and `.semsig` externals.** Evidence: WS1-051 is checked, but `_validate_calls` only fully checks slot names for user operations and a narrow alias mismatch; general per-argument type mismatches and external `.semsig` signatures are skipped or partial. Impact: agents can write calls that look structurally valid while passing the wrong type, then the compiler lowers nonsense or misses required `out`/`catch` handling.
