- [ ] R-141 **Status-returning runtime intrinsics can declare `catch`, but lowering never wires their status into `ifError`.** Evidence: `_emit_branch` lowers `branch ifError` to `err` from `_call_info`, or to constant false when `err` is absent, at `semanticscript/compiler/semanticscript.py:9144-9156`; the event/gui/http/c/family intrinsic branches call native symbols and store only `result` at `9863-9933`, `9968-9981`, and `9990-10028`; `_call_info[call.name] = (result, err)` then records `err == None` at `10123`, while the catch variable is still bound to constant 0 at `10128-10130`. Repro: a valid source with `json.createDocument` over malformed text `"{"`, a `catch JsonAccessError`, and `branch ifError parseBad goto badPath` passed `check -` with only an unused-binding warning, then `run -` exited 0 on the success path instead of returning the catch-path code 7. `apps/taskforge-web/src/main.sem` uses this pattern heavily, for example `json.createDocument` catch rows at `1065-1069` and `json.serializeDocument` catch rows at `1698-1703`. README link: call/catch semantics, FFI out-param ABI, native runtime errors, and agent-safe execution. Impact: JSON parse/serialization, SQLite, bcrypt/log, GUI, HTTP, event, and libc status failures can be silently treated as success, pushing null handles, empty strings, or bad native status values into later memory-owning code. Fix direction: give every intrinsic lowering an explicit error predicate (`status != 0`, handle/string null, or native out-param status), or route these surfaces through the existing status-plus-outParam runtimeBinding ABI so `err` is always populated when a `catch` exists. ->test: malformed JSON parse, too-small JSON serialize scratch, failed sqlite prepare, bcrypt bad cost, GUI invalid handle, HTTP response failure, and event allocation failure all take their `branch ifError` path; success cases keep `ifError` false. _(partial: the core lowering bug is fixed for the family-intrinsic surfaces. _emit_call now wires `err` from the native result whenever a `catch` exists: retkind "i" status calls -> `icmp ne i32 <call>, 0` (the SS_*_OK==0 convention — covers sqlite exec/step/bind/finalize/close, json scalar setters, bcrypt hash/verify/randomBytes/base64, log), and the unambiguous creation-handle calls via _FAMILY_HANDLE_ERR -> their failure sentinel (json.createDocument/createEmptyDocument `==0`, json.documentRoot `<0`, sqlite.openDatabase/prepareStatement `==0`) — this fixes R-141's showcase repro (json.createDocument over malformed text now takes its branch ifError path) and the failed-sqlite-prepare/bcrypt-bad-cost cases. Validated at IR level: test_r141_family_intrinsic_catch_wires_iferror asserts a real icmp feeds the branch, never `br i1 false`; full 1112-test suite green; taskforge-web (which uses this pattern heavily) checks clean. REMAINING: (a) value-returning "h" (columnInt64/cursorInt64 — 0 is valid data) and cursor-mutation "h" (objectFieldAt/setObjectFieldObject/Array/appendArrayElementObject — 0 aliases root) and every "s" string return need the status+outParam ABI to surface errors (overlaps R-142 — too-small-serialize-scratch case); (b) the separate gui/http/event/libc intrinsic lowering branches (evidence lines ~9863-9933/9968-9981) still store only result with err=None and need the same wiring. Deferred as follow-on once the status+outParam ABI lands.)_

- [x] R-144 **`standard.http` URL codec adapters allocate unchecked returned strings with no ownership/free path.** Evidence: `ss_http_url_encode_str` computes `capacity = n * 3 + 1`, allocates with `malloc`, and returns the buffer at `semanticscript/runtime/ss_http.c:35-42`; `ss_http_url_decode_str` computes `capacity = n + 1`, allocates, and returns at `45-52`; neither adapter has checked size arithmetic, a caller-visible free, or request-scope ownership. - [x] R-145 **`bcrypt.base64UrlEncode` can overflow capacity math before the output-size guard.** Evidence: `ss_base64url_encode` accepts an `int` byte count and computes `((input_byte_count * 4) + 2) / 3 + 1` in signed `int` before comparing with `output_capacity` at `semanticscript/runtime/native_bcrypt/sem_bcrypt_runtime.c:226-270`; the app-facing runtime is shipped through `semanticscript/runtime/manifest.json:94-115`, and `apps/taskforge-web/src/main.sem:1468` exercises the exposed helper. - [x] R-146 **Async delay/timeout/interval setup failures can leave futures waiting forever.** Evidence: `ss_async_get_loop` ignores loop-init failure at `semanticscript/runtime/ss_async.c:31-35`; `ss_async_new` does not check future creation and ignores `ss_async_timer_start` at `61-72`; `ss_async_timeout_start` ignores timeout timer setup at `82-89`; `ss_async_interval_tick` ignores timer setup and then waits until a flag flips at `234-247`; `ss_async_future_await` loops while a future is not ready at `semanticscript/runtime/native_async/sem_async_runtime.c:599-610`. Repro: static failure-path review shows loop allocation/init failure, future allocation failure, or timer init/start failure can leave the awaited future or interval flag permanently unset, so the runtime spins instead of surfacing a typed async error. README link: async runtime, retry/timeout stability, task lifecycle, and long-running toolchain reliability. Impact: memory pressure or libuv setup failures can hang the compiler/runtime process in async code paths, including timeout wrappers that are supposed to bound work. Fix direction: propagate every loop/future/timer setup status into the future result, add a failed/closed state for interval waits, and make awaits bounded by deterministic error paths rather than readiness-only polling. ->test: injected loop-init failure, future allocation failure, delay timer-start failure, timeout timer-start failure, interval timer-start failure, and normal delay/timeout behavior all terminate with the expected typed result. _(done — every loop/future/timer setup failure now terminates deterministically instead of spinning forever. ss_async_new bails on a NULL loop (loop-init failed) and a NULL future (create failed), and if the value timer can't arm it completes the future with a terminal SS_ASYNC_FAILED status + SS_ASYNC_ERR_ENGINE so await returns (ss_async_future_await only exits on a broken loop, NOT a never-completing future — that was the hang). ss_async_interval_tick checks the tick timer-start, returns a -1 failed-tick sentinel instead of an unbounded `while(!wait.fired)` spin, and breaks the drive loop if run_once reports the loop unavailable. ss_async_timeout_start fails the setup (FAILED) if the timeout timer can't arm rather than silently degrading to an unbounded wait, and won't re-arm an already-resolved future. ss_async_future_await was already bounded (exits on loop error). clang -Wall -Wextra clean; the 4 async examples (delay/fanout/interval/timeout) + e2e async tests rebuild and pass (the success paths); test_async_setup_failures_dont_hang guards the failure-handling (the failure paths need libuv-injection to drive at runtime). Full 1118-test suite green.)_

README link: packaged compiler, release engineering, offline toolchain distribution, and agent-safe build surfaces. Impact: the compiler release path can consume CI/agent sessions without identifying the failed phase, and packaging failures are not machine-readable like the rest of the toolchain. Fix direction: add a configurable packaging timeout, capture and report subprocess stderr/stdout with a `sem.package.v1` or release-build diagnostic, clean partial work dirs safely, and smoke the produced executable before returning success. ->test: fake/sleeping PyInstaller times out with a named phase, missing PyInstaller reports a structured dependency error, normal packaging still emits the executable path, and the result runs `version --json` plus a hello-world smoke. _(done — the packager is now bounded and machine-readable. build() preflights PyInstaller (structured dependency-missing error, no late ModuleNotFoundError), clears a stale --workpath, runs the freeze via _run_bounded with a configurable timeout (SEM_PACKAGE_TIMEOUT, default 1800s) + captured output so a stuck import scan / AV-lock / bad install times out with a named phase (pyinstaller-freeze) instead of hanging, and a non-zero freeze surfaces its stderr/stdout tail. On success it verifies the artifact exists then smokes it (version --json envelope validation + a hello-world `run -` JIT) before returning. Every failure is a sem.package.v1 envelope (ok:false + status + phase); __main__ writes it to stderr + exits 1, and `--json` emits the success envelope. Tests: test_packaging_failures_are_bounded_and_structured covers timeout-names-phase, non-zero-surfaces-output, missing-PyInstaller dependency error, and version-json smoke validation; the full end-to-end freeze+run smoke stays in the release/compiler-exe workflows (PyInstaller is too heavy for the unit suite). Full 1119-test suite green.)_
- [x] R-150 **`standard.log` reports success after partial writes and silent truncation.** Evidence: `ss_log_write_line` calls `fwrite`, `fputc`, and `fflush` but ignores their return values and always returns `SS_LOG_OK` at `semanticscript/runtime/native_log/sem_log_runtime.c:88-96`; `log_escape_json` truncates long fields into fixed buffers at `99-123`; `ss_log_event` clamps an oversized formatted line and still writes it at `145-151`; `ss_log_http_access` mirrors the truncation while returning `void` at `171-180`. Repro: static runtime review shows full-disk, closed-handle, permission, or short-write errors are lost after the file is opened, and long messages/paths are silently truncated while callers observe success or no status at all. README link: structured logging, observability, runtime error contracts, and agent-safe diagnostics. Impact: production/server logs can lose or corrupt audit data while the app believes logging succeeded, which undermines debugging of compiler/runtime crashes and webServer stability incidents. Fix direction: check every stdio return value, propagate `SS_LOG_ERR_ENGINE` through status-returning log intrinsics, make truncation explicit or dynamically size the JSON line with checked allocation, and give access logging a status path. ->test: forced fwrite/fputc/fflush failure, long message truncation, long path access log, permission-denied log directory, normal log event, and HTTP access log all report deterministic statuses. _(DONE: silent truncation is now reported. log_escape_json returns 1 when the source had to be truncated to fit its fixed buffer; ss_log_event folds that (plus the assembled-line snprintf clamp) into the return: it still WRITES the truncated line so the entry is not lost, but returns SS_LOG_ERR_TRUNCATED (new code) instead of SS_LOG_OK so logInfo/logWarn/logError callers learn the record was not faithful (a write failure SS_LOG_ERR_ENGINE dominates). ss_log_http_access changed void -> int with the same status path (no external callers, so ABI-safe), giving the access log a deterministic status. Validated: clang -Wall -Wextra clean; a standalone C driver exercises short->OK, >2048-message->TRUNCATED, normal-access->OK, >1024-path->TRUNCATED, short-access->OK (all pass); full 1125-test suite green; test_log_reports_truncation guards the machinery. Combined with the earlier write-result checks (ss_log_write_line now checks fwrite/fputc/fflush), every stdio failure and every truncation on the status-returning log surfaces is now reported. NB: the taskforge-web app-suite check is flaky in this repeated-run dev session (a leftover zombie webServer answers with a stale DB -> 409) — confirmed PRE-EXISTING by reproducing it with these changes git-stashed, so unrelated to this fix.)_

- [ ] R-151 **Runtime manifest still compiles Windows-only `standard.net`, async, and GUI sources on POSIX targets.** Evidence: `semanticscript/runtime/manifest.json` keeps `ss_net.c`, libuv `src/win/*.c`, and `native_win32_gui/sem_win32_gui_runtime.c` in top-level `sources`, while `_resolve_runtime_links` in `semanticscript/compiler/semanticscript.py` merges those base sources with the selected platform instead of replacing them. Repro: on Linux or macOS, compile a program that imports `standard.net`, `standard.async`, or `standard.gui` and the native build sees Winsock/Win32 or Windows libuv sources. README link: The runtime manifest section promises platform-specific native links. Impact: cross-platform programs fail at native-link time even when the SemanticScript code itself is portable. Fix direction: move OS-specific source lists under platform overlays, add POSIX net/gui fallbacks or block them with a clear diagnostic, and make the resolver support replacement/removal of base sources. ->test: manifest-resolution unit tests plus native build smoke tests for net/async/gui on Windows and POSIX targets.

- [x] R-152 **HTTP client request construction checks truncation after already using the truncated offset.** Evidence: `semanticscript/runtime/native_http/sem_http_runtime.c` computes `request_capacity` with unchecked `strlen` additions, then accumulates several `snprintf` return values into `written` and immediately uses `request + written` / `request_capacity - written` before checking whether the previous write exceeded the buffer. Repro: call `http.fetch` with an oversized method/path/host/header/body combination that makes the first or second `snprintf` truncate; the next append can form an out-of-bounds pointer or underflow the remaining length. README link: This reopens the HTTP native safety work tracked by the runtime safety section. Impact: long but valid caller input can corrupt memory or crash the process before the function can return `SS_ERR_HTTP_REQUEST`. Fix direction: use checked size addition, validate each `snprintf` result before advancing, and append through a bounded builder helper. ->test: ASAN/UBSAN HTTP client tests for huge path/header/body values plus a normal request regression. _(done: the http client request builder now validates each snprintf result (n<0 or written+n>=request_capacity) BEFORE using written as the offset/remaining for the next snprintf, so a truncating/negative write can no longer make request+written point past the buffer or request_capacity-written underflow into a huge size_t (which the next snprintf would write through). Per-step checks also catch an undersized buffer from the capacity math. The http runtime rebuilds + test_http_server + http-runtime-gauntlet + taskforge-web + taskforge-api-client all PASS (the server probe exercises the client builder with valid input). Forcing truncation needs oversized method/path/host, so a source-guard test (test_http_client_request_checks_truncation_per_step) keeps the per-step checks.)_

- [x] R-153 **HTTP response writes narrow `size_t` lengths through `int` before `send`.** Evidence: `send_all` in `semanticscript/runtime/native_http/sem_http_runtime.c` accepts a `size_t byte_count` but casts each remaining chunk to `int` for `send`; `ss_http_response_text` and `ss_http_response_bytes` can store response body sizes above `INT_MAX`. Repro: serve a bytes/text response larger than `INT_MAX` on POSIX and the negative `int` argument is converted back to a huge `size_t` by `send`, reading past the response buffer; on Windows it can fail unpredictably. README link: Native runtime I/O must not reinterpret buffer sizes across C ABI boundaries. Impact: large responses can cause memory disclosure, crashes, or false transport failures. Fix direction: chunk sends to a bounded maximum (`INT_MAX` on Windows and a conservative `size_t` chunk on POSIX), reject configured response sizes above the server limit, and preserve short-write error reporting. ->test: unit test `send_all` with mocked oversized counts and an integration response-size limit test. _(done: send_all now clamps each send() request to INT_MAX (SS_SEND_CHUNK_MAX) instead of casting (byte_count - sent_count) — a size_t — straight to int, so a response body larger than INT_MAX is sent in safe-sized chunks (the loop already handles partial sends) rather than passing send() a negative/truncated length. Normal responses (< INT_MAX) are unchanged: http runtime rebuilds + test_http_server + http-runtime-gauntlet + taskforge-web all PASS. A >2GB response is impractical to send in a test, so a source-guard (test_http_send_all_clamps_chunk_to_int_max) keeps the clamp in place.)_

- [x] R-154 **HTTP server accepts malformed or ambiguous `Content-Length` as a valid body size.** Evidence: `parse_content_length` in `semanticscript/runtime/native_http/sem_http_runtime.c` finds the first header and returns `strtol(value, NULL, 10)` without checking `errno`, the end pointer, trailing bytes, or duplicate conflicting `Content-Length` headers. Repro: send requests with `Content-Length: abc`, `Content-Length: 12junk`, an overflowing number, or two different content lengths; the server treats some as zero/truncated bodies and leaves unread bytes on the socket. README link: The HTTP server contract depends on deterministic request framing before route handlers execute. Impact: handlers can process a different body from the one sent by the client, which creates request-smuggling-style behavior and hard-to-debug failures. Fix direction: parse the header with strict decimal validation, reject overflow and trailing junk, and reject duplicate conflicting lengths before reading the body. ->test: server parser tests for malformed, overflowing, duplicate-same, duplicate-conflicting, and valid content lengths. _(done: parse_content_length now validates strictly — strtol with an endptr (reject no-digits + trailing junk like 12junk), errno==ERANGE (reject overflow), reject negative, and scan ALL Content-Length headers rejecting a second one with a different value (RFC 7230 3.3.3 request-smuggling). Any of these returns -1, which the caller already turns into a 413; a single valid Content-Length parses as before. The http runtime rebuilds + test_http_server + http-runtime-gauntlet + taskforge-web (valid Content-Length POSTs) all PASS. Injecting raw malformed requests needs a socket harness, so a source-guard test (test_http_content_length_strict_parse) keeps the strict parse in place.)_

- [x] R-155 **`json.cursorInt64` casts finite JSON doubles to `Int64` without type or range checks.** Evidence: `ss_json_cursor_int64` in `semanticscript/runtime/native_json/sem_json_runtime.c` returns `(long long)node->as.double_value` for `JSON_NODE_DOUBLE`; finite values outside the `long long` range have undefined or implementation-defined behavior and fractional values silently truncate. Repro: parse JSON containing `{"n": 1e20}` or `{"n": 1.9}` and read it through `json.cursorInt64`; behavior depends on the C compiler/runtime instead of returning the supplied default or an error status. README link: JSON cursor APIs are expected to preserve typed data access rather than coerce unsafe numeric forms. Impact: malformed or unexpected numeric input can produce non-portable integers and hide schema violations. Fix direction: require `JSON_NODE_INTEGER` for `cursorInt64`, or perform explicit finite/range/integrality checks before conversion and return the default/status on mismatch. ->test: cursor tests for integer, fractional double, huge finite double, missing key, and default propagation. _(done: ss_json_cursor_int64 now range-checks a JSON double before the long long cast — only a finite, in-range value [-2^63, 2^63) is converted (truncating toward zero); an out-of-range value or NaN/Inf falls back to missing_default instead of UB. The node->kind already gates the type. Validated by the json runtime rebuilding cleanly + all 7 apps passing (taskforge-web's full parse round-trip); test_json_cursor_int64_range_checked guards the range check stays. JIT/native share the same compiled shim.)_

- [x] R-156 **Legacy JSON scalar find helpers accept malformed scalar values.** Evidence: `ss_json_find_int64` uses `atoll`, `ss_json_find_double` uses `atof`, and `ss_json_find_bool` only checks `strncmp` prefixes in `semanticscript/runtime/native_json/sem_json_runtime.c`; trailing tokens such as `12abc`, `.5junk`, or `truex` can be accepted as valid values. Repro: feed a JSON-like string with malformed scalar suffixes to the native find helpers and observe successful reads that do not match full JSON tokens. README link: Native JSON helpers should share the strict parsing expectations of the main JSON parser. Impact: embedders that call these helper functions can accept invalid data and diverge from parser-backed SemanticScript behavior. Fix direction: route scalar lookup through the parsed JSON tree or validate token boundaries and full numeric grammar before returning success. ->test: native helper tests covering trailing junk, bool prefixes, fractional/negative numbers, and valid exact tokens. _(done: ss_json_find_int64/double/bool now parse strictly — int64/double use strtod with an endptr + a json_value_terminator check (end/whitespace/structural char) so a junk suffix like 12abc or .5junk is rejected (12.5 is still accepted and truncated; an out-of-range double is guarded per R-155), and bool requires an exact true/false token followed by a terminator (truex rejected). The lenient atoll/atof/strncmp-prefix paths are gone. The json runtime rebuilds cleanly + all 7 apps pass (test_apps); these legacy helpers have no .sem call site, so a source-guard test (test_json_find_helpers_reject_malformed_scalars) ensures the strict parsing stays.)_

- [x] R-157 **Compact-profile programs check/build but do not run, emit IR, or target wasm consistently.** Evidence: `cmd_build`, `cmd_check`, `cmd_test`, and related commands call `parse_compact(_read_program_source(...))`, but `cmd_run`, `cmd_emit_ir`, `cmd_inspect_ir`, and `cmd_wasm` still call `parse(_read_program_source(...))` directly. Repro: create a compact-profile source that relies on compact syntax accepted by `semanticscript check` or `semanticscript build`, then run `semanticscript run`, `emit-ir`, `inspect-ir`, or `wasm` against the same path and observe a parse failure. README link: CLI help advertises EAV/compact source files or projects for these commands. Impact: CI and developer workflows can validate a program and then fail at execution/codegen with a different parser. Fix direction: centralize command source loading/parsing so all source-consuming commands honor the same compact-profile detection. ->test: compact fixture exercised through check, build, run, emit-ir, inspect-ir, and wasm. _(done: cmd_run (both the --json strict-lint path and the in-process/child run path), cmd_emit_ir, cmd_inspect_ir, and cmd_wasm now parse with parse_compact (the compact-profile-aware parser) like check/build/test, instead of the strict EAV parse — so a compact-profile source that checks/builds clean also runs, emits IR, inspects IR, and targets wasm. parse_compact is a superset (canonical passes through), so canonical programs are unaffected. Test: test_compact_profile_consistent_across_commands.)_

- [x] R-158 **`test --lane` reports pass when the selected lane discovers zero tests.** Evidence: `run_tests` filters lanes with `lanes = {lane: lanes.get(lane, [])}` and then computes `runtimeStatus` with `all(t["status"] == "pass" for t in tests)`, so an empty selected lane is reported as `pass`; `cmd_test` now treats that composite status as success. Repro: run `semanticscript test path --lane e2e` on a project with no `e2e` tests and the command exits successfully instead of reporting that the lane was empty. README link: The test contract relies on CI lanes actually executing their selected test population. Impact: misspelled or unimplemented lanes can silently skip coverage while producing green CI. Fix direction: distinguish `no-tests` from `pass` after lane filtering, fail selected empty lanes unless an explicit allow-empty flag is provided, and include the lane name in diagnostics. ->test: test-runner fixtures for empty all-lanes, empty selected lane, populated selected lane, and `--discover` output. _(done: run_tests distinguishes a selected-but-empty lane as compositeStatus 'no-tests' (capturing selected_lane before the loop rebinds it), and cmd_test makes that ok:false / rc 1 unless --allow-empty is passed — a misspelled or unimplemented --lane can no longer green CI. Test: test_test_lane_empty_is_not_pass.)_

- [x] R-159 **`run --json --entry` ignores the requested entry point.** Evidence: `cmd_run` passes `entry=getattr(args, "entry", None)` to `jit_run` only in the text-output path; the JSON path calls `_record_run_full(src)` without forwarding `args.entry`. Repro: define two runnable operations, invoke `semanticscript run file --entry alternate --json`, and the JSON result executes the default entry rather than `alternate`. README link: JSON mode should be a serialization option, not a semantic change in command selection. Impact: tooling that uses JSON output cannot target the same explicit entry points as human CLI runs, and test helpers that rely on JSON records can exercise the wrong operation. Fix direction: thread the entry argument through `_record_run_full` or call the same run helper from both output paths. ->test: CLI tests comparing text and JSON `run --entry` output for a fixture with distinct entry operations. _(done: cmd_run's --json path now honors --entry by running that operation via _record_run_entry (run - --entry <op>) instead of always running the project entry. Test: test_run_json_entry_strict_and_empty_stdout asserts --entry main vs failOp select different exit codes.)_

- [x] R-160 **Duplicate diagnostic registry keys silently shadow shipped diagnostic meanings.** Evidence: `DIAGNOSTICS` in `semanticscript/compiler/semanticscript.py` defines `SS2601` twice (unknown `.semsig` schema version at line 369, invalid webServer route method at line 534) and `SS3043` twice (duplicate `export c` symbol at line 354, gated configure operation at line 494); Python keeps only the later entry. Repro: `explain SS2601` cannot return the `.semsig` schema-version repair even though `load_semsig` raises `code="SS2601"`, and `explain SS3043` cannot explain duplicate exported C symbols even though the linter emits that code. README link: The diagnostic registry is supposed to be the single source of truth for emitted codes. Impact: agents and users get the wrong repair text for real compiler errors, while coverage tests that only check code presence can still pass. Fix direction: assign unique codes, add a startup/unit guard that rejects duplicate registry keys before dictionary construction loses them, and update all emitters/tests. ->test: duplicate-key fixture fails the registry guard; `explain SS2601` and `explain SS3043` each cover only one emitted meaning; every emitted code maps to exactly one repair. _(done: the two duplicate registry keys are split into unique codes — the shadowed `.semsig` unknown-schema-version SS2601 -> SS2620 (load_semsig + docstring), and the shadowed duplicate-`export c` SS3043 -> SS3045 (_lint_c_exports); SS2601 now uniquely means webServer route-method and SS3043 gated-configure, so explain returns the correct repair for all four. test_no_duplicate_diagnostic_registry_keys scans the source and rejects any future duplicate registry key; test_shadowed_diagnostic_codes_now_unique asserts each meaning. Affected tests updated.)_

- [x] R-161 **webServer host, port, and route-path config rows pass check but fail or wrap at startup.** Evidence: `_validate_webserver_abi` validates route methods and handler ABIs, but not `host`, `port`, or that route paths begin with `/`; `_emit_webserver_entry` calls `int(port_row.payload[0])` directly and lowers the value to `i32`, while `ss_http_serve_routes` casts the port to `unsigned short` and the native route compiler rejects non-slash paths only at server startup. Repro: a webServer with `port nope` tracebacks during lowering, `port -1` or `port 70000` wraps to a different listening port, and `route GET api handler` can pass source checks before `ss_http_server_run` returns `SS_HTTP_ERR_CONFIG`. README link: webServer config should be a source-lane contract, not a native startup surprise. Impact: generated servers can pass `check`/agent preflight and then fail or bind the wrong port at runtime. Fix direction: lint `host` and `port` literals, require `1..65535`, require route paths to be `/`-prefixed with no empty segments except root, and convert malformed values to structured diagnostics. ->test: invalid port token, negative port, over-65535 port, missing-leading-slash route, trailing-slash route, and valid root/dynamic routes. _(done — added source-lane validation in _validate_webserver_abi: SS2604 rejects a port that is not a bare integer in 1..65535 (closes the `int("nope")` lowering traceback and the u16-cast wrap for -1/0/70000), SS2605 requires route paths to be `/`-prefixed with no empty `//`/trailing-slash segments except root `/` (the bare `*` catch-all stays legal), SS2606 rejects an empty/whitespace host. Registered all three in DIAGNOSTICS. Test: test_webserver_host_port_route_path_validation covers invalid/negative/over-65535/zero port, missing-slash + trailing-slash + internal-empty route, whitespace host, and valid root/`*`/dynamic routes; existing SS2601/2602/2603 + dynamic-routing tests stay green; full 1110-test suite passes; main.sem + http-runtime-gauntlet lint clean.)_

- [x] R-162 **`run --json --strict` skips the strict lint gate.** Evidence: `cmd_run` handles the JSON branch before parsing/linting for `--strict`; it calls `_record_run_full(src)`, whose child invocation is just `run -` and does not forward `--strict`. The non-JSON path applies `_filter_diagnostics_strict` before `jit_run`. Repro: run a fixture with a T3 warning under `semanticscript run fixture.sem --strict --json`; it executes and returns a `sem.run.v1` envelope instead of blocking like `semanticscript run fixture.sem --strict`. README link: `--json` should preserve command semantics and only change serialization. Impact: CI/agents that request machine-readable output can bypass the opinionated strict gate they explicitly asked for. Fix direction: perform the same strict preflight before the JSON child run or forward `--strict` through the child helper and encode lint failures as `sem.run.v1` `ok:false`. ->test: T3-warning fixture under run text/json strict, clean fixture under both modes, and deny-tier fixture under both modes. _(done: cmd_run's --json path now runs the --strict lint gate too — a strict-promoted error is reported as a sem.run.v1 lint-error envelope (ok:false, rc 1) instead of being silently skipped. Test: test_run_json_entry_strict_and_empty_stdout.)_

- [x] R-163 **`json.destroyDocument` is lowered as `void` even though the contract declares an `Int32` out status.** Evidence: `semanticscript/sigs/standard.json.semsig` declares `destroyDocument out released Int32`, but `_FAMILY_RT` maps `json.destroyDocument` to `("ss_json_destroy", "v", None)` and `_emit_call` only binds `out` values when `retkind != "v"`. The shim `ss_json_destroy` in `semanticscript/runtime/ss_json.c` also returns `void`. Repro: a source call that captures `destroyDocument out released` leaves `released` unbound or unusable, and a caller cannot branch on cleanup status despite the semsig row promising an `Int32` result. README link: `.semsig` contracts must match the ABI a runtimeBinding actually exposes. Impact: cleanup code and generated ownership checks can compile against a contract the lowerer cannot satisfy, hiding failed or duplicate cleanup paths. Fix direction: either change the semsig to `discards`/void semantics with no status, or make the shim return an `Int32` status and bind it consistently; add a semsig-vs-family-runtime conformance check. ->test: `destroyDocument` with captured out, discarded cleanup, double-destroy behavior, and family mapping conformance. _(done: aligned the .semsig contract to the runtime reality — standard.json.semsig's destroyDocument no longer declares `out released Int32` (the _FAMILY_RT mapping is retkind 'v'/void and ss_json_destroy returns void), so callers can't compile against a status the lowerer/shim never provide; the apps already `discards` it. Added test_json_semsig_matches_family_runtime_return_kind: every json intrinsic's semsig `out` must match its family-runtime return kind (void => no out; value => out), guarding against future semsig-vs-ABI drift. The shim/family-rt are unchanged (no runtime-C edit).)_

- [x] R-164 **SQLite `columnText` returns a borrowed SQLite buffer as a stable SemanticScript `String`.** Evidence: `ss_sqlite_statement_column_text` in `semanticscript/runtime/native_sqlite/sem_sqlite_runtime.c` returns `sqlite3_column_text(statement->handle, column_index)` directly, and `ss_sqlite_column_text` in `semanticscript/runtime/ss_sqlite.c` forwards that pointer; SQLite invalidates that memory on the next `step`, `reset`, or `finalize`, but the SemanticScript side has no borrowed-view/lifetime row attached to the returned `String`. Repro: read `columnText`, finalize or step the statement, then use the stored string in a later console/log/JSON/SQL call; the pointer can reference freed or overwritten SQLite memory while the source checker sees an ordinary `String`. README link: The memory-safety model distinguishes owned resources from borrowed views, but this runtime shim hides the borrow. Impact: normal database iteration can produce use-after-finalize or stale-text reads outside the structured error model. Fix direction: copy column text into owned runtime memory with a cleanup contract, or mark it as a borrowed view tied to the statement lifetime and enforce consume-before-step/finalize in the checker. ->test: use-after-finalize, step-before-use, reset-before-use, copied-text survival, and valid immediate-use column reads under ASAN/lifetime lint. _(done — the runtime hands sqlite3_column_text's borrowed pointer straight into a SemanticScript String with no copy (retkind "s" stores the raw i8* as the String), and the codebase already enforces this as a borrowed-view via the SS1901 lint. R-164's gap was that the invalidator set only covered step/reset; finalize frees the statement, so a column String read before finalize and used after it is a use-after-free the source checker otherwise misses (it sees an ordinary String). Added sqlite.finalizeStatement to _COLUMN_INVALIDATOR_TARGETS so step/reset/finalize-then-use all warn; updated the SS1901 message + docstring. This takes the reviewer's stated second option (borrowed-view tied to statement lifetime, enforce consume-before-step/finalize). Test: test_sqlite_column_used_after_finalize_warns; existing step-warn / sibling-no-warn stay green. Limitation: the lint is intra-operation+lexical (a column String stored across operations still relies on the borrowed-view model, not a runtime copy).)_

- [ ] R-165 **`graph` and `query` still use the strict EAV parser, so compact-profile programs can check/build/run but fail agent analysis.** Evidence: `cmd_graph` and `cmd_query` call `parse(_read_program_source(args.path))` in `semanticscript/compiler/semanticscript.py`, while compact-aware commands route through `parse_compact`; a compact source accepted by `check --json` and `docs --json` returns `sem.graph.v1`/`sem.query.v1` `compiler-error` at the bare `operation main` row. README link: compact profile, MCP/agent analysis surfaces, and the R-157 consistency contract. Impact: a valid compact-profile project can go green in source/codegen lanes but break graph/query retrieval and MCP agent workflows. Fix direction: centralize source loading/parsing for every analysis command, switch graph/query to `parse_compact`, and extend the compact-profile command matrix beyond run/emit-ir/inspect-ir/wasm. ->test: one compact fixture passes check, docs, graph calls/control, query effects/capabilities/calls, and the matching MCP tools; strict EAV fixtures still produce identical graphs.

- [x] R-166 **`net.fetchText` URL port parsing accepts malformed ports and trailing junk.** Evidence: `ss_net_parse_url` in `semanticscript/runtime/ss_net.c` uses `atoi(colon + 1)` after finding `:` before the path, with no end-pointer, digit-only, range, or trailing-token validation; `http://example.com:80junk/path` is treated as port 80, `:abc` becomes port 0, and out-of-range values are passed through to `getaddrinfo`. README link: network stdlib, URL safety, SSRF/resource-policy checks, and `HttpSafeUrl` expectations. Impact: validation and runtime behavior diverge, malformed URLs can route to a different endpoint than the source text says, and policy/debugging around outbound network calls becomes unreliable. Fix direction: parse with `strtol`/`strtoul` plus `errno` and end-pointer checks, require decimal digits only until `/`, enforce `1..65535`, and consider carrying the original authority including a valid port into the Host header. ->test: valid default port, valid explicit port, `:abc`, `:80junk`, `:-1`, `:0`, `:65536`, and path/query cases all return deterministic success/failure without attempting a socket connection. _(DONE: strict port parse (digits, 1..65535, no junk) in ss_net_parse_url. 2026-06-01)_

- [ ] R-167 **`sem.build.v1` is advertised but `build` has no JSON mode and most build outcomes are plaintext.** Evidence: `SEM_SURFACES` includes `sem.build.v1`, `version --json` advertises it, and `cmd_build` emits that envelope only for the `unknown-platform` branch; the argparse `build` subcommand has no `--json`, successful builds print only the output path, and normal `EavError` failures go to stderr as `semanticscript: ...`. README link: machine-facing `sem.*.v1` surfaces, build/native toolchain diagnostics, MCP/agent-safe contracts. Impact: agents discover a build surface from `version --json` but cannot request structured build results for success, compiler errors, native-link failures, or timeouts. Fix direction: add `build --json`, return a `sem.build.v1` envelope for success and every failure path (`path-error`, `compiler-error`, `unknown-platform`, `native-build-failed`, `timeout`), and keep text mode unchanged. ->test: build success, missing compiler, unknown platform, parse failure, native link failure, and timeout all assert envelope shape and exit code under `--json`; `version --json` surface registry matches the command capability.

- [ ] R-168 **`test --discover --json` ignores JSON mode and emits plaintext discovery output.** Evidence: `cmd_test` branches on `args.discover` before emitting a `sem.test.v1` envelope and never checks `args.json` in that path; `semanticscript test examples/hello_world.sem --discover --json` prints `0 test operation(s)` with exit 0 instead of JSON even though the subcommand is JSON-native. README link: machine-facing test lane contracts, MCP tool dispatch, and the R-097 command-wide `--json` rule. Impact: agents can request JSON and receive text, breaking schema consumers only on the discovery submode. Fix direction: make discover mode return `sem.test.v1` with `status:"discovered"`, selected lane, tests by lane, and total count when `--json` is present; keep the current text output for non-JSON discovery. ->test: discover all lanes, selected populated lane, selected empty lane, and normal execution all assert envelope/text parity.

- [ ] R-169 **`slice --json` emits plaintext errors for an unknown entity.** Evidence: `cmd_slice` parses first, then catches `slice_entity` `EavError` by writing `semanticscript: ...` to stderr regardless of `args.json`; `semanticscript slice examples/hello_world.sem missingEntity --json` exits 2 with plaintext `no entity named` instead of a `sem.slice.v1` or `sem.error.v1` envelope. README link: `sem.slice.v1` entity-scoped agent contract and R-098 JSON-capable failure handling. Impact: MCP/editor callers cannot distinguish a missing entity from process failure when they ask for the slice surface. Fix direction: branch on `args.json` in the entity-error path and emit a structured envelope with `status:"not-found"`, `entity`, and diagnostics; also consider moving parse into the same command-local envelope so slice failures consistently name `sem.slice.v1`. ->test: valid slice text, valid slice JSON, missing entity JSON, missing entity text, and parse-error JSON shapes.

- [ ] R-170 **`bench` advertises EAV/compact input but still uses the strict EAV parser.** Evidence: the `bench` subparser help says `EAV/compact source file or project`, but `cmd_bench` uses `parse(src)` for target detection and every timing iteration; the compact fixture that passes `check --json`, `run`, `emit-ir`, and `inspect-ir` fails under `bench --runs 1 --json` with `first row for entity 'operation' must be its is row`. README link: compact profile consistency, `sem.bench.v1`, and the R-157 parser consistency contract. Impact: the performance surface cannot benchmark the same compact-profile programs accepted by the source and execution lanes. Fix direction: use `parse_compact` for source parsing in bench, keep project-root cwd handling, and ensure the child run probe uses the same expanded source semantics as the timed lower/run loop. ->test: compact fixture passes `bench --json`, canonical fixture preserves timings, webServer target still skips run, and malformed compact input reports structured compiler diagnostics.

- [ ] R-171 **`repin --check --json` reports stale locks with `ok:true`.** Evidence: in the `cmd_repin` check branch, `_json_envelope("sem.repin.v1", lockPath=..., upToDate=up_to_date, wrote=False)` omits `ok`, so a stale lock (`upToDate:false`, process exit 1) still inherits `_json_envelope`'s default `ok:true`. README link: dependency lockfile CI gate, `sem.repin.v1`, and machine-readable command status semantics. Impact: an agent or CI wrapper that keys off the envelope can treat a stale dependency lock as successful even though the command failed. Fix direction: set `ok=up_to_date`, include an explicit `status:"up-to-date"|"stale"`, and keep the rc behavior unchanged. ->test: current lock check returns ok true/rc 0, stale lock check returns ok false/rc 1, missing manifest remains path-error, and text mode output is unchanged.

- [x] R-172 **`net.fetchText` misparses query-only URLs and can send URI fragments to the server.** Evidence: `ss_net_parse_url` in `semanticscript/runtime/ss_net.c` splits the authority only at the first `/`; `http://example.com?x=1` has no slash, so `host_end` becomes the end of the string and the host becomes `example.com?x=1` instead of host `example.com` plus path `/?x=1`. The same path copy includes `#fragment` when a slash is present, even though fragments are client-side only and must not be sent in the request target. README link: `HttpSafeUrl`, URL parsing, network stdlib, and SSRF/resource-policy checks. Impact: valid URLs can fail DNS or route differently than the source text implies, while fragment-bearing URLs leak client-only fragment text to the upstream server. Fix direction: parse the authority up to the first of `/`, `?`, or `#`; synthesize `/?query` for query-only URLs; strip fragments before sending; and share validation with the port parser from R-166. ->test: `http://host`, `http://host/path`, `http://host?x=1`, `http://host/path?x=1`, `http://host/path#frag`, and `http://host?x=1#frag` produce deterministic host/path pairs before any socket attempt. _(DONE: authority ends at /?#; query-only URLs keep the query, fragment stripped. 2026-06-01)_

- [ ] R-173 **`test --json` still reports a full pass when the whole selected test population is empty.** Evidence: `run_tests` only marks `no-tests` when a specific `--lane` was selected; with no lane, `tests=[]` makes `all(t["status"] == "pass" for t in tests)` vacuously true, so `semanticscript test examples/hello_world.sem --json` returns `ok:true`, `runtimeHarnessStatus:"pass"`, and `compositeStatus:"pass"` with an empty `tests` array. README link: CI test lane contract and R-158's zero-discovery fix. Impact: a project with no test operations can green the default test lane, and `--allow-empty` is not required or even consulted for the all-lanes empty case. Fix direction: distinguish empty-all-lanes from pass, return `compositeStatus:"no-tests"`/`ok:false` unless `--allow-empty` is set, and keep selected-lane behavior consistent with R-158. ->test: empty all-lanes, empty all-lanes with `--allow-empty`, empty selected lane, populated selected lane, and failing populated lane all assert `ok`, status, and exit code.

- [x] R-174 **Static `HEAD` requests send the full file body.** Evidence: `find_static_route` in `semanticscript/runtime/native_http/sem_http_runtime.c` explicitly matches both `GET` and `HEAD`, but the static-file branch calls `ss_http_response_file` and then `send_response(... response.body ...)`; `send_response` always writes the body whenever `body_length > 0`, with no request-method guard. README link: webServer/static HTTP runtime and standard HTTP semantics. Impact: clients issuing `HEAD` receive a response body, wasting bandwidth and violating cache/probe expectations while the route table says `HEAD` is supported. Fix direction: carry the request method into the response sender or add a static-branch no-body path for `HEAD`, preserving `Content-Length`, ETag, and Last-Modified headers without sending bytes. ->test: static `GET` returns body, static `HEAD` returns identical headers and zero body, cached `HEAD` 304 stays bodyless, dynamic explicit `HEAD` route behavior is defined, and existing GET/static probes remain green. _(DONE: static HEAD sends headers + Content-Length but no body (response.head_only). 2026-06-01)_

- [ ] R-175 **`c.atoll` silently accepts malformed request IDs.** Evidence: `ss_c_atoll` in `semanticscript/runtime/ss_libc.c` is a direct `return atoll(text);` wrapper with no end-pointer, overflow, or digit-only validation; `apps/taskforge-web/src/main.sem` uses `c.atoll` for delete/complete/uncomplete todo IDs, so `12junk` can be treated as ID 12 and `abc` as 0. README link: trust-boundary parsing, `rawExternal` input handling, and the standard libc shim surface. Impact: web handlers can mutate records based on a numeric prefix of malformed client input, bypassing the intended invalid-ID path. Fix direction: add a strict parse intrinsic or replace `ss_c_atoll` with `strtoll` plus `errno`/end-pointer checks that returns a status/result; update request handlers to reject malformed IDs before SQL mutation. ->test: valid decimal ID, leading/trailing whitespace policy, `12junk`, empty string, negative value policy, overflow value, and the three taskforge-web mutation routes all assert reject-or-accept behavior.

- [ ] R-176 **Compact-profile source still fails `lint`, `doctor`, and `trace`, splitting the source-lane tooling.** Evidence: `cmd_lint`, `cmd_doctor`, and `cmd_trace` in `semanticscript/compiler/semanticscript.py` still call `parse(_read_source(args.path))`; the compact fixture that passes `check --json` returns a `sem.lint.v1` `compiler-error` at the bare `operation main` row, and `trace`/`doctor` emit the same strict-parser error in plaintext. README link: compact profile, source-lane check/lint, and the R-157/R-165 parser consistency contracts. Impact: agents can accept a compact program as clean through `check`, then fail when requesting detailed lint/doctor diagnostics or a trace for the exact same source. Fix direction: route lint/doctor/trace through the same `parse_compact(_read_program_source(...))` helper used by compact-aware surfaces, and audit the remaining source consumers for either compact support or explicit canonical-only documentation. ->test: one compact fixture passes check, lint, doctor, trace, docs, graph, query, bench, and canonical fixtures preserve existing output.

- [ ] R-177 **The fallback HTTP server accepts malformed request lines instead of rejecting them before app dispatch.** Evidence: `parse_request_line` in `semanticscript/runtime/native_http/sem_http_runtime.c` only splits on spaces; it never validates a nonempty token method, an origin-form path beginning with `/`, an allowed HTTP version, or trailing tokens after the version, and the `version` variable is otherwise unused. Requests such as `GET  HTTP/1.1`, `GET / HTTP/9.9`, or `GET / HTTP/1.1 extra` can continue into static/dynamic route dispatch rather than returning 400. README link: webServer HTTP runtime, raw request boundary handling, and route/middleware contracts. Impact: protocol-invalid traffic can invoke notFound/methodNotAllowed handlers or route matching with ambiguous method/path metadata, pushing transport validation into app code that should never see malformed requests. Fix direction: implement a strict request-line parser, reject malformed methods/targets/versions/trailing fields with 400 before building `SSHttpRequest`, and keep route matching only for valid HTTP/1.0 or HTTP/1.1 origin-form requests. ->test: valid GET/POST/HEAD request lines dispatch, empty method, empty path, absolute-form path policy, invalid version, missing version, and extra-token request lines all return 400 without invoking handlers.

- [ ] R-178 **Unsupported chunked request bodies are dispatched as empty or partial bodies.** Evidence: `handle_client` calls `parse_content_length` before `parse_headers` and reads exactly the declared Content-Length; it never inspects `Transfer-Encoding`. A request with `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` and no Content-Length gets `content_length == 0`, then `request.body_length == 0` while the route handler still runs; a TE+CL conflict is not rejected either. README link: webServer request-body handling, `rawExternal` body parsing, and request-smuggling defenses. Impact: clients or proxies that send chunked bodies can cause handlers to process an empty body, or can create CL/TE ambiguity where the app and upstream disagree about the request payload. Fix direction: either implement bounded chunked decoding before dispatch or fail closed by rejecting any unsupported `Transfer-Encoding`; always reject TE+CL ambiguity. ->test: normal Content-Length body works, chunked-only request returns 400/501 without handler invocation until decoding exists, TE+CL conflict is rejected, and oversized decoded chunks hit the existing request-size ceiling.

## Definition of Done (per workstream)
- **WS1:** Hello World (§18) + web-handler (§19) parse → lower → run; value/control/type semantics tested with no-op-lowering-fails guards.
- **WS2:** every §17 #1–#55 + MD rule has a passing lint test + a rejecting fixture; diagnostic registry complete; source-mapping works.
- **WS3:** console/sqlite/http/html/test/build modules have `.semsig` + impl + tests; build.sem→lock→run reproducible; supply-chain allowlist enforced.
- **WS4:** `sem fmt` total/idempotent/round-trip green; slice+doctor+verify-patch power the diagnose→slice→edit→verify→summarize loop; editor moves with parser.
- **Cross-cutting:** conformance matrix green; drift guard green; §34 migration completed with split preserved.

## App execution — make the X-040–047 ports actually RUN (not just lint)

The X-040–047 ports above lint clean, but their external surface was deferred
(§27): the `net`/`event`/`gui`/`terminal` runtimes and the `webServer` codegen
target were never built, so only `html-template-lab` JIT-runs. This wave lands
the runtimes/codegen (EAV-clean: each intrinsic lowers to a `runtimeBinding`-style
native call, reusing the legacy C runtimes under `legacy/SemanticScript/runtime`
where one already exists) so every app JIT-runs and is **e2e-tested**.

- [x] APP-RUN-0 `html-template-lab` — already JIT-runs (console + `html.render`); add a permanent e2e smoke asserting the rendered dashboard document. →test: app runs, output contains the full escaped `<ol>` document. _(done: `tests/test_apps.py` JIT-runs the project and asserts the full document — doctype, title, the `<ol class="todo-list">` with all three todo rows, and `</html>`; exit 0. Wired into the CI integration loop. This is the extensible app-e2e harness the rest of APP-RUN-1..6 register into.)_
- [x] APP-RUN-1 `taskforge-api-client` — HTTP-GET client runtime (`ss_net_fetch_text`/`ss_net_free_text`, winsock) + model `net.fetchText`/`net.freeTextBody`. →test: start the local http server, run the app, assert it prints the fetched body. _(done: `semanticscript/runtime/ss_net.c` is a self-contained winsock HTTP/1.1 GET client (parse url → connect → GET → strip headers → heap-owned body); `runtime()` declares the two externs; `_emit_call` models `net.fetchText` (read the request record's `url` field → fetch → wrap the body in the `out` response record's `body` field, resolving field names by name with a field-0 fallback so the compiler stays agnostic to the app's record types) and the void `net.freeTextBody`; `_referenced_runtime_symbols` pulls in `ss_net_*` so the new `ss_net` manifest lib (ws2_32) builds+registers. `tests/test_apps.py` stands up a local server on 127.0.0.1:18090 and asserts the app fetches+prints all three bodies (exit 0). The app source is unchanged.)_
- [x] APP-RUN-2 `taskforge-tui` — provide `c.terminalReadKey` headless-safe (stdin/EOF) so the state machine runs without a console; the other `c.*` are libc (already resolved). →test: feed scripted keystrokes on stdin, assert the rendered table + clean exit. _(DONE — `tests/test_apps.py::_run_tui` feeds a scripted keystroke and asserts the rendered table + clean save/exit. `ss_c_terminalReadKey` reads one byte from stdin and reports Esc (27) on EOF so the read loop quits. Needed beyond that: the `pointer.offset`/`loadByte`/`storeByte` intrinsics (byte-addressed ops over an OpaquePointer); the rest of the `c.*` seam shimmed in `ss_libc.c` (putchar/puts/fflush/fopen/fclose/fgets/memmove + variadic printf/fprintf via the v*-counterparts, generalizing the snprintf case) with one pinned return type per symbol so a discards-vs-out call pair agrees on one extern; and `compare.<op>Int32` resolving an enum-variant operand (e.g. `ScreenMode EditMode`) by its declared type → the i32 discriminant.)_
- [x] APP-RUN-3 `event-stream-smoke` — event/stream runtime (`ss_event_*`) + model the 7 `event.*`. →test: emit one event to two subscribers, receive+ack+close, assert the printed receipts and exit 0. _(done: `ss_event.c` is an in-process pub/sub — a stream queue of monotonic ids + a per-subscription read cursor (Int64/OpaquePointer handles); `runtime()` declares the 7 externs; `_emit_call` models all 7 `event.*` (extracting the relevant args, ignoring receive's out-buffer params); `_referenced_runtime_symbols` + the new `ss_event` manifest lib build+register them. **Refined-rewrite fix:** the v0.1 async shape started both receives before the append, but the single-thread backend runs `start` eagerly, so receive saw an empty queue → 0 → fail; reordered `main` to append→receive (synchronous pub/sub, op-count unchanged so the X-047 parity guard still holds). `tests/test_apps.py` asserts both listeners print their handled line + exit 0.)_
- [x] APP-RUN-4 `desktop-window-smoke` — headless widget runtime + model the 16 `gui.*`; headless-safe. →test: create→event→close headless, assert exit 0. _(done: `ss_widget.c` is an in-memory headless toolkit (monotonic handle stubs; mutators no-op with a 0 status; `application_run` returns 0 — no event delivered headlessly); new `ss_widgets` manifest lib. Generic `gui.*` dispatch in `_emit_call`: resolve the call's args in order, derive a **fixed** return type per target (creators→i64 handle, `textBoxText`→i8\* String, else→i32 status — fixed because a target is called both with `out Int32` and `discards`, so the extern signature must be consistent), call `ss_widget_<snake>`, store the result only when the call has an `out`. Used a separate `ss_widget_` prefix to avoid colliding with the real Win32 `ss_gui_*` (which back gui_health_demo). The app JIT-runs all 31 `gui.*` calls + the loop and exits 0 headlessly; `tests/test_apps.py` asserts exit 0.)_
- [x] APP-RUN-5 `http-runtime-gauntlet` — model the 17 `http.*` request/response/multipart/SSE intrinsics + lower the `webServer` codegen target onto `ss_http_server_run`. →test: start it, drive representative routes, assert responses. _(done: **the `webServer` codegen target** — `generate()` accepts `target webServer` and `_emit_webserver_entry` synthesizes the entry (named after the project entry, which `jit_run`/`build` call) that builds parallel method/path/handler arrays — handlers are the lowered `int(request,response)` ops bitcast to `SSHttpHandler` — and calls the new `ss_http_serve_routes` (assembles the `SSHttpServerConfig` + blocks in `ss_http_server_run`). Generic `http.*` dispatch over the legacy `ss_http_*` accessors (getters→i8\* String/bytes, `*Length`→i64, `response*`→i32; force-exported in the manifest like the other legacy symbols) + `pointer.isNull` for null-guarding nullable reads. Fixed a real bug: webServer host/route-path payloads keep their literal quotes, so paths started with `"` not `/` and the config was rejected (`SS_HTTP_ERR_CONFIG`) — `_unquote` strips them. `tests/test_apps.py` starts the server and probes `/health`→ok, `/reflect/method`→GET, `/reflect/path`→the path, `/`→banner. App source unchanged.)_
## Toolchain + agentic-tool parity port (TOOL-0..7)

Port the legacy `sem` toolchain's missing surface into the new `semanticscript`
compiler **with upgrades**, get the MCP server into working order, and refresh
the docs the agentic surface serves for the EAV/`semanticscript` reality. Core
compiler/language/runtime parity is already done (all 7 apps + 188 examples run);
this wave closes the *toolchain* gap (CLI agentic tools, MCP, docs-search,
self-diagnostics, IR inspection, bench, dependency tooling). One TOOL per commit.

**Contract fidelity:** the legacy tools' JSON **response structures and input
parameters** were well-liked — review the legacy `sem` surfaces and *preserve
those schemas* (same fields, same shapes, same param names, versioned
`sem.<tool>.v1` envelopes) when porting. Only the *content* changes to the
revised EAV/`semanticscript` language; every ported surface is tested against
current `.sem` programs.

- [x] TOOL-0 `mcp` to working order + smoke — verify the JSON-RPC handshake end to end (`initialize` → `tools/list` → `tools/call`), ensure every advertised tool actually runs, and add a permanent MCP smoke test. →test: a scripted init+list+call over stdin returns valid results; smoke wired into the suite. _(DONE — the handshake works (initialize→serverInfo, tools/list→12 tools, tools/call→`sem.<tool>.v1` surfaces, unknown-tool→JSON-RPC −32602). Fixed a real contract bug: 9 of 12 tools take a required `path` positional but `tools/list` advertised `path` as optional for all, so an agent calling them bare hit an argparse error — `EAV_MCP_TOOLS` now carries a `requires_path` flag and `tools/list` emits an accurate `inputSchema` with `required: ["path"]` (plus the optional `search`/`get` for `docs`). `tests/test_mcp.py` drives the full handshake + every advertised tool + the error path (12/12 callable) and is wired into the CI integration loop.)_
- [x] TOOL-1 MCP + agent docs refresh for EAV — update the docs the `agent_docs`/`mcp` surface serves so they describe the new `semanticscript/` package, the current subject-first syntax, the apps, the self-contained runtime, and getting-started; drop stale `sem`/legacy/v0.1 references. →test: `agent-docs`/the MCP doc tool returns refreshed, EAV-accurate content with no `semsc`/`semlint` references. _(DONE — the surfaces were already EAV-renamed (no `semsc`/`semlint`); this **enriched** them for the recent upgrades. `EAV_AGENT_RULES` now covers `<name> is <kind>`, `uses`+`grants`, JIT-run vs native build (console+webServer + the clang/zig prereq), the self-contained `semanticscript/` layout, `apps/`, and getting-started. Skills gained **full bodies** (was summary-only) and two new entries — `eav-toolchain` (the CLI + MCP surface) and `eav-apps` (worked patterns) — and `cmd_skills` restores the legacy list-vs-get contract (`skills` lists summaries; `skills <name>` returns the body, preserving the `sem.skills.v1` envelope). Added a `skills` MCP tool (with an optional `skill` arg); the MCP smoke now passes 13/13.)_
- [x] TOOL-2 agentic CLI tool audit + repair — verify the agent-facing commands (`skills`, `agent-docs`, `eval`, `explain`, `fix`, `patch`, `verify-patch`, `query`, `describe`, `scaffold`, `new`, `graph`, `trace`) run and emit valid structured (JSON) output on a current `.sem`; repair any broken/stale ones. →test: each agentic command runs on a fixture and returns its documented surface; a harness asserts it. _(DONE — audited all agent-facing commands; **none were broken** (the earlier rc=2s were wrong-arg invocations). The structured tools emit versioned `sem.<tool>.v1` envelopes — `agentDocs`/`skills`/`check`/`query`/`graph`/`fixPlan`/`eval`/`docsIndex`/`deps`/`context`/`symbols`/`size`/`readiness` — and the content tools (`explain` → the diagnostic registry entry, `describe` → entity card, `trace` → liveness/defer, `scaffold` → a runnable program) return meaningful text. `tests/test_agent_tools.py` exercises 18 cases (correct args, asserting each surface/marker) and is wired into the CI integration loop, guarding the agent surface + its response structures against regression.)_
- [x] TOOL-3 `emit-ir` + `inspect-ir` — IR inspection: `emit-ir` (textual + optimized IR; a superset of `lower`) and `inspect-ir` (a structured per-function/symbol summary). →test: both produce valid IR/summary for hello_world. _(DONE — `emit-ir <path> [--optimized] [-o OUT]` emits textual LLVM IR, optionally through the LLVM new-pass-manager `default<O2>` pipeline (3481→1833 bytes on hello_world; graceful fallback + note if the pass API is unavailable). `inspect-ir <path> [--json]` emits a `sem.inspectIr.v1` summary — defined functions sorted by instruction count (with block/instr counts), declared externs, global count, IR bytes. Both registered in `main()`; `tests/test_agent_tools.py` now covers emit-ir, emit-ir --optimized, and inspect-ir (21/21).)_
- [x] TOOL-4 `status` + `clean` — a toolchain self-diagnostic (`status`: version, contract, compiler path, C-compiler availability, runtime-cache location/size, platform/triple) and `clean` (clear the runtime/build cache). →test: `status --json` reports the environment; `clean` empties the cache. _(DONE — `status [--json]` emits `sem.status.v1` (contract `eav-0.3.1` + release `0.4.0-beta.1` from version.json, compiler path, Python/llvmlite versions, platform + default triple, C-compiler availability, runtime-cache bytes); needs `_ensure_native_init()` before the triple query. `clean [--json]` emits `sem.clean.v1` and rmtree's the cached `_build` artifacts (freed 50 files / 8 MB in a smoke). `status` is in the agent-tools harness; `clean` is verified manually (destructive — not run in the shared suite).)_
- [x] TOOL-5 `search` + `index` — search the docs + diagnostic-code registry (and build a docs `index`). →test: `search <code|term>` returns matching diagnostics/doc sections. _(DONE — `index [--json]` emits `sem.codeIndex.v1`, the full catalog of all 170 diagnostic codes (compile-time SS/MD + runtime SSR) with tier + summary — the companion to `explain <code>`. `search <query> [--limit N] [--json]` emits `sem.search.v1`, keyword-ranking the registry over code/summary/`found`/`suggested` (a code-name hit scores 10, term frequency adds). Both registered + in the agent-tools harness.)_
- [x] TOOL-6 `bench` — a benchmark harness timing parse→lower→JIT-run (best-of-N), optionally the native build. →test: `bench hello_world` reports timings and exits 0. _(DONE — `bench <path> [--runs N] [--json]` reports the best-of-N parse / lower / end-to-end JIT-run times in ms (`sem.bench.v1`). The run phase mutes the JIT'd program's OS-level stdout (fd 1) via `os.dup2` so the program's output can't pollute bench's own (a Python-level redirect can't catch the C runtime's writes), and is skipped for a non-terminating target (webServer) — `runnable:false`, `runMsBest:null`. In the agent-tools harness (now 25/25).)_
- [x] TOOL-7 dependency tooling parity — assess `deps`/`mod tidy` vs the legacy granular ops; implement `repin` (local re-pin of the lock) and document the registry/network-dependent ops (`download`/`get`/`latest`/`self`/`update`/`bootstrap`) as beta-deferred (no package registry yet). →test: `repin` updates the lock; the deferred ops are documented. _(DONE — the dependency model already exists (`deps` → `sem.deps.v1`; `mvs_select` MVS; `mod_tidy` generating `build.sem.lock`) but had no CLI. Added `repin <project|build.sem> [--check] [--json]` → `sem.repin.v1`: regenerates `build.sem.lock` from `build.sem` via MVS (highest-version selected, sha256 digests, deterministic); `--check` verifies the lock is current without writing (CI gate, exit 1 if stale). `tests/test_agent_tools.py` does a temp-project write→check round-trip. The network/registry ops (`download`/`get`/`latest`/`update`/`self`/`bootstrap`) are **beta-deferred** — they need a package registry + remote fetch that doesn't exist yet (noted in the CHANGELOG known-gaps and the `cmd_repin` docstring).)_

- [x] TOOL-8 unified relevance-ranked search (real agentic retrieval) — replaced the shallow `docs --search`/diagnostics-only `search` with a **TF-IDF retrieval over a multi-source corpus**: the 170 diagnostics, the 5 skills (full bodies), the task templates (add-route/add-db-query/add-cleanup/…), the agent rules, the spec README (chunked by heading), and a project's entities when `--path` is given. Scoring is TF-IDF with a 3× title boost + prefix-tolerant matching; each `sem.search.v1` match carries `{source,id,kind,title,score,snippet,ref}` where `ref` is the command to fetch the full doc (`explain CODE`/`skills NAME`/`task NAME`/`docs PATH --get ENTITY`). `--source` filters by source. `docs --get` now falls back to the closest entity (difflib) on a near-miss name (`fuzzyMatch:true`). →test: cross-source queries return relevant ranked results — "add a route…"→[spec]+[template add-route]+[skill]; "clean up a database handle"→[spec]+cleanup diagnostics; "divide by zero"→the div0 codes; fuzzy `--get mian`→`main`. (TOOL-8)

- [x] APP-RUN-6 `taskforge-web` — the full stack: `sqlite`/`json`/`bcrypt`/`http`/`log`/`components` intrinsics + the `webServer` target. →test: register→login→todo CRUD round-trip over http, assert persisted state. _(DONE — the live server runs the full register→login→create-todo→list round-trip against real sqlite+bcrypt+json+log, asserted by `tests/test_apps.py::_run_webserver_crud`. The native runtime was **ported out of `legacy/` into `semanticscript/runtime/native_*`** so the new compiler is self-contained. Root-cause fixes that unblocked it: (1) the JIT loaded each runtime DLL into a local `ctypes.CDLL` that was GC'd on return → Windows unmapped it before `finalize_object` baked addresses, leaving symbols resolved to null — now kept alive in `_LOADED_RUNTIME_DLLS`; (2) `c.snprintf` lowered to a bare `snprintf`, which has no exported symbol on Windows UCRT → its unresolvable relocation cascaded and zeroed the entry's `serve_routes` call → routed through an exported `ss_c_snprintf`→`vsnprintf` shim; (3) `c.cString` symbol was `ss_c_cstring` but referenced as `ss_c_cString`; (4) `JsonText` `body json` islands weren't embedded (stayed null) → `_make_module_storage` now emits the island text; (5) `ss_json_read_string` returned NULL for absent optional fields → defaults to `""`; (6) `_emit_return nil` on a Result op returned the Err value (wrong IR type) → returns the OK-type null sentinel; (7) owned-resource handles whose deferred cleanup re-emits at multiple returns are now entry-block-slot-backed so they dominate every exit.)_

- **Iteration 3 — cross-cutting & forward:** §22 ordering, §24 tooling, §26 .semsig, §29 #1–#25 register, §31 layers, §34 minimal-core migration, conformance/drift/docs → WS4, Cross-cutting X1–X4.
- **Checklist:** every spec section §1–§34 ✓ has ≥1 item; every §29 gap #1–#25 ✓ tracked; every §17 invariant #1–#55 ✓ has a check+fixture; every §33 resolution ✓ has a behavior test; the call/task/cleanup split ✓ preserved across L1 (WS1-044), lint (WS2-010), and migration (X-017).
